## Act I / 第一幕

### Scene I / 第一场

===Original===
**Horner's** lodging.

*Enter **Horner**, and **Quack** following him at a distance.*

Horner
*Aside.* A quack is as fit for a pimp, as a midwife for a bawd; they are still but in their way, both helpers of nature.—*Aloud.* Well, my dear doctor, hast thou done what I desired?

Quack
I have undone you forever with the women, and reported you throughout the whole town as bad as an eunuch, with as much trouble as if I had made you one in earnest.

===Chinese===
**霍纳**（Horner）的寓所。

*（**霍纳**上，**庸医**（Quack）远远跟在后面。）*

**霍纳.**（旁白）庸医拉皮条，正像产婆当老鸨——都算各安本业，同是「自然」的帮手。（高声）好了，亲爱的大夫，我托你办的事，办妥了么？

**庸医.** 我已经叫您在女人跟前永世翻了身：满城都传遍了，说您跟阉人一样不中用——我费的这番气力，跟真把您阉了一回不相上下。

===Original===
Horner
But have you told all the midwives you know, the orange wenches at the playhouses, the city husbands, and old fumbling keepers of this end of the town? for they'll be the readiest to report it.

Quack
I have told all the chambermaids, waiting-women, tire-women, and old women of my acquaintance; nay, and whispered it as a secret to 'em, and to the whisperers of Whitehall; so that you need not doubt 'twill spread, and you will be as odious to the handsome young women, as—

Horner
As the smallpox. Well—

Quack
And to the married women of this end of the town, as—

Horner
As the great one; nay, as their own husbands.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 可你把认得的产婆都关照到了吗？戏园子里的卖橙女、城里的商贾丈夫、还有城里这一头那些老得不利索的包养汉——都说了吗？他们传这个最起劲。

**庸医.** 我认得的使女丫头、贴身女仆、梳妆娘子、老太太，一个不落；不但告诉了，还咬着耳朵当体己话说，连白厅里那些专好递话的也都通了风。您只管放心，保管传得开：往后那些俊俏的年轻娘们，要嫌恶您像——

**霍纳.** 像嫌恶天花。好——

**庸医.** 城里这一头那些有夫之妇呢，要嫌恶您像——

**霍纳.** 像嫌恶大天花；不，像嫌恶她们自家的丈夫。

===Original===
Quack
And to the city dames, as aniseed Robin, of filthy and contemptible memory; and they will frighten their children with your name, especially their females.

Horner
And cry, Horner's coming to carry you away. I am only afraid 'twill not be believed. You told 'em it was by an English-French disaster, and an English-French chirurgeon, who has given me at once not only a cure, but an antidote for the future against that damned malady, and that worse distemper, love, and all other women's evils?

Quack
Your late journey into France has made it the more credible, and your being here a fortnight before you appeared in public, looks as if you apprehended the shame, which I wonder you do not. Well, I have been hired by young gallants to belie 'em t'other way; but you are the first would be thought a man unfit for women.

Horner
Dear Mr. Doctor, let vain rogues be contented only to be thought abler men than they are, generally 'tis all the pleasure they have; but mine lies another way.

===Chinese===
**庸医.** 至于城里的太太们，要嫌恶您像那茴香罗宾——那匹腌臜透顶、遗臭万名的畜生；她们要拿您的名字吓唬孩子，尤其是小姑娘。

**霍纳.** 还要喊着：「霍纳来把你们抓走喽！」我只怕人家不肯信。你跟他们说了没有：说我是遭了一场英法合璧的灾祸，请了位英法合璧的外科大夫，一剂药下去，不但治好了那桩该死的恶疾，还给我留了味防身的解毒药，往后那更凶的病——爱情——连同一切女人的病灾，都沾不上我的身？

**庸医.** 您新近这趟法国之行，正叫这话听着可信；您回来先躲了半个月才出头露面，倒像是真怕这个臊似的——怪就怪您竟不臊。嘿，年轻的花花公子花钱雇我造谣，向来都是往自个儿脸上贴金；肯叫人把自己说成沾不得女人的，您是头一份。

**霍纳.** 亲爱的大夫先生，由着那些虚荣的浪子去独享「被当成比实际更能干的男人」的乐子罢——那通常就是他们全部的快活；我的快活却在另一条道上。

===Original===
Quack
You take, methinks, a very preposterous way to it, and as ridiculous as if we operators in physic should put forth bills to disparage our medicaments, with hopes to gain customers.

Horner
Doctor, there are quacks in love as well as physic, who get but the fewer and worse patients for their boasting; a good name is seldom got by giving it one's self; and women, no more than honour, are compassed by bragging. Come, come, Doctor, the wisest lawyer never discovers the merits of his cause till the trial; the wealthiest man conceals his riches, and the cunning gamester his play. Shy husbands and keepers, like old rooks, are not to be cheated but by a new unpractised trick: false friendship will pass now no more than false dice upon 'em; no, not in the city.

===Chinese===
**庸医.** 依我看，您这条路子可算走得本末倒置，荒唐得就像我们行医的贴出告示糟践自家药铺，指望这样招揽主顾。

**霍纳.** 大夫，情场上有江湖郎中，跟医道上一个样，吹牛皮只会叫病号越招越少、越招越糟；好名声从来少有自个儿封的；女人跟名誉一样，都不是吹出来的。得了，大夫：最精明的律师不到开庭不亮案由；最阔的人藏富不露，最老练的赌客不显手法。疑心重的丈夫和包养汉，跟赌桌上的老滑头一样，只有没使过的新招儿才骗得动：如今拿假交情对付他们，跟灌铅的骰子一样吃不开——城里也不兴。

===Original===
*Enter **Boy**.*

Boy
There are two ladies and a gentleman coming up.

*Exit.*

Horner
A pox! some unbelieving sisters of my former acquaintance, who, I am afraid, expect their sense should be satisfied of the falsity of the report. No—this formal fool and women!

*Enter **Sir Jasper Fidget**, **Lady Fidget**, and **Mrs. Dainty Fidget**.*

Quack
His wife and sister.

===Chinese===
*（**童儿**上。）*

**童儿.** 有两位太太和一位先生上楼来了。

*（下。）*

**霍纳.** 该死！准是从前相好的、不肯信邪的姊妹们，只怕要亲身来验一验那话儿是真是假。不对——是这位一板一眼的蠢货，还带着女眷！

*（**贾斯珀·菲吉特爵士**（Sir Jasper Fidget）、**菲吉特夫人**（Lady Fidget）与**丹蒂·菲吉特小姐**（Mrs. Dainty Fidget）上。）*

**庸医.** 是他的太太和妹妹。

===Original===
Sir Jasper
My coach breaking just now before your door, sir, I look upon as an occasional reprimand to me, sir, for not kissing your hands, sir, since your coming out of France, sir; and so my disaster, sir, has been my good fortune, sir; and this is my wife and sister, sir.

Horner
What then, sir?

Sir Jasper
My lady, and sister, sir.—Wife, this is Master Horner.

Lady Fidget
Master Horner, husband!

Sir Jasper
My lady, my Lady Fidget, sir.

Horner
So, sir.

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 先生，敝人的马车偏巧在您门前断了轴，先生，我看这是老天顺手给我的一番训诫，先生，训我打您打法国回来，先生，竟一直没来给您请安，先生；这么着，我的灾祸倒成了我的造化，先生；这位是内人和舍妹，先生。

**霍纳.** 那又如何呢，先生？

**贾斯珀爵士.** 夫人，还有舍妹，先生。——太太，这位是霍纳先生。

**菲吉特夫人.** 霍纳先生，丈夫！

**贾斯珀爵士.** 夫人，我的菲吉特夫人，先生。

**霍纳.** 是了，先生。

===Original===
Sir Jasper
Won't you be acquainted with her, sir?—*Aside.* So, the report is true, I find, by his coldness or aversion to the sex; but I'll play the wag with him.—*Aloud.* Pray salute my wife, my lady, sir.

Horner
I will kiss no man's wife, sir, for him, sir; I have taken my eternal leave, sir, of the sex already, sir.

Sir Jasper
*Aside.* Ha! ha! ha! I'll plague him yet.—*Aloud.* Not know my wife, sir?

Horner
I do know your wife, sir; she's a woman, sir, and consequently a monster, sir, a greater monster than a husband, sir.

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 您不愿同她结交么，先生？——（旁白）瞧这股子冷淡劲儿，谣言果然不假，他真是见不得女色；不过我要逗他一逗。——（高声）就请赏脸吻一吻内人，我的夫人，先生。

**霍纳.** 我可不替旁人去吻人家的太太，先生；我同女人这一路，先生，已经永别了，先生。

**贾斯珀爵士.**（旁白）哈！哈！哈！回头还得撩拨撩拨他。——（高声）连我太太都不肯认识，先生？

**霍纳.** 我认识您太太，先生；她是女人，先生，因此就是个怪物，先生，比丈夫还大的怪物，先生。

===Original===
Sir Jasper
A husband! how, sir?

Horner
So, sir; but I make no more cuckolds, sir. *Makes horns.*

Sir Jasper
Ha! ha! ha! Mercury! Mercury!

Lady Fidget
Pray, Sir Jasper, let us be gone from this rude fellow.

Mrs. Dainty
Who, by his breeding, would think he had ever been in France?

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 丈夫！此话怎讲，先生？

**霍纳.** 就是这么讲，先生；不过我再也不制造戴绿帽的了，先生。（做角手势。）

**贾斯珀爵士.** 哈！哈！哈！墨丘利！墨丘利！

**菲吉特夫人.** 求你了，贾斯珀爵士，快带咱们离开这个粗坯罢。

**丹蒂小姐.** 就凭他这副教养，谁想得到他还去过法国？

===Original===
Lady Fidget
Foh! he's but too much a French fellow, such as hate women of quality and virtue for their love to their husbands. Sir Jasper, a woman is hated by 'em as much for loving her husband as for loving their money. But pray let's be gone.

Horner
You do well, madam; for I have nothing that you came for. I have brought over not so much as a bawdy picture, no new postures, nor the second part of the *Ecole des Filles*; nor—

Quack
Hold, for shame, sir! what d'ye mean? you'll ruin yourself forever with the sex—*Apart to **Horner**.*

Sir Jasper
Ha! ha! ha! he hates women perfectly, I find.

Mrs. Dainty
What pity 'tis he should!

===Chinese===
**菲吉特夫人.** 呸！他何止像法国人，简直太像了——专恨那些有身份、有德行的太太爱自个儿的丈夫。贾斯珀爵士，女人爱丈夫，他们恨；女人爱他们的钱，他们也恨；恨得一样狠。快，咱们走罢。

**霍纳.** 夫人走得好；你们要的东西，我这里一概没有。我从法国回来，连张淫画都没捎，没有新姿势图，也没有《女子学校》（*Ecole des Filles*）的续篇；更没有——

**庸医.** 打住，不嫌害臊么，先生！您这是干什么？您要在女人跟前永世翻不了身——（向霍纳旁白。）

**贾斯珀爵士.** 哈！哈！哈！我看他真真是恨透了女人。

**丹蒂小姐.** 多可惜，他不该恨呀！

===Original===
Lady Fidget
Ay, he's a base fellow for't. But affectation makes not a woman more odious to them than virtue.

Horner
Because your virtue is your greatest affectation, madam.

Lady Fidget
How, you saucy fellow! would you wrong my honour?

Horner
If I could.

Lady Fidget
How d'ye mean, sir?

===Chinese===
**菲吉特夫人.** 可不是么，为这个他就是个下作坯。不过在她们眼里，叫女人更讨嫌的不是装腔作势，倒是贞洁。

**霍纳.** 因为夫人的贞洁，正是顶大的装腔作势呀，夫人。

**菲吉特夫人.** 什么，你这放肆的东西！想糟蹋我的名节？

**霍纳.** 要是我办得到。

**菲吉特夫人.** 你这话怎么讲，先生？

===Original===
Sir Jasper
Ha! ha! ha! no, he can't wrong your ladyship's honour, upon my honour. He, poor man—hark you in your ear—a mere eunuch. *Whispers.*

Lady Fidget
O filthy French beast! foh! foh! why do we stay? let's be gone: I can't endure the sight of him.

Sir Jasper
Stay but till the chairs come; they'll be here presently.

Lady Fidget
No, no.

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 哈！哈！哈！不，他糟蹋不了夫人的名节，我拿名节担保。他呀，可怜人——您附耳过来——实实在在是个阉人。（耳语。）

**菲吉特夫人.** 哎呀，腌臜的法国畜生！呸！呸！咱们还待着做什么？走罢：多瞧他一眼我都受不得。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 等轿子来了再走；这就到。

**菲吉特夫人.** 不等，不等。

===Original===
Sir Jasper
Nor can I stay longer. 'Tis, let me see, a quarter and half quarter of a minute past eleven. The council will be sat; I must away. Business must be preferred always before love and ceremony with the wise, Mr. Horner.

Horner
And the impotent, Sir Jasper.

Sir Jasper
Ay, ay, the impotent, Master Horner; hah! hah! hah!

Lady Fidget
What, leave us with a filthy man alone in his lodgings?

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 我也再待不住了。现在是——让我看看——十一点过一刻又半刻，掐得准准的。理事会要开席了；我得赶紧。霍纳先生，在明白人看来，公务永远得摆在爱情和礼数前头。

**霍纳.** 还有摆在不能人事的人前头，贾斯珀爵士。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 是，是，摆在不能人事的人前头，霍纳先生；哈！哈！哈！

**菲吉特夫人.** 什么，把我们扔在他寓所里，跟一个腌臜男人独处？

===Original===
Sir Jasper
He's an innocent man now, you know. Pray stay, I'll hasten the chairs to you.—Mr. Horner, your servant; I should be glad to see you at my house. Pray come and dine with me, and play at cards with my wife after dinner; you are fit for women at that game yet, ha! ha!—*Aside.* 'Tis as much a husband's prudence to provide innocent diversion for a wife as to hinder her unlawful pleasures; and he had better employ her than let her employ herself.—*Aloud.* Farewell.

Horner
Your servant, Sir Jasper.

*Exit **Sir Jasper**.*

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 他如今是个无害的人了，你是知道的。务请留下，我去催轿子。——霍纳先生，失陪；盼望您到舍下来坐坐。务请来舍下便饭，饭后陪我太太打牌；打那种牌，您对付女人还使得，哈！哈！——（旁白）给太太备下无害的消遣，跟拦住她偷情一样，都是做丈夫的精明；与其放她自行消遣，不如替她安排。——（高声）再会。

**霍纳.** 失陪，贾斯珀爵士。

*（贾斯珀爵士下。）*

===Original===
Lady Fidget
I will not stay with him, foh!—

Horner
Nay, madam, I beseech you stay, if it be but to see I can be as civil to ladies yet as they would desire.

Lady Fidget
No, no, foh! you cannot be civil to ladies.

Mrs. Dainty
You as civil as ladies would desire?

Lady Fidget
No, no, no, foh! foh! foh!

*Exeunt **Lady Fidget** and **Mrs. Dainty Fidget**.*

===Chinese===
**菲吉特夫人.** 我才不跟他待在一处，呸！——

**霍纳.** 别呀，夫人，千万留下，就当看看我对太太们还能不能照旧客气周全。

**菲吉特夫人.** 不，不，呸！你对太太们客气不起来。

**丹蒂小姐.** 你还能对太太们客气周全？

**菲吉特夫人.** 不，不，不，呸！呸！呸！

*（菲吉特夫人与丹蒂·菲吉特小姐同下。）*

===Original===
Quack
Now, I think, I, or you yourself, rather, have done your business with the women.

Horner
Thou art an ass. Don't you see already, upon the report, and my carriage, this grave man of business leaves his wife in my lodgings, invites me to his house and wife, who before would not be acquainted with me out of jealousy?

Quack
Nay, by this means you may be the more acquainted with the husbands, but the less with the wives.

===Chinese===
**庸医.** 这下我看，是我——不如说是您自个儿——把您跟女人的缘分断送干净了。

**霍纳.** 你是头蠢驴。这都看不出来么：单凭这谣言加我这份做派，那位一本正经的忙人就把自家太太撂在我寓所里，还请我上他家、陪太太——先前他倒是因为吃醋，不肯同我结交呢。

**庸医.** 话是这么说；可这么一来，您同丈夫们倒热络了，同太太们反倒生分了。

===Original===
Horner
Let me alone; if I can but abuse the husbands, I'll soon disabuse the wives. Stay—I'll reckon you up the advantages I am like to have by my stratagem. First, I shall be rid of all my old acquaintances, the most insatiable sort of duns, that invade our lodgings in a morning; and next to the pleasure of making a new mistress is that of being rid of an old one, and of all old debts. Love, when it comes to be so, is paid the most unwillingly.

Quack
Well, you may be so rid of your old acquaintances; but how will you get any new ones?

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 瞧我的罢；只要把丈夫们哄住，太太们那边，我很快就能叫她们卸下防备。慢着——我给你细细算算这条妙计的好处。头一件，我能甩掉全部旧相好——那是顶不知足的一路讨债鬼，大清早就闯进咱们的寓所；而仅次于弄上手一个新情人的乐事，便是甩掉一个旧的，连旧债一并勾销。情债，到了要还的份上，还起来顶不情愿。

**庸医.** 好，旧相好您是这么甩得掉了；可新的又上哪儿捞去？

===Original===
Horner
Doctor, thou wilt never make a good chemist, thou art so incredulous and impatient. Ask but all the young fellows of the town if they do not lose more time, like huntsmen, in starting the game, than in running it down. One knows not where to find 'em; who will or will not. Women of quality are so civil, you can hardly distinguish love from good breeding, and a man is often mistaken: but now I can be sure she that shows an aversion to me loves the sport, as those women that are gone, whom I warrant to be right. And then the next thing is, your women of honour, as you call 'em, are only chary of their reputations, not their persons; and 'tis scandal they would avoid, not men. Now may I have, by the reputation of an eunuch, the privileges of one, and be seen in a lady's chamber in a morning as early as her husband; kiss virgins before their parents or lovers; and may be, in short, the passe-partout of the town. Now, doctor.

Quack
Nay, now you shall be the doctor; and your process is so new that we do not know but it may succeed.

Horner
Not so new neither; *probatum est*, doctor.

Quack
Well, I wish you luck, and many patients, whilst I go to mine.

*Exit.*

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 大夫，你这人又不肯信、又沉不住气，一辈子当不成好药剂师。去问问城里所有的年轻人：他们像猎户似的，惊起猎物搭的工夫，是不是比追捕猎物还多。谁也摸不清上哪儿找，哪个肯、哪个不肯。有身份的太太们客气周到，你简直分不清那是情意还是教养，男人常常看走眼：如今我可拿得稳了——对我满脸嫌恶的，恰恰是爱这一路的，方才走的那两位就是，我担保货真价实。再者，你们所谓的名节妇人，惜的只是名声，不是身子；她们要避的是丑闻，不是男人。这么着，我顶着阉人的名声，就能享阉人的待遇：一清早出现在太太闺房，来得跟她丈夫一样早；当着爹娘和情人的面亲吻姑娘；总而言之，做全城通行的万能钥匙（passe-partout）。怎么样，大夫。

**庸医.** 嗨，这回您倒该当大夫了；您这套方子新鲜得很，保不齐真能灵。

**霍纳.** 也不算新鲜了；「已验有效」（*probatum est*），大夫。

**庸医.** 好，祝您走运，病号盈门；我也瞧我自个儿的病号去了。

*（下。）*

===Original===
*Enter **Harcourt** and **Dorilant**.*

Harcourt
Come, your appearance at the play yesterday, has, I hope, hardened you for the future against the women's contempt, and the men's raillery; and now you'll abroad as you were wont.

Horner
Did I not bear it bravely?

Dorilant
With a most theatrical impudence, nay, more than the orange-wenches show there, or a drunken vizard-mask, or a great-bellied actress; nay, or the most impudent of creatures, an ill poet; or what is yet more impudent, a secondhand critic.

===Chinese===
*（**哈考特**（Harcourt）与**多里兰特**（Dorilant）上。）*

**哈考特.** 得了，你昨天在戏园子里露的那一面，我希望，往后任凭女人们怎么小看、爷们们怎么打趣，你都脸皮够硬了；如今你又照老样子出门走动了。

**霍纳.** 我不是硬挺过来了么？够体面罢？

**多里兰特.** 挺过来了，那股不要脸的劲儿真够舞台腔的——比戏园子里的卖橙女还足，比喝醉的蒙面女、挺着大肚子的女戏子还足；比天下顶不要脸的造物——蹩脚诗人——还足；比更要不得的二手批评家还足。

===Original===
Horner
But what say the ladies? have they no pity?

Harcourt
What ladies? The vizard-masks, you know, never pity a man when all's gone, though in their service.

Dorilant
And for the women in the boxes, you'd never pity them when 'twas in your power.

Harcourt
They say 'tis pity but all that deal with common women should be served so.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 那女人们怎么说？她们就不发点慈悲？

**哈考特.** 什么女人们？蒙面女你是知道的：等你油水榨干，哪怕是为她们效过力，也断不怜你。

**多里兰特.** 至于包厢里那些太太，从前你有钱有势时，可从没可怜过她们。

**哈考特.** 人家说，凡是同窑姐儿有来往的，都该受这么一遭，才解气。

===Original===
Dorilant
Nay, I dare swear they won't admit you to play at cards with them, go to plays with 'em, or do the little duties which other shadows of men are wont to do for 'em.

Horner
What do you call shadows of men?

Dorilant
Half-men.

Horner
What, boys?

Dorilant
Ay, your old boys, old *beaux garçons*, who, like superannuated stallions, are suffered to run, feed, and whinny with the mares as long as they live, though they can do nothing else.

===Chinese===
**多里兰特.** 还有呢，我敢起誓，她们再不会让你凑局打牌、陪她们看戏，也不叫你干那些「男人的影子」惯常替她们当的零碎差事了。

**霍纳.** 你们说的「男人的影子」是些什么？

**多里兰特.** 半个男人。

**霍纳.** 什么，娃娃么？

**多里兰特.** 对，你们那号老娃娃、老「俊哥儿」（*beaux garçons*）；好比超龄退役的种马，任它一辈子跟母马群一道跑、一道吃、一道打响鼻，别的事一概干不成了。

===Original===
Horner
Well, a pox on love and wenching! Women serve but to keep a man from better company. Though I can't enjoy them, I shall you the more. Good fellowship and friendship are lasting, rational, and manly pleasures.

Harcourt
For all that, give me some of those pleasures you call effeminate too; they help to relish one another.

Horner
They disturb one another.

Harcourt
No, mistresses are like books. If you pore upon them too much, they doze you, and make you unfit for company; but if used discreetly, you are the fitter for conversation by 'em.

Dorilant
A mistress should be like a little country retreat near the town; not to dwell in constantly, but only for a night and away, to taste the town the better when a man returns.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 得了，叫爱情和嫖姑娘都遭瘟去罢！女人不过是拦着男人、不叫他与更好的伴儿来往。我既然享用不成她们，正好多同你们亲近。哥们义气和交情，才是长久、在理、有男子气的乐子。

**哈考特.** 话虽如此，你叫做脂粉气的那些乐子，也分我一点；两样对搭着才够味。

**霍纳.** 两样只会互相搅局。

**哈考特.** 不，情人好比书。一味死啃，就把你啃得昏昏欲睡，上不得台盘；用得有分寸，倒叫你谈吐更见精神。

**多里兰特.** 情人就该像城郊一处乡间小别墅；不是叫你长住的，只过一夜就走，这样回城来才更咂得出城里的滋味。

===Original===
Horner
I tell you, 'tis as hard to be a good fellow, a good friend, and a lover of women, as 'tis to be a good fellow, a good friend, and a lover of money. You cannot follow both, then choose your side. Wine gives you liberty, love takes it away.

Dorilant
Gad, he's in the right on't.

Horner
Wine gives you joy; love, grief and tortures, besides surgeons. Wine makes us witty; love, only sots. Wine makes us sleep; love breaks it.

Dorilant
By the world he has reason, Harcourt.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 我告诉你，要做个好哥们、好朋友，又做女人的相好，就跟要做好哥们、好朋友，又做钱财的贪主一样难。两头不能兼顾，那就选边站罢。酒给你自由，爱把它收走。

**多里兰特.** 天哪，他这话在理。

**霍纳.** 酒给你欢喜；爱给你愁苦加刑罚，外带外科大夫。酒叫人风趣；爱只叫人变痴汉。酒催人入睡；爱搅人的觉。

**多里兰特.** 皇天在上，他有理，哈考特。

===Original===
Horner
Wine makes—

Dorilant
Ay, wine makes us—makes us princes; love makes us beggars, poor rogues, egad—and wine—

Horner
So, there's one converted.—No, no, love and wine, oil and vinegar.

Harcourt
I grant it; love will still be uppermost.

Horner
Come, for my part, I will have only those glorious manly pleasures of being very drunk and very slovenly.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 酒叫人——

**多里兰特.** 对，酒叫我们——叫我们当王爷；爱叫我们当叫花子、当穷瘪三，真的——还有酒——

**霍纳.** 瞧，这儿感化了一个。——不，不，情跟酒，就像油跟醋。

**哈考特.** 我认账；不过爱总归浮在上头。

**霍纳.** 来罢，我这一份，就只要那种光荣的、男子气的乐子：喝个烂醉，邋里邋遢。

===Original===
*Enter **Boy**.*

Boy
Mr. Sparkish is below, sir.

*Exit.*

Harcourt
What, my dear friend! a rogue that is fond of me only, I think, for abusing him.

Dorilant
No, he can no more think the men laugh at him than that women jilt him; his opinion of himself is so good.

===Chinese===
*（**童儿**上。）*

**童儿.** 先生，斯帕基什（Sparkish）先生在楼下。

*（下。）*

**哈考特.** 什么，我亲爱的朋友！这家伙偏偏喜欢我，我想，就因为我老糟践他。

**多里兰特.** 哪里，他打死也想不到爷们儿在笑话他，就像想不到娘们儿在耍他；他对自己评价高着呢。

===Original===
Horner
Well, there's another pleasure by drinking I thought not of—I shall lose his acquaintance, because he cannot drink: and you know 'tis a very hard thing to be rid of him; for he's one of those nauseous offerers at wit, who, like the worst fiddlers, run themselves into all companies.

Harcourt
One that, by being in the company of men of sense, would pass for one.

Horner
And may so to the shortsighted world; as a false jewel amongst true ones is not discerned at a distance. His company is as troublesome to us as a cuckold's when you have a mind to his wife's.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 嘿，喝酒还有一样好处我先前没盘算到——这样就能甩脱他，因为他喝不得酒：你们知道，要摆脱他可难了；他是那种叫人作呕的硬凑趣的活宝，好比顶蹩脚的提琴手，哪儿有人堆就往哪儿扎。

**哈考特.** 靠着混在明白人堆里，就想混充个明白人。

**霍纳.** 眼神不济的世人没准儿真叫他瞒过去了；就像假宝石混在真宝石里，离远了瞧不出。他碍我们的事，就跟你瞧上了谁家老婆、那家的绿帽丈夫偏在跟前碍事一样。

===Original===
Harcourt
No, the rogue will not let us enjoy one another, but ravishes our conversation; though he signifies no more to't than Sir Martin Mar-all's4 gaping, and awkward thrumming upon the lute, does to his man's voice and music.

Dorilant
And to pass for a wit in town shows himself a fool every night to us, that are guilty of the plot.

Horner
Such wits as he are, to a company of reasonable men, like rooks to the gamesters; who only fill a room at the table, but are so far from contributing to the play, that they only serve to spoil the fancy of those that do.

Dorilant
Nay, they are used like rooks too, snubbed, checked, and abused; yet the rogues will hang on.

Horner
A pox on 'em, and all that force nature, and would be still what she forbids 'em! Affectation is her greatest monster.

===Chinese===
**哈考特.** 可不是，这家伙不叫咱们好好受用彼此，专会糟蹋咱们的谈话；其实他在这上头的用场，还不及马丁·马奥尔爵士4那副张着嘴、笨手拨弄琵琶的架势，配他仆人的嗓子和曲子来得大。

**多里兰特.** 他在城里混充才子，夜夜在我们这些晓得内情的局内人跟前露怯。

**霍纳.** 他这路才子，搁在一屋子明白人里，就像赌桌上的骗棍对赌客：只配把桌边的位子填满，非但帮不上局，反倒败坏真赌客的兴致。

**多里兰特.** 还不止呢，他们受的待遇也跟骗棍一样：挨抢白、受钳制、遭痛骂；可这些家伙偏要死赖着不撤。

**霍纳.** 叫他们遭瘟去罢，还有一切硬拧着天性、非要当天性不许他们当的东西的人！装腔作势，是天性造出的头号怪物。

===Original===
Harcourt
Most men are the contraries to that they would seem. Your bully, you see, is a coward with a long sword; the little humbly-fawning physician, with his ebony cane, is he that destroys men.

Dorilant
The usurer, a poor rogue, possessed of mouldy bonds and mortgages; and we they call spendthrifts, are only wealthy, who lay out his money upon daily new purchases of pleasure.

Horner
Ay, your arrantest cheat is your trustee or executor; your jealous man, the greatest cuckold; your churchman the greatest atheist; and your noisy pert rogue of a wit, the greatest fop, dullest ass, and worst company, as you shall see; for here he comes.

*Enter **Sparkish**.*

===Chinese===
**哈考特.** 世人多半同他们装出来的正相反。你瞧，逞凶的恶棍挎着长剑，其实是个胆小鬼；那个点头哈腰、拄乌木手杖的小个子郎中，才是真正要人命的。

**多里兰特.** 放印子钱的是个穷瘪三，守着一堆发了霉的债券抵押契；倒是他们叫做败家子的我们，才真阔气——天天把本钱拿去置办新出的快活。

**霍纳.** 对，头一号的骗子是你的受托人、遗嘱执行人；顶会吃醋的，绿帽戴得顶大；教会里的人顶不信神；至于那个吵吵嚷嚷、轻狂自是的所谓才子——头号花花公子、顶蠢的蠢驴、顶要命的同伴——你们这就瞧罢；他来了。

*（**斯帕基什**上。）*

===Original===
Sparkish
How is't, sparks? how is't? Well, faith, Harry, I must rally thee a little, ha! ha! ha! upon the report in town of thee, ha! ha! ha! I can't hold i'faith; shall I speak?

Horner
Yes; but you'll be so bitter then.

Sparkish
Honest Dick and Frank here shall answer for me; I will not be extreme bitter, by the universe.

Harcourt
We will be bound in a ten thousand pound bond, he shall not be bitter at all.

Dorilant
Nor sharp, nor sweet.

Horner
What, not downright insipid?

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 各位好吗，哥儿们？好吗？嘿，说真的，哈利，我可要打趣你两句了，哈！哈！哈！说说城里传你的那些话，哈！哈！哈！实在憋不住了——我可以说么？

**霍纳.** 说罢；只是你到时候嘴上不留情。

**斯帕基什.** 老实的迪克和弗兰克在这儿给我作保：凭宇宙起誓，我绝不往死里刻薄。

**哈考特.** 我们情愿立一张一万镑的保票：他一丁点儿也不许刻薄。

**多里兰特.** 不得辣，也不得甜。

**霍纳.** 怎么，难道还不落个寡淡无味？

===Original===
Sparkish
Nay then, since you are so brisk, and provoke me, take what follows. You must know, I was discoursing and rallying with some ladies yesterday, and they happened to talk of the fine new signs in town—

Horner
Very fine ladies, I believe.

Sparkish
Said I, I know where the best new sign is.—Where? says one of the ladies.—In Covent-Garden, I replied.—Said another, In what street?—In Russel-street, answered I.—Lord, says another, I'm sure there was never a fine new sign there yesterday.—Yes, but there was, said I again; and it came out of France, and has been there a fortnight.

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 那好啊，既然你们这么来劲招惹我，就接招罢。你们得知道，昨天我正同几位太太闲谈打趣，她们碰巧说起城里漂亮的新招牌——

**霍纳.** 想必是几位漂亮的太太。

**斯帕基什.** 我说：我知道顶漂亮的新招牌在哪儿。——在哪儿？一位太太问。——在科文特花园，我答道。——另一位问：在哪条街？——罗素街，我回答。——天哪，又一位说，我敢保昨天罗素街可没有什么漂亮新招牌。——有的，有的，我又说；那招牌还是打法国运来的，挂了半个月了。

===Original===
Dorilant
A pox! I can hear no more, prithee.

Horner
No, hear him out; let him tune his crowd a while.

Harcourt
The worst music, the greatest preparation.

Sparkish
Nay, faith, I'll make you laugh.—It cannot be, says a third lady.—Yes, yes, quoth I again.—Says a fourth lady—

Horner
Look to't, we'll have no more ladies.

===Chinese===
**多里兰特.** 遭瘟！我听不下去了，行行好。

**霍纳.** 别，听他说完；让他先把琴弦调一阵子。

**哈考特.** 顶蹩脚的曲子，顶大的排场。

**斯帕基什.** 别急，说真的，保管叫你们笑。——不可能，第三位太太说。——可能，可能，我又道。——第四位太太说——

**霍纳.** 留神，太太们可不能再添了。

===Original===
Sparkish
No—then mark, mark, now. Said I to the fourth, Did you never see Mr. Horner? he lodges in Russel-street, and he's a sign of a man, you know, since he came out of France; ha! ha! ha!

Horner
But the devil take me if thine be the sign of a jest.

Sparkish
With that they all fell a-laughing, till they bepissed themselves. What, but it does not move you, methinks? Well, I see one had as good go to law without a witness, as break a jest without a laugher on one's side.—Come, come, sparks, but where do we dine? I have left at Whitehall an earl, to dine with you.

Dorilant
Why, I thought thou hadst loved a man with a title, better than a suit with a French trimming to't.

Harcourt
Go to him again.

Sparkish
No, sir, a wit to me is the greatest title in the world.

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 不添——那你们可听好了，听好了。我对第四位说：您没见过霍纳先生么？他就住在罗素街；您知道，他打法国回来，成了一块「男子汉招牌」了；哈！哈！哈！

**霍纳.** 不过，你这句要也算得笑话的招牌，就让魔鬼把我抓了去。

**斯帕基什.** 这一下她们全笑开了，一直笑到尿了裤子。怎么，竟挠不着你们的痒处？唉，我看人讲笑话身边没个捧场的，还不如打官司不带证人。——得了得了，哥儿们，咱们上哪儿吃饭？为了同你们吃饭，我把白厅一位伯爵都撂下了。

**多里兰特.** 咦，我还当你爱有头衔的爷们，胜过爱一身镶法国绦子的衣裳呢。

**哈考特.** 快回伯爵那儿去罢。

**斯帕基什.** 不，先生，「才子」在我眼里是天底下顶大的头衔。

===Original===
Horner
But go dine with your earl, sir; he may be exception. We are your friends, and will not take it ill to be left, I do assure you.

Harcourt
Nay, faith, he shall go to him.

Sparkish
Nay, pray, gentlemen.

Dorilant
We'll thrust you out, if you won't; what, disappoint anybody for us?

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 还是去陪你的伯爵吃饭罢，先生；他没准儿要见怪。我们是你的朋友，撂下我们决不往心里去，这个我担保。

**哈考特.** 话说回来，他就该回伯爵那儿去。

**斯帕基什.** 别价，各位爷。

**多里兰特.** 你不走我们就把你推出去；怎么，还肯为我们回掉旁人？

===Original===
Sparkish
Nay, dear gentlemen, hear me.

Horner
No, no, sir, by no means; pray go, sir.

Sparkish
Why, dear rogues—

Dorilant
No, no.

*They all thrust him out of the room.*

All
Ha! ha! ha!

*Reenter **Sparkish**.*

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 哎，各位亲爱的爷，听我说。

**霍纳.** 不必，不必，先生；千万请去，先生。

**斯帕基什.** 嗐，亲爱的哥们——

**多里兰特.** 不，不。

*（众人把他推出房去。）*

**众人.** 哈！哈！哈！

*（斯帕基什又上。）*

===Original===
Sparkish
But, sparks, pray hear me. What, d'ye think I'll eat then with gay shallow fops and silent coxcombs? I think wit as necessary at dinner, as a glass of good wine; and that's the reason I never have any stomach when I eat alone.—Come, but where do we dine?

Horner
Even where you will.

Sparkish
At Chateline's?

Dorilant
Yes, if you will.

Sparkish
Or at the Cock?5

Dorilant
Yes, if you please.

Sparkish
Or at the Dog and Partridge?

Horner
Ay, if you have a mind to't; for we shall dine at neither.

Sparkish
Pshaw! with your fooling we shall lose the new play; and I would no more miss seeing a new play the first day, than I would miss sitting in the wit's row. Therefore I'll go fetch my mistress, and away.

*Exit.*

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 可是，哥儿们，千万听我说。怎么，你们当我肯跟那些轻浮浅薄的花花公子、闷嘴的绣花枕头一道吃饭？我看吃饭同好酒一样，缺不得才气；所以我一个人吃饭，从来提不起胃口。——得了，咱们上哪儿吃？

**霍纳.** 你说到哪儿就哪儿。

**斯帕基什.** 上夏特兰馆（Chateline's）？

**多里兰特.** 行啊，随你。

**斯帕基什.** 还是上「公鸡」馆5？

**多里兰特.** 行啊，随你高兴。

**斯帕基什.** 还是上「狗与鹧鸪」馆？

**霍纳.** 嗯，你乐意就去；反正咱们哪家也吃不成。

**斯帕基什.** 啧！跟你们这么胡搅，新戏都要误了；新戏头一天我是一定要看的，就像才子席上的位子我是一定要坐的。那我这就去接我的情人，回头见。

*（下。）*

===Original===
*Enter **Pinchwife**.*

Horner
Who have we here? Pinchwife?

Pinchwife
Gentlemen, your humble servant.

Horner
Well, Jack, by thy long absence from the town, the grumness of thy countenance, and the slovenliness of thy habit, I should give thee joy, should I not, of marriage?

Pinchwife
*Aside.* Death! does he know I'm married too? I thought to have concealed it from him at least.—*Aloud.* My long stay in the country will excuse my dress; and I have a suit of law that brings me up to town, that puts me out of humour. Besides, I must give Sparkish tomorrow five thousand pounds to lie with my sister.

===Chinese===
*（**平奇怀夫**（Pinchwife）上。）*

**霍纳.** 这位是谁？平奇怀夫？

**平奇怀夫.** 各位先生，卑人这厢有礼。

**霍纳.** 好啊，杰克，瞧你久不进城、眉头紧锁、衣衫邋遢这光景，我该当贺你一贺——贺你新婚，对不对？

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）要命！他连我结了婚也知道？我还当至少瞒得过他。——（高声）我在乡下待得久，衣冠上就怠慢了；进城是为一桩官司，搅得心里烦。再说，明天我还得点交五千镑给斯帕基什，好叫他睡我妹妹。

===Original===
Horner
Nay, you country gentlemen, rather than not purchase, will buy anything; and he is a cracked title, if we may quibble. Well, but am I to give thee joy? I heard thou wert married.

Pinchwife
What then?

Horner
Why, the next thing that is to be heard, is, thou'rt a cuckold.

Pinchwife
Insupportable name! *Aside.*

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 瞧你们乡绅，只要能置办，什么都肯买；要说俏皮话，这可是一桩「产权带裂纹」的买卖。好罢，我究竟该不该贺你？听说你结了婚。

**平奇怀夫.** 那又怎样？

**霍纳.** 怎样？接下来就该听说，你戴绿帽了。

**平奇怀夫.** 挨千刀的名目！（旁白。）

===Original===
Horner
But I did not expect marriage from such a whoremaster as you; one that knew the town so much, and women so well.

Pinchwife
Why, I have married no London wife.

Horner
Pshaw! that's all one. That grave circumspection in marrying a country wife, is like refusing a deceitful pampered Smithfield jade, to go and be cheated by a friend in the country.

Pinchwife
*Aside.* A pox on him and his simile!—*Aloud.* At least we are a little surer of the breed there, know what her keeping has been, whether foiled or unsound.

Horner
Come, come, I have known a clap gotten in Wales; and there are cousins, justices' clerks, and chaplains in the country, I won't say coachmen. But she's handsome and young?

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 只是我没想到你这号老嫖客也结婚；你是深谙城里事、也深谙女人的呀。

**平奇怀夫.** 嗐，我娶的又不是伦敦老婆。

**霍纳.** 啐！还不是一码事。娶乡下老婆时那副一本正经的小心劲儿，就好比回绝了史密斯菲尔德集市上一匹喂惯了的滑头母马，转身到乡下叫朋友诓去一匹。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）叫他和他的比方一道遭瘟去！——（高声）至少那边的血统靠得住些，晓得她是怎么调养大的，失没失过蹄、带没带暗疾。

**霍纳.** 得了得了，威尔士得淋病的事我就见过；乡下也有表亲、治安官的书记、教堂牧师——马车夫我都不提。不过她总归年轻标致罢？

===Original===
Pinchwife
*Aside.* I'll answer as I should do.—*Aloud.* No, no; she has no beauty but her youth, no attraction but her modesty: wholesome, homely, and huswifely; that's all.

Dorilant
He talks as like a grazier as he looks.

Pinchwife
She's too awkward, ill-favoured, and silly to bring to town.

Harcourt
Then methinks you should bring her to be taught breeding.

Pinchwife
To be taught! no, sir, I thank you. Good wives and private soldiers should be ignorant—I'll keep her from your instructions, I warrant you.

Harcourt
The rogue is as jealous as if his wife were not ignorant. *Aside.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）我得当着本分答话。——（高声）不，不；她除了年轻没有姿色，除了本分没有魅力：健壮、家常、会持家；就这么些。

**多里兰特.** 他人长得像贩牲口的，说话也像。

**平奇怀夫.** 她太粗手笨脚、相貌寒碜、人又憨，带不进城里来。

**哈考特.** 那依我说，你倒该带她来学学教养。

**平奇怀夫.** 学教养！不必了，先生，多谢。好老婆跟列兵一样，就该无知——我保管不叫她沾各位的教诲。

**哈考特.**（旁白）这小子醋劲这么大，倒像他老婆当真不无知似的。

===Original===
Horner
Why, if she be ill-favoured, there will be less danger here for you than by leaving her in the country. We have such variety of dainties that we are seldom hungry.

Dorilant
But they have always coarse, constant, swingeing stomachs in the country.

Harcourt
Foul feeders indeed!

Dorilant
And your hospitality is great there.

Harcourt
Open house; every man's welcome.

Pinchwife
So, so, gentlemen.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 这么说，她既然相貌寒碜，搁在城里倒比撂在乡下稳妥。我们这儿珍馐滋味见得多了，轻易不觉得饿。

**多里兰特.** 可乡下人的胃口，向来又粗、又恒、又吓人。

**哈考特.** 真真是不挑嘴的饿痨！

**多里兰特.** 再说你们乡下待客又殷勤。

**哈考特.** 敞开大门；来者皆是客。

**平奇怀夫.** 好了，好了，诸位。

===Original===
Horner
But prithee, why shouldst thou marry her? If she be ugly, ill-bred, and silly, she must be rich then.

Pinchwife
As rich as if she brought me twenty thousand pound out of this town; for she'll be as sure not to spend her moderate portion, as a London baggage would be to spend hers, let it be what it would: so 'tis all one. Then, because she's ugly, she's the likelier to be my own; and being ill-bred, she'll hate conversation; and since silly and innocent, will not know the difference betwixt a man of one-and-twenty and one of forty.

Horner
Nine—to my knowledge. But if she be silly, she'll expect as much from a man of forty-nine, as from him of one-and-twenty. But methinks wit is more necessary than beauty; and I think no young woman ugly that has it, and no handsome woman agreeable without it.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 可你倒说说，何必娶她？她要是丑、没教养、又憨，那必定有钱了。

**平奇怀夫.** 有钱得很——抵得上她从城里给我带来两万镑；因为她那份不大的嫁妆，保管一个子儿不动，正如换个伦敦娘们儿，有多少得花多少：所以两下扯平。再者，她丑，就更是我一个人的；没教养，就讨厌应酬；又憨又天真，分不清二十一岁的男人和四十岁的男人有什么两样。

**霍纳.** 四十九——据我所知。可她要是真憨，她管四十九岁的男人要的，会同管二十一岁的一样多。依我看，脑子比脸蛋更要紧；我看有了脑子的年轻女人就不丑，没了脑子的标致女人也不可爱。

===Original===
Pinchwife
'Tis my maxim, he's a fool that marries; but he's a greater that does not marry a fool. What is wit in a wife good for, but to make a man a cuckold?

Horner
Yes, to keep it from his knowledge.

Pinchwife
A fool cannot contrive to make her husband a cuckold.

Horner
No; but she'll club with a man that can: and what is worse, if she cannot make her husband a cuckold, she'll make him jealous, and pass for one: and then 'tis all one.

Pinchwife
Well, well, I'll take care for one. My wife shall make me no cuckold, though she had your help, Mr. Horner. I understand the town, sir.

Dorilant
His help! *Aside.*

Harcourt
He's come newly to town, it seems, and has not heard how things are with him. *Aside.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 这是我的格言：结婚的是傻子；可不娶个傻子的，是更大的傻子。老婆有脑子顶什么用？不就是叫丈夫戴绿帽么。

**霍纳.** 不，用处是瞒着丈夫、不叫知道。

**平奇怀夫.** 傻婆娘自个儿想不出法子叫丈夫戴绿帽。

**霍纳.** 是想不出；可她会同想得出的人搭伙：更糟的是，她若叫丈夫戴不成绿帽，就叫丈夫吃醋，混充一个戴绿帽的：反正一个样。

**平奇怀夫.** 好了好了，我自会当心。我老婆休想叫我戴绿帽，哪怕有霍纳先生你搭把手。我懂城里这一套，先生。

**多里兰特.**（旁白）他搭把手！

**哈考特.**（旁白）看来他新近进城，还没听说他如今是什么行情。

===Original===
Horner
But tell me, has marriage cured thee of whoring, which it seldom does?

Harcourt
'Tis more than age can do.

Horner
No, the word is, I'll marry and live honest: but a marriage vow is like a penitent gamester's oath, and entering into bonds and penalties to stint himself to such a particular small sum at play for the future, which makes him but the more eager; and not being able to hold out, loses his money again, and his forfeit to boot.

Dorilant
Ay, ay, a gamester will be a gamester whilst his money lasts, and a whoremaster whilst his vigour.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 不过告诉我，结了婚，可把你嫖的毛病治好了？婚姻治好这毛病的可不多。

**哈考特.** 这可是年岁都办不到的事。

**霍纳.** 办得到；口头禅总是：「我这就结婚，往后规规矩矩做人」；可结婚誓约就像赌徒的悔赌咒，又是立契、又是认罚，限定往后只准小注玩玩；这么一限，反倒勾得他更馋；熬到头熬不住，钱又输个精光，罚金还得搭上。

**多里兰特.** 是，是，赌徒有钱赌到底，嫖客有力气嫖到底。

===Original===
Harcourt
Nay, I have known 'em, when they are broke, and can lose no more, keep a fumbling with the box in their hands to fool with only, and hinder other gamesters.

Dorilant
That had wherewithal to make lusty stakes.

Pinchwife
Well, gentlemen, you may laugh at me; but you shall never lie with my wife: I know the town.

Horner
But prithee, was not the way you were in better? is not keeping better than marriage?

Pinchwife
A pox on't! the jades would jilt me, I could never keep a whore to myself.

===Chinese===
**哈考特.** 不止呢，我见过输光了、再无可输的，还把骰盅攥在手里摸来弄去过干瘾，光碍真赌客的事。

**多里兰特.** 碍的还都是下得起大注的。

**平奇怀夫.** 好了，先生们，你们尽管笑我；可你们休想睡我老婆：我懂城里这一套。

**霍纳.** 不过说真的，你原先那条道不是更好么？包养不比结婚强？

**平奇怀夫.** 遭瘟的东西！那些贱蹄子净耍我，我连一个窑姐儿都没能独享过。

===Original===
Horner
So, then you only married to keep a whore to yourself. Well, but let me tell you, women, as you say, are like soldiers, made constant and loyal by good pay, rather than by oaths and covenants. Therefore I'd advise my friends to keep rather than marry, since too I find, by your example, it does not serve one's turn; for I saw you yesterday in the eighteenpenny place with a pretty country-wench.

Pinchwife
*Aside.* How the devil! did he see my wife then? I sat there that she might not be seen. But she shall never go to a play again. *Aside.*

Horner
What! dost thou blush, at nine-and-forty, for having been seen with a wench?

Dorilant
No, faith, I warrant 'twas his wife, which he seated there out of sight; for he's a cunning rogue, and understands the town.

Harcourt
He blushes. Then 'twas his wife; for men are now more ashamed to be seen with them in public than with a wench.

Pinchwife
*Aside.* Hell and damnation! I'm undone, since Horner has seen her, and they know 'twas she. *Aside.*

Horner
But prithee, was it thy wife? She was exceeding pretty: I was in love with her at that distance.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 原来如此，你结婚，就为了独享一个贱蹄子。好罢，有句话奉告：女人么，照你方才说的，跟当兵的一样，忠贞是饷银养出来的，不是誓约和契约拴出来的。所以我劝朋友们，宁养外室，莫进洞房；何况照你这先例看，结婚也不顶事：昨天我还在十八便士的座位上瞧见你，带着个标致的乡下姑娘。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）见鬼！他到底瞧见我老婆了？我坐那儿就为叫人瞧不见她。往后她再也别想看戏。

**霍纳.** 什么！四十九岁的人了，带着个姑娘叫人瞧见，还臊得脸红？

**多里兰特.** 不，说真的，我保险那是他老婆，他才特意拣个背人的座位安置她；这小子精着呢，懂城里这一套。

**哈考特.** 他脸红了。那就是老婆无疑了；如今男人当众带着老婆，可比带着姑娘还害臊。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）地狱和天罚！全完了，霍纳瞧见了她，他们也知道那就是她。

**霍纳.** 喂，说正经的，那可是你老婆？她标致得很：隔着那一段距离，我已经爱上她了。

===Original===
Pinchwife
You are like never to be nearer to her. Your servant, gentlemen. *Offers to go.*

Horner
Nay, prithee stay.

Pinchwife
I cannot; I will not.

Horner
Come, you shall dine with us.

Pinchwife
I have dined already.

Horner
Come, I know thou hast not: I'll treat thee, dear rogue; thou sha't spend none of thy Hampshire money today.

Pinchwife
Treat me! So, he uses me already like his cuckold. *Aside.*

Horner
Nay, you shall not go.

Pinchwife
I must; I have business at home.

*Exit.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 你怕是永远也挨不近她了。各位先生，告辞。（作势欲走。）

**霍纳.** 嗨，别走。

**平奇怀夫.** 不能留；偏不留。

**霍纳.** 来，同我们吃饭去。

**平奇怀夫.** 吃过了。

**霍纳.** 来，我知道你没吃：我请客，亲爱的家伙；今天一个子儿汉普郡的钱也用不着你破费。

**平奇怀夫.** 请我的客！好嘛，他这就把我当他的绿帽丈夫支使上了。（旁白。）

**霍纳.** 嗐，不许走。

**平奇怀夫.** 必须走；家里有事。

*（下。）*

===Original===
Harcourt
To beat his wife. He's as jealous of her, as a Cheapside husband of a Covent-garden wife.

Horner
Why, 'tis as hard to find an old whoremaster without jealousy and the gout, as a young one without fear, or the pox:—

As gout in age from pox in youth proceeds,
So wenching past, then jealousy succeeds;
The worst disease that love and wenching breeds.

*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
**哈考特.** 回家打老婆去。他那份醋劲，赛过切普赛德的丈夫守着科文特花园的老婆。

**霍纳.** 唉，要找个不吃醋、不得痛风的老年嫖客，跟找个不知怕、不生疮的年轻嫖客一样难：

老年痛风，打少年疮毒里来，
寻花事毕，醋意接着登台；
情场花柳养出的病，顶数它厉害。

*（同下。）*

## Act II / 第二幕

### Scene I / 第一场

===Original===
A room in **Pinchwife's** house.

***Mrs. Margery Pinchwife** and **Alithea**. **Pinchwife** peeping behind at the door.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
Pray, sister, where are the best fields and woods to walk in, in London?

Alithea
*Aside.* A pretty question!—*Aloud.* Why, sister, Mulberry-garden and St. James's park; and, for close walks, the New Exchange.6

Mrs. Pinchwife
Pray, sister, tell me why my husband looks so grum here in town, and keeps me up so close, and will not let me go a-walking, nor let me wear my best gown yesterday.

Alithea
O, he's jealous, sister.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Jealous! what's that?

Alithea
He's afraid you should love another man.

Mrs. Pinchwife
How should he be afraid of my loving another man, when he will not let me see any but himself?

===Chinese===
平奇怀夫家中一室。

*（**玛格丽·平奇怀夫太太**（Mrs. Margery Pinchwife）与**阿莉西娅**（Alithea）在场；**平奇怀夫**躲在门后偷觑。）*

**平奇怀夫太太.** 好姐姐，伦敦顶好溜达的草地树林在哪儿呀？

**阿莉西娅.**（旁白）好个问题！——（高声）嗐，妹妹，桑树园和圣詹姆斯公园；要想背人的幽径，就数新交易所6了。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 好姐姐，你倒说说，我男人进了城怎么老绷着脸，把我关得这么紧，不放我出去溜达，昨天连我顶好的衣裳也不叫穿？

**阿莉西娅.** 嗐，他吃醋呢，妹妹。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 吃醋！那是怎么讲？

**阿莉西娅.** 他怕你爱上别的男人。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 他不许我见别的男人，只许见他自己，那还怕什么我爱上别的男人呢？

===Original===
Alithea
Did he not carry you yesterday to a play?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Ay; but we sat amongst ugly people. He would not let me come near the gentry, who sat under us, so that I could not see 'em. He told me, none but naughty women sat there, whom they toused and moused. But I would have ventured, for all that.

Alithea
But how did you like the play?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Indeed I was weary of the play; but I liked hugeously the actors. They are the goodliest, properest men, sister!

Alithea
O, but you must not like the actors, sister.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 他昨天不是带你去看戏了么？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 是去了；可我们坐在一堆丑八怪中间。他不许我靠近坐在下层的上等人，我连瞧都瞧不见他们。他说，坐那儿的没一个好女人，尽叫人摸呀捏呀的。可我倒真愿意冒这个险呢。

**阿莉西娅.** 那戏你瞧着怎么样？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 戏么，老实说我瞧腻了；戏子爷们我可喜欢得不得了。他们真是顶体面、顶俊的男人，姐姐！

**阿莉西娅.** 嗐，妹妹，你可不能喜欢戏子。

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
Ay, how should I help it, sister? Pray, sister, when my husband comes in, will you ask leave for me to go a-walking?

Alithea
A-walking! ha! ha! Lord, a country-gentlewoman's pleasure is the drudgery of a footpost; and she requires as much airing as her husband's horses.—*Aside.* But here comes your husband: I'll ask, though I'm sure he'll not grant it.

Mrs. Pinchwife
He says he won't let me go abroad for fear of catching the pox.

Alithea
Fy! the smallpox you should say.

*Enter **Pinchwife**.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
O my dear, dear bud, welcome home! Why dost thou look so fropish? who has nangered thee?

Pinchwife
You're a fool. ***Mrs. Pinchwife** goes aside, and cries.*

Alithea
Faith, so she is, for crying for no fault, poor tender creature!

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 是么，可我怎么忍得住呢，姐姐？求求你，姐姐，等会儿我男人进来，你替我讨个准，放我出去溜达溜达好么？

**阿莉西娅.** 溜达！哈！哈！天哪，乡绅太太的消遣，竟跟步行信差的苦差一样；她也同丈夫的马一样，天天得遛一遛透气。——（旁白）你男人来了：我这就开口，虽然他保险不肯。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 他说不放我出门，是怕我惹上恶疮。

**阿莉西娅.** 啐！你该说「天花」。

*（**平奇怀夫**上。）*

**平奇怀夫太太.** 哎呀，我亲亲的乖乖，你可回来了！怎么苦巴巴绷着脸？谁惹你着恼了？

**平奇怀夫.** 你是个蠢货。（*平奇怀夫太太**走到一旁，哭了。*）

**阿莉西娅.** 说真的，可不就是么——无端端的就哭，可怜这嫩心肠的人儿！

===Original===
Pinchwife
What, you would have her as impudent as yourself, as arrant a jilflirt, a gadder, a magpie; and to say all, a mere notorious town-woman?

Alithea
Brother, you are my only censurer; and the honour of your family will sooner suffer in your wife there than in me, though I take the innocent liberty of the town.

Pinchwife
Hark you, mistress, do not talk so before my wife.—The innocent liberty of the town!

Alithea
Why, pray, who boasts of any intrigue with me? what lampoon has made my name notorious? what ill women frequent my lodgings? I keep no company with any women of scandalous reputations.

Pinchwife
No, you keep the men of scandalous reputations company.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 怎么，你想把她调教得跟你一样不要脸？一样地会卖俏、会串门子、长舌头；一句话，做个响当当的城里婆娘？

**阿莉西娅.** 哥哥，也就你一个人挑我的理；要说有辱你家门风，你那位太太可比我快得多，我不过沾点城里人无伤大雅的自由罢了。

**平奇怀夫.** 听着，小姐，别当着我太太的面说这些。——好一个无伤大雅的城里自由！

**阿莉西娅.** 嗐，请问，谁吹嘘同我有过私情？哪首讽刺诗把我的名字编派得满城风雨？什么不三不四的女人常来我住处？名声坏的女人，我一个也不来往。

**平奇怀夫.** 是么，你同名声坏的男人来往。

===Original===
Alithea
Where? would you not have me civil? answer 'em in a box at the plays, in the drawing-room at Whitehall, in St. James'-park, Mulberry-garden, or—

Pinchwife
Hold, hold! Do not teach my wife where the men are to be found: I believe she's the worse for your town-documents already. I bid you keep her in ignorance, as I do.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Indeed, be not angry with her, bud, she will tell me nothing of the town, though I ask her a thousand times a day.

Pinchwife
Then you are very inquisitive to know, I find?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Not I indeed, dear; I hate London. Our place-house in the country is worth a thousand of't: would I were there again!

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 在哪儿来往？难道要我没规矩不成？在戏园子的包厢里应他们一句话，在白厅的觐见室里，在圣詹姆斯公园、桑树园，或者——

**平奇怀夫.** 打住，打住！别教我太太男人都上哪儿找去：我看她叫你这份城里经熏染得已经不轻了。我叫你把她蒙在鼓里，跟我一样。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 真的呢，乖乖，别冲她发火，她什么都不肯跟我讲城里的事，我一天问上一千遍也不成。

**平奇怀夫.** 这么看，你这人倒真是爱打听的了，嗯？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 我才不呢，亲爱的；我讨厌伦敦。咱们乡下的老宅子抵得上一千个伦敦：真想这会儿就回去！

===Original===
Pinchwife
So you shall, I warrant. But were you not talking of plays and players when I came in?—*To **Alithea**.* You are her encourager in such discourses.

Mrs. Pinchwife
No, indeed, dear; she chid me just now for liking the playermen.

Pinchwife
*Aside.* Nay, if she be so innocent as to own to me her liking them, there is no hurt in't.—*Aloud.* Come, my poor rogue, but thou likest none better than me?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Yes, indeed, but I do. The playermen are finer folks.

Pinchwife
But you love none better than me?

Mrs. Pinchwife
You are my own dear bud, and I know you. I hate a stranger.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 你会回去的，我担保。——（向阿莉西娅）方才我进来之前，你们不是正说戏和戏子么？讲这些话，就是你撺掇她的。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 没有，真的，亲爱的；她刚还骂我来着，怪我喜欢戏子爷们。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）嗐，她天真得当面认账说喜欢他们，可见还不碍事。——（高声）来，我可怜的小东西，你顶喜欢的总还是我罢？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 不，真的，还真不是。戏子爷们是更体面的人呢。

**平奇怀夫.** 可你谁也不爱得超过我，嗯？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 你是我自个儿的亲乖乖，我认得你。我讨厌生人。

===Original===
Pinchwife
Ay, my dear, you must love me only; and not be like the naughty town-women, who only hate their husbands, and love every man else; love plays, visits, fine coaches, fine clothes, fiddles, balls, treats, and so lead a wicked town-life.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Nay, if to enjoy all these things be a town-life, London is not so bad a place, dear.

Pinchwife
How! if you love me, you must hate London.

Alithea
The fool has forbid me discovering to her the pleasures of the town, and he is now setting her agog upon them himself. *Aside.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
But, husband, do the town-women love the playermen too?

Pinchwife
Yes, I warrant you.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Ay, I warrant you.

Pinchwife
Why, you do not, I hope?

Mrs. Pinchwife
No, no, bud. But why have we no playermen in the country?

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 对，亲爱的，你只许爱我一个；别学城里那些坏婆娘——偏恨自个儿的丈夫，偏爱旁的一切男人；爱看戏、爱串门、爱漂亮马车、漂亮衣裳、提琴、舞会、酒席，就这么过一种邪恶的城里日子。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 嗐，要是享用这一切就叫城里日子，那伦敦也不算个坏地方呀，亲爱的。

**平奇怀夫.** 什么！你要是爱我，就得恨伦敦。

**阿莉西娅.**（旁白）这蠢货不许我向她透露城里的乐趣，这会儿倒自己勾起她的馋来了。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 可是，当家的，城里的婆娘们也爱戏子爷们么？

**平奇怀夫.** 爱，我担保。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 嗯，我担保。

**平奇怀夫.** 怎么，你总不会也爱罢，我希望？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 不，不，乖乖。可咱们乡下怎么就没有戏子爷们呢？

===Original===
Pinchwife
Ha!—Mrs. Minx, ask me no more to go to a play.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Nay, why, love? I did not care for going: but when you forbid me, you make me, as 'twere, desire it.

Alithea
So 'twill be in other things, I warrant. *Aside.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
Pray let me go to a play, dear.

Pinchwife
Hold your peace, I wo' not.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Why, love?

Pinchwife
Why, I'll tell you.

Alithea
Nay, if he tell her, she'll give him more cause to forbid her that place. *Aside.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 哈！——小骚蹄子，往后别再求我去看戏了。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 嗐，这是为什么呢，好人儿？我本来也不怎么想去：可你一禁，倒叫我，怎么说呢，偏想要去了。

**阿莉西娅.**（旁白）别的事保管也是这样。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 求你放我去看戏罢，亲爱的。

**平奇怀夫.** 住嘴，我不答应。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 为什么呀，好人儿？

**平奇怀夫.** 我告诉你为什么。

**阿莉西娅.**（旁白）他真要告诉她，她反倒更要给他添堵，叫他把那地方禁得更死。

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
Pray why, dear?

Pinchwife
First, you like the actors; and the gallants may like you.

Mrs. Pinchwife
What, a homely country girl! No, bud, nobody will like me.

Pinchwife
I tell you yes, they may.

Mrs. Pinchwife
No, no, you jest—I won't believe you: I will go.

Pinchwife
I tell you then, that one of the lewdest fellows in town, who saw you there, told me he was in love with you.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Indeed! who, who, pray who was't?

Pinchwife
I've gone too far, and slipped before I was aware; how overjoyed she is! *Aside.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 到底为什么呀，亲爱的？

**平奇怀夫.** 头一件，你喜欢戏子；公子哥儿们呢，也可能喜欢上你。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 什么，我这乡下黄毛丫头！不会的，乖乖，没人会喜欢我。

**平奇怀夫.** 我告诉你，会的，可能。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 不，不，你逗我呢——我不信你的：我就是要去。

**平奇怀夫.** 那我告诉你：城里顶下流的一个浪子，在那儿瞧见了你，跟我说他爱上了你。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 真的么！谁呀，谁呀，快说是谁呀？

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）说漏了，不留神栽了一跤；瞧她乐成这样！

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
Was it any Hampshire gallant, any of our neighbours? I promise you, I am beholden to him.

Pinchwife
I promise you, you lie; for he would but ruin you, as he has done hundreds. He has no other love for women but that; such as he look upon women, like basilisks, but to destroy 'em.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Ay, but if he loves me, why should he ruin me? answer me to that. Methinks he should not, I would do him no harm.

Alithea
Ha! ha! ha!

Pinchwife
'Tis very well; but I'll keep him from doing you any harm, or me either. But here comes company; get you in, get you in.

Mrs. Pinchwife
But, pray, husband, is he a pretty gentleman that loves me?

Pinchwife
In, baggage, in. *Thrusts her in, and shuts the door.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 是汉普郡的公子不是，咱们的街坊？说真的，我倒要谢谢他呢。

**平奇怀夫.** 我替你担保，你撒谎；他只想毁掉你，就像他毁掉成百个女人一样。他对女人没有第二种爱；他这号人看女人，就像蛇怪看猎物，只为毁灭她们。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 嗯，可他既然爱我，为什么要毁我呢？你答答这句。要叫我说呀，他不会的，我又不害他。

**阿莉西娅.** 哈！哈！哈！

**平奇怀夫.** 好得很；可我会拦住他，不叫他害你，也不叫他害我。来客了；进去，进去。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 可是，当家的，那个爱我的，是个俊俏的公子爷么？

**平奇怀夫.** 滚进去，贱货，进去。（把她推进门去，关上门。）

===Original===
*Enter **Sparkish** and **Harcourt**.*

Pinchwife
What, all the lewd libertines of the town brought to my lodging by this easy coxcomb! 'sdeath, I'll not suffer it.

Sparkish
Here, Harcourt, do you approve my choice?—*To **Alithea**.* Dear little rogue, I told you I'd bring you acquainted with all my friends, the wits and—***Harcourt** salutes her.*

Pinchwife
Ay, they shall know her, as well as you yourself will, I warrant you.

Sparkish
This is one of those, my pretty rogue, that are to dance at your wedding tomorrow; and him you must bid welcome ever, to what you and I have.

Pinchwife
Monstrous! *Aside.*

===Chinese===
*（**斯帕基什**与**哈考特**上。）*

**平奇怀夫.** 什么，城里荒唐的浪子们，全叫这个软耳朵的绣花枕头领到我家来了！该死，我受不了。

**斯帕基什.** 喂，哈考特，我挑的人你可中意？——（向阿莉西娅）亲爱的小坏蛋，我说过要叫你认全我所有的朋友，都是些才子——（*哈考特**上前向她行吻手礼。*）

**平奇怀夫.** 是啊，他们会「认得」她的，保管认得跟你自个儿一样透。

**斯帕基什.** 我的小坏蛋，这位就是明天要在你婚礼上跳舞的；往后我跟你有什么，你都得永远欢迎他来一份。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）无法无天！

===Original===
Sparkish
Harcourt, how dost thou like her, faith? Nay, dear, do not look down; I should hate to have a wife of mine out of countenance at anything.

Pinchwife
Wonderful! *Aside.*

Sparkish
Tell me, I say, Harcourt, how dost thou like her? Thou hast stared upon her enough, to resolve me.

Harcourt
So infinitely well, that I could wish I had a mistress too, that might differ from her in nothing but her love and engagement to you.

Alithea
Sir, Master Sparkish has often told me that his acquaintance were all wits and raillieurs, and now I find it.

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 哈考特，说真的，你看她怎么样？别低头呀，亲爱的；我最恨我媳妇不管为什么事害臊脸红。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）妙极了！

**斯帕基什.** 你倒是说呀，哈考特，你看她怎么样？你把她瞧了个够，总该给我个回话了。

**哈考特.** 好得没法说了；只盼我自己也有这么一位情人，处处同她一样，只除了她对你那份情意和婚约。

**阿莉西娅.** 先生，斯帕基什先生常跟我说，他相熟的全是才子、俏皮人，如今一看果然。

===Original===
Sparkish
No, by the universe, madam, he does not rally now; you may believe him. I do assure you, he is the honestest, worthiest, truehearted gentlemen—a man of such perfect honour, he would say nothing to a lady he does not mean.

Pinchwife
Praising another man to his mistress! *Aside.*

Harcourt
Sir, you are so beyond expectation obliging, that—

Sparkish
Nay, egad, I am sure you do admire her extremely; I see't in your eyes.—He does admire you, madam.—By the world, don't you?

Harcourt
Yes, above the world, or the most glorious part of it, her whole sex: and till now I never thought I should have envied you, or any man about to marry, but you have the best excuse for marriage I ever knew.

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 不，凭宇宙起誓，夫人，他这会儿可不是打趣；你尽管信他。我向你担保，他是顶老实、顶可贵、顶真心的一位君子——荣誉心这样十全十美的人，对太太小姐绝不说半句不打算兑现的话。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）当着情人的面夸旁的男人！

**哈考特.** 先生，您殷勤得出人意料，以致——

**斯帕基什.** 得了，说真的，我保险你把她佩服得五体投地；你眼睛里写着呢。——他真佩服你，夫人。——凭全世界起誓，是不是？

**哈考特.** 是，高过全世界，也高过世上顶光彩的那一部分——她们整个女性：直到方才，我从没想过会眼红你，或是眼红任何要结婚的男人；可你这份结婚的理由，是我平生所知顶体面的。

===Original===
Alithea
Nay, now, sir, I'm satisfied you are of the society of the wits and raillieurs, since you cannot spare your friend, even when he is but too civil to you; but the surest sign is, since you are an enemy to marriage—for that I hear you hate as much as business or bad wine.

Harcourt
Truly, madam, I was never an enemy to marriage till now, because marriage was never an enemy to me before.

Alithea
But why, sir, is marriage an enemy to you now? because it robs you of your friend here? for you look upon a friend married, as one gone into a monastery, that is, dead to the world.

Harcourt
'Tis indeed, because you marry him; I see, madam, you can guess my meaning. I do confess heartily and openly, I wish it were in my power to break the match; by Heavens I would.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 得了，先生，如今我算信了，你确实是才子和俏皮人一伙的——朋友对你这么客气，你还饶不了他；可最准的凭据还是，你是婚姻的仇人——听说你恨婚姻，恨得跟恨公务、恨酸酒一个样。

**哈考特.** 说真的，夫人，我从前的确不是婚姻的仇人——因为从前婚姻也从没害过我。

**阿莉西娅.** 可如今婚姻怎么又害起你来了呢，先生？是因为它夺走了你这位朋友么？你们把结了婚的朋友，看作进了修道院的人——就是说，对尘世死了心的人。

**哈考特.** 正是因为你要嫁的是他；夫人，我看得出，你猜到我的意思了。我打心底里、敞开了认账：但愿我有法子拆了这门亲；皇天在上，我一定拆。

===Original===
Sparkish
Poor Frank!

Alithea
Would you be so unkind to me?

Harcourt
No, no, 'tis not because I would be unkind to you.

Sparkish
Poor Frank! no gad, 'tis only his kindness to me.

Pinchwife
Great kindness to you indeed! Insensible fop, let a man make love to his wife to his face! *Aside.*

Sparkish
Come, dear Frank, for all my wife there, that shall be, thou shalt enjoy me sometimes, dear rogue. By my honour, we men of wit condole for our deceased brother in marriage, as much as for one dead in earnest: I think that was prettily said of me, ha, Harcourt?—But come, Frank, be not melancholy for me.

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 可怜的弗兰克！

**阿莉西娅.** 你就忍心这么待我么？

**哈考特.** 不，不，我可不是要亏待你。

**斯帕基什.** 可怜的弗兰克！不，真的，他只是疼我。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）疼你，可真疼到家了！没知觉的草包，由着人家当面追求他老婆！

**斯帕基什.** 来，亲爱的弗兰克，别管那边我那未过门的媳妇，你照样可以时不时享用享用我，亲爱的家伙。凭我的荣誉起誓，我们才子们哀悼一位「阵亡」于婚姻的兄弟，同哀悼真死了一个兄弟一样伤心：我看这句我说得够俏皮了，嗯，哈考特？——来罢，弗兰克，别为我伤心。

===Original===
Harcourt
No, I assure you, I am not melancholy for you.

Sparkish
Prithee, Frank, dost think my wife that shall be there, a fine person?

Harcourt
I could gaze upon her till I became as blind as you are.

Sparkish
How as I am? how?

Harcourt
Because you are a lover, and true lovers are blind, stock blind.

Sparkish
True, true; but by the world she has wit too, as well as beauty: go, go with her into a corner, and try if she has wit; talk to her anything, she's bashful before me.

Harcourt
Indeed if a woman wants wit in a corner, she has it nowhere.

===Chinese===
**哈考特.** 放心，我向你担保，我不是为你伤心。

**斯帕基什.** 说真的，弗兰克，你看我那位未过门的媳妇，人品标致么？

**哈考特.** 我能一直盯着她瞧，一直瞧到变得跟你一样瞎。

**斯帕基什.** 我怎么就瞎了？怎么讲？

**哈考特.** 因为你是个恋人，真正的恋人都是瞎子，瞎得瓷实。

**斯帕基什.** 对，对；不过凭全世界起誓，她不光有貌，还有脑子呢：去，同她到角落里去，试试她有没有脑子；随你同她说什么，她当着我害臊。

**哈考特.** 说的是，女人要是在角落里没脑子，那她走到哪儿也没有。

===Original===
Alithea
Sir, you dispose of me a little before your time—*Aside to **Sparkish**.*

Sparkish
Nay, nay, madam, let me have an earnest of your obedience, or—go, go, madam—***Harcourt** courts **Alithea** aside.*

Pinchwife
How, sir! if you are not concerned for the honour of a wife, I am for that of a sister; he shall not debauch her. Be a pander to your own wife! bring men to her! let 'em make love before your face! thrust 'em into a corner together, then leave 'em in private! is this your town wit and conduct?

Sparkish
Ha! ha! ha! a silly wise rogue would make one laugh more than a stark fool, ha! ha! I shall burst. Nay, you shall not disturb 'em; I'll vex thee, by the world. *Struggles with **Pinchwife** to keep him from **Harcourt** and **Alithea**.*

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 先生，你可有点提前把我处置了——（向斯帕基什旁白。）

**斯帕基什.** 别呀，别呀，夫人，先让我预支一点你的听话做定钱，要不——去，去罢，夫人——（*哈考特**在一旁向阿莉西娅求爱。*）

**平奇怀夫.** 什么，先生！你不在乎做妻子的名节，我可在乎做妹妹的名节；不许他勾坏她。给自个儿的老婆当皮条客！把男人往她跟前领！由他们当面谈情！把两个塞进一个角落，再撇下他们私处！这就是你城里人的才智和章法？

**斯帕基什.** 哈！哈！哈！装聪明的傻蛋，比十足的蠢货更逗人笑，哈！哈！我要笑破肚皮了。偏不，偏不叫你搅了他们；气死你，凭全世界起誓。（拦住平奇怀夫，不让他过去搅哈考特和阿莉西娅。）

===Original===
Alithea
The writings are drawn, sir, settlements made; 'tis too late, sir, and past all revocation.

Harcourt
Then so is my death.

Alithea
I would not be unjust to him.

Harcourt
Then why to me so?

Alithea
I have no obligation to you.

Harcourt
My love.

Alithea
I had his before.

Harcourt
You never had it; he wants, you see, jealousy, the only infallible sign of it.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 先生，婚书已经写就，财产已安排停当；太迟了，先生，覆水难收。

**哈考特.** 那我的死期也定了。

**阿莉西娅.** 我不愿对他不义。

**哈考特.** 那为何又这样对我？

**阿莉西娅.** 我对你并无亏欠。

**哈考特.** 我的爱情就是。

**阿莉西娅.** 他的爱我先前就有了。

**哈考特.** 你从没有过他的爱；你瞧，他就缺醋劲——那是爱情唯一不会走眼的凭据。

===Original===
Alithea
Love proceeds from esteem; he cannot distrust my virtue: besides, he loves me, or he would not marry me.

Harcourt
Marrying you is no more sign of his love than bribing your woman, that he may marry you, is a sign of his generosity. Marriage is rather a sign of interest than love; and he that marries a fortune covets a mistress, not loves her. But if you take marriage for a sign of love, take it from me immediately.

Alithea
No, now you have put a scruple in my head; but in short, sir, to end our dispute, I must marry him, my reputation would suffer in the world else.

Harcourt
No; if you do marry him, with your pardon, madam, your reputation suffers in the world, and you would be thought in necessity for a cloak.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 爱情生于敬重；他不疑我的贞洁：再说，他爱我，不然不会娶我。

**哈考特.** 娶你，算不得他爱情的凭据，正如他为了娶你而买通你的贴身女仆，算不得他大方的凭据。婚姻毋宁说是利害的凭据，不是爱情的；娶一份家财的人，是贪一位主妇，不是爱她。不过你要是把婚姻当爱情的凭据，那就趁早收下我的。

**阿莉西娅.** 不成了，你这会儿叫我心里起了疑影；可长话短说，先生，为结束咱们的争执：我非嫁他不可，不然我的名声要在世上受损。

**哈考特.** 恰恰相反；你要是真嫁了他——恕我直言，夫人——你的名声才要在世上受损，人家要当你急着找件遮羞的外衣。

===Original===
Alithea
Nay, now you are rude, sir.—Mr. Sparkish, pray come hither, your friend here is very troublesome, and very loving.

Harcourt
Hold! hold!—*Aside to **Alithea**.*

Pinchwife
D'ye hear that?

Sparkish
Why, d'ye think I'll seem to be jealous, like a country bumpkin?

Pinchwife
No, rather be a cuckold, like a credulous cit.

Harcourt
Madam, you would not have been so little generous as to have told him.

Alithea
Yes, since you could be so little generous as to wrong him.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 嗐，你这可就无礼了，先生。——斯帕基什先生，请你过来，你这位朋友好缠人，还满嘴情话。

**哈考特.** 别喊！别喊！——（向阿莉西娅旁白。）

**平奇怀夫.** 听见没有？

**斯帕基什.** 怎么，你当我肯摆出一副吃醋的架势，像乡巴佬似的？

**平奇怀夫.** 对，不如当个戴绿帽的，像轻信的城里市侩。

**哈考特.** 夫人，您总不至于那么小气，去告诉他罢。

**阿莉西娅.** 我就要告诉他，谁叫你那么小气，去糟践他。

===Original===
Harcourt
Wrong him! no man can do't, he's beneath an injury: a bubble, a coward, a senseless idiot, a wretch so contemptible to all the world but you, that—

Alithea
Hold, do not rail at him, for since he is like to be my husband, I am resolved to like him: nay, I think I am obliged to tell him you are not his friend.—Master Sparkish, Master Sparkish!

Sparkish
What, what?—*To **Harcourt**.* Now, dear rogue, has not she wit?

Harcourt
Not so much as I thought, and hoped she had. *Speaks surlily.*

Alithea
Mr. Sparkish, do you bring people to rail at you?

Harcourt
Madam—

Sparkish
How! no; but if he does rail at me, 'tis but in jest, I warrant: what we wits do for one another, and never take any notice of it.

===Chinese===
**哈考特.** 糟践他！没人糟践得了他，他还配不上受这个委屈呢：一个冤大头、懦夫、没知觉的白痴，除你之外全世界都瞧不上的一路货，以致——

**阿莉西娅.** 住口，不许骂他，他既要做我的丈夫，我打定主意要喜欢他：不但如此，我想我有义务告诉他，你不是他的朋友。——斯帕基什先生！斯帕基什先生！

**斯帕基什.** 什么，什么？——（向哈考特）怎么样，亲爱的家伙，她是不是有脑子？

**哈考特.** 不如我指望、我祈望的那样多。（说得悻悻然。）

**阿莉西娅.** 斯帕基什先生，你专领人来骂你的么？

**哈考特.** 夫人——

**斯帕基什.** 怎么！不是；他就算骂我，也不过是逗趣，我担保：这是我们才子彼此的情分，从不往心里去。

===Original===
Alithea
He spoke so scurrilously of you, I had no patience to hear him; besides, he has been making love to me.

Harcourt
True, damned telltale woman! *Aside.*

Sparkish
Pshaw! to show his parts—we wits rail and make love often, but to show our parts: as we have no affections, so we have no malice, we—

Alithea
He said you were a wretch below an injury—

Sparkish
Pshaw!

Harcourt
Damned, senseless, impudent, virtuous jade! Well, since she won't let me have her, she'll do as good, she'll make me hate her. *Aside.*

Alithea
A common bubble—

Sparkish
Pshaw!

Alithea
A coward—

Sparkish
Pshaw, pshaw!

Alithea
A senseless, drivelling idiot—

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 他把你骂得那样下作，我听得都没了耐性；再说，他一直在向我求爱。

**哈考特.**（旁白）好哇，该死的长舌妇！

**斯帕基什.** 啐！那是显本事——我们才子骂人、求爱是家常便饭，都为显显本事：我们既没有真心，也就没有恶意，我们——

**阿莉西娅.** 他说你是连委屈都够不上的可怜虫——

**斯帕基什.** 啐！

**哈考特.**（旁白）该死的、没知觉的、不要脸的、贞洁的婆娘！好，她既不肯归我，就来个更狠的：叫我把她恨上。

**阿莉西娅.** 一个不折不扣的冤大头——

**斯帕基什.** 啐！

**阿莉西娅.** 一个懦夫——

**斯帕基什.** 啐！啐！

**阿莉西娅.** 一个没知觉、流口水的白痴——

===Original===
Sparkish
How! did he disparage my parts? Nay, then, my honour's concerned, I can't put up that, sir, by the world—brother, help me to kill him—*Aside.* I may draw now, since we have the odds of him:—'tis a good occasion, too, before my mistress—*Offers to draw.*

Alithea
Hold, hold!

Sparkish
What, what?

Alithea
*Aside.* I must not let 'em kill the gentleman neither, for his kindness to me: I am so far from hating him, that I wish my gallant had his person and understanding. Nay, if my honour—

Sparkish
I'll be thy death.

Alithea
Hold, hold! Indeed, to tell the truth, the gentleman said after all, that what he spoke was but out of friendship to you.

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 什么！他小瞧我的才分？那可关系我的荣誉了，这个我咽不下，先生，凭全世界起誓——内兄，帮我宰了他——（旁白）现在可以拔剑了，我们人多势众：——又是在我情人跟前，好时机——（作势拔剑。）

**阿莉西娅.** 住手，住手！

**斯帕基什.** 怎么，怎么？

**阿莉西娅.**（旁白）可我也不能由他们杀了这位先生，他待我这么殷勤：我非但不恨他，倒巴望我的未婚夫有他这份人品和头脑。只是，我的名节——

**斯帕基什.** 我跟你拼了。

**阿莉西娅.** 住手，住手！说真的，实话说罢，这位先生到末了说了，他那些话全是出于对你的交情。

===Original===
Sparkish
How! say, I am, I am a fool, that is, no wit, out of friendship to me?

Alithea
Yes, to try whether I was concerned enough for you; and made love to me only to be satisfied of my virtue, for your sake.

Harcourt
Kind, however. *Aside.*

Sparkish
Nay, if it were so, my dear rogue, I ask thee pardon; but why would not you tell me so, faith?

Harcourt
Because I did not think on't, faith.

Sparkish
Come, Horner does not come; Harcourt, let's be gone to the new play.—Come, madam.

Alithea
I will not go, if you intend to leave me alone in the box, and run into the pit, as you use to do.

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 什么！说我是个、是个蠢货——就是说，没有才分——是出于对我的交情？

**阿莉西娅.** 是呀，为试试我对你够不够上心；他向我求爱，也只为验一验我的贞洁，是为你。

**哈考特.**（旁白）不管怎么说，好心。

**斯帕基什.** 嗐，要是这样，亲爱的家伙，我请你原谅；可你怎么不早这么跟我说呢，啊？

**哈考特.** 因为我没想到这上头，真的。

**斯帕基什.** 得了，霍纳不来；哈考特，咱们上新戏去罢。——来，夫人。

**阿莉西娅.** 我不去，要是你打算把我一个人撂在包厢里，自己钻到池座去，像你往常那样。

===Original===
Sparkish
Pshaw! I'll leave Harcourt with you in the box to entertain you, and that's as good; if I sat in the box, I should be thought no judge but of trimmings.—Come away, Harcourt, lead her down.

*Exeunt **Sparkish, Harcourt**, and **Alithea**.*

Pinchwife
Well, go thy ways, for the flower of the true town fops, such as spend their estates before they come to 'em, and are cuckolds before they're married. But let me go look to my own freehold.—How!

*Enter **Lady Fidget**, **Mrs. Dainty Fidget**, and **Mrs. Squeamish**.*

Lady Fidget
Your servant, sir: where is your lady? We are come to wait upon her to the new play.

Pinchwife
New play!

Lady Fidget
And my husband will wait upon you presently.

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 啐！我把哈考特留在包厢里陪你说话，一样的好；我要是坐在包厢里，人家要当我只会鉴赏花边绦子了。——走罢，哈考特，搀她下楼。

*（斯帕基什、哈考特、阿莉西娅同下。）*

**平奇怀夫.** 好啊，你真算得城里纨绔里的一朵鲜花——家产还没到手就先花个精光，人还没结婚就先当上了绿帽汉。可我得去照看自家那份祖业了。——咦！

*（**菲吉特夫人**、**丹蒂·菲吉特小姐**与**斯奎米什太太**（Mrs. Squeamish）上。）*

**菲吉特夫人.** 先生，您安：尊夫人呢？我们来陪她去看新戏。

**平奇怀夫.** 新戏！

**菲吉特夫人.** 我丈夫过一会儿就来拜望您。

===Original===
Pinchwife
*Aside.* Damn your civility.—*Aloud.* Madam, by no means; I will not see Sir Jasper here, till I have waited upon him at home; nor shall my wife see you till she has waited upon your ladyship at your lodgings.

Lady Fidget
Now we are here, sir?

Pinchwife
No, Madam.

Mrs. Dainty
Pray, let us see her.

Mrs. Squeamish
We will not stir till we see her.

Pinchwife
*Aside.* A pox on you all!—*Goes to the door, and returns.* She has locked the door, and is gone abroad.

Lady Fidget
No, you have locked the door, and she's within.

Mrs. Dainty
They told us below she was here.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）去你们的客气。——（高声）夫人，使不得；我还没登门拜望，不能在这儿见贾斯珀爵士；我太太也一样，没上府上拜见过夫人之前，不能见您。

**菲吉特夫人.** 那我们人都到了，先生？

**平奇怀夫.** 也不行，夫人。

**丹蒂小姐.** 求您了，让我们见见她罢。

**斯奎米什太太.** 不见到她我们不走。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）叫你们一齐遭瘟！（走到门边，又折回。）她把门反锁了，出门去了。

**菲吉特夫人.** 不对，是你锁的门，人就在里头。

**丹蒂小姐.** 楼下的人说她就在这儿。

===Original===
Pinchwife
*Aside.* Will nothing do?—*Aloud.* Well, it must out then. To tell you the truth, ladies, which I was afraid to let you know before, lest it might endanger your lives, my wife has just now the smallpox come out upon her; do not be frightened; but pray be gone, ladies; you shall not stay here in danger of your lives; pray get you gone, ladies.

Lady Fidget
No, no, we have all had 'em.

Mrs. Squeamish
Alack, alack!

Mrs. Dainty
Come, come, we must see how it goes with her; I understand the disease.

Lady Fidget
Come!

Pinchwife
*Aside.* Well, there is no being too hard for women at their own weapon, lying, therefore I'll quit the field.

*Exit.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）这也不行那也不行？——（高声）好罢，只好抖出来了。跟诸位说句实话——先前不敢叫你们知道，怕危及性命：我太太这会儿天花刚发出来；别害怕；可千万请回避，夫人们；不能叫你们留在这儿冒性命之险；请回罢，夫人们。

**菲吉特夫人.** 不怕，不怕，我们都出过天花了。

**斯奎米什太太.** 哎呀，哎呀！

**丹蒂小姐.** 来，来，总得瞧瞧她的病势怎样；这种病我懂。

**菲吉特夫人.** 走！

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）罢了，跟女人斗她们的本行武器——撒谎——是斗不过的，我还是撤出阵地罢。

*（下。）*

===Original===
Mrs. Squeamish
Here's an example of jealousy!

Lady Fidget
Indeed, as the world goes, I wonder there are no more jealous, since wives are so neglected.

Mrs. Dainty
Pshaw! as the world goes, to what end should they be jealous?

Lady Fidget
Foh! 'tis a nasty world.

Mrs. Squeamish
That men of parts, great acquaintance, and quality, should take up with and spend themselves and fortunes in keeping little playhouse creatures, foh!

Lady Fidget
Nay, that women of understanding, great acquaintance, and good quality, should fall a-keeping too of little creatures, foh!

===Chinese===
**斯奎米什太太.** 瞧这吃醋的活榜样！

**菲吉特夫人.** 说真的，照如今这世道，丈夫们这样冷落妻子，我倒奇怪吃醋的怎么不多几个。

**丹蒂小姐.** 啐！照如今这世道，吃醋又图个什么呢？

**菲吉特夫人.** 呸！这世道真腌臜。

**斯奎米什太太.** 有才分、有门路、有身份的爷们，竟甘心把自个儿和家产都赔进去，包养那些戏园子里的小玩物，呸！

**菲吉特夫人.** 可不是，有见识、有门路、有身份的太太们，竟也倒贴着包养起小玩物来，呸！

===Original===
Mrs. Squeamish
Why, 'tis the men of quality's fault; they never visit women of honour and reputation as they used to do; and have not so much as common civility for ladies of our rank, but use us with the same indifferency and ill-breeding as if we were all married to 'em.

Lady Fidget
She says true; 'tis an arrant shame women of quality should be so slighted; methinks birth—birth should go for something; I have known men admired, courted, and followed for their titles only.

Mrs. Squeamish
Ay, one would think men of honour should not love, no more than marry, out of their own rank.

Mrs. Dainty
Fy, fy, upon 'em! they are come to think cross breeding for themselves best, as well as for their dogs and horses.

Lady Fidget
They are dogs and horses for't.

===Chinese===
**斯奎米什太太.** 嗐，这都怪有身份的爷们；他们再不像从前那样登门看望有廉耻、有名声的太太们；对我们这等级的小姐连寻常的客气都没有，拿我们不当回事、没上没下，倒像我们全都嫁了他们似的。

**菲吉特夫人.** 她说得对；有身份的太太们受这般冷落，真是奇耻；要叫我说，出身——出身总该算点数；我就见过有的男人，单凭头衔就叫人仰慕、追求、捧着。

**斯奎米什太太.** 是呀，照理讲，讲体面的爷们不该越出本阶层去爱，正如不该越出本阶层去娶。

**丹蒂小姐.** 呸，呸，瞧他们！他们如今倒认为自个儿也来「杂交」最好，跟他们的狗和马一样。

**菲吉特夫人.** 他们那样才真是狗和马呢。

===Original===
Mrs. Squeamish
One would think, if not for love, for vanity a little.

Mrs. Dainty
Nay, they do satisfy their vanity upon us sometimes; and are kind to us in their report, tell all the world they lie with us.

Lady Fidget
Damned rascals, that we should be only wronged by 'em! To report a man has had a person, when he has not had a person, is the greatest wrong in the whole world that can be done to a person.

Mrs. Squeamish
Well, 'tis an arrant shame noble persons should be so wronged and neglected.

Lady Fidget
But still 'tis an arranter shame for a noble person to neglect her own honour, and defame her own noble person with little inconsiderable fellows, foh!

===Chinese===
**斯奎米什太太.** 照理说，就算不为爱情，总该为点虚荣罢。

**丹蒂小姐.** 可不，他们有时倒真在我们身上满足虚荣；还在报告里对我们行好——逢人便说同我们睡过。

**菲吉特夫人.** 该死的恶棍，害我们只吃亏在这种事上！人家明明没沾过这个人，偏说沾过——天底下对一个人所能下的最大的毒手，莫过于此。

**斯奎米什太太.** 可不是，尊贵的人儿遭这样的冤枉和冷落，真是奇耻大辱。

**菲吉特夫人.** 可尊贵的人儿自贬身价，糟蹋自个儿尊贵的身子，去搭那些不上台面的小猢狲，这才是更大的奇耻大辱，呸！

===Original===
Mrs. Dainty
I suppose the crime against our honour is the same with a man of quality as with another.

Lady Fidget
How! no sure, the man of quality is likest one's husband, and therefore the fault should be the less.

Mrs. Dainty
But then the pleasure should be the less.

Lady Fidget
Fy, fy, fy, for shame, sister! whither shall we ramble? Be continent in your discourse, or I shall hate you.

Mrs. Dainty
Besides, an intrigue is so much the more notorious for the man's quality.

Mrs. Squeamish
'Tis true that nobody takes notice of a private man, and therefore with him 'tis more secret; and the crime's the less when 'tis not known.

===Chinese===
**丹蒂小姐.** 我琢磨着，冒犯我们名节的罪过，跟有身份的男人相干，和跟别人相干，都是一回事。

**菲吉特夫人.** 什么！可不是一回事；有身份的男人最像自个儿的丈夫，所以罪过理当轻些。

**丹蒂小姐.** 那乐趣可也就该少些了。

**菲吉特夫人.** 呸，呸，呸，不知羞，妹妹！咱们这都扯到哪儿去了？说话收着点，不然我要恨你了。

**丹蒂小姐.** 再说，男人的身份越高，私情就越瞒不住。

**斯奎米什太太.** 说的也是，没人在意无名小卒，同他来往就隐秘些；丑事不传开，罪过也就轻些。

===Original===
Lady Fidget
You say true; i'faith, I think you are in the right on't: 'tis not an injury to a husband, till it be an injury to our honours; so that a woman of honour loses no honour with a private person; and to say truth—

Mrs. Dainty
So, the little fellow is grown a private person—with her—*Apart to **Mrs. Squeamish**.*

Lady Fidget
But still my dear, dear honour—

*Enter **Sir Jasper Fidget**, **Horner**, and **Dorilant**.*

Sir Jasper
Ay, my dear, dear of honour, thou hast still so much honour in thy mouth—

Horner
That she has none elsewhere. *Aside.*

Lady Fidget
Oh, what d'ye mean to bring in these upon us?

===Chinese===
**菲吉特夫人.** 你说得对；说真的，我想你这话在理：还没伤到我们的名节，就算不得伤害丈夫；所以同无名小卒来往，有身份的太太不失身份；说实在的——

**丹蒂小姐.** 瞧，那位小个子爷们，到她嘴里成「无名小卒」了——（向斯奎米什太太旁白。）

**菲吉特夫人.** 可我还有我亲亲的名节呢——

*（**贾斯珀·菲吉特爵士**、**霍纳**与**多里兰特**上。）*

**贾斯珀爵士.** 是啊，我的名节亲亲，你嘴里总是挂着那么多名节——

**霍纳.**（旁白）旁的地方可就一点儿没有了。

**菲吉特夫人.** 哎呀，你们把这号人带到我们跟前来干什么？

===Original===
Mrs. Dainty
Foh! these are as bad as wits.

Mrs. Squeamish
Foh!

Lady Fidget
Let us leave the room.

Sir Jasper
Stay, stay; faith, to tell you the naked truth—

Lady Fidget
Fy, Sir Jasper! do not use that word naked.

Sir Jasper
Well, well, in short I have business at Whitehall, and cannot go to the play with you, therefore would have you go—

Lady Fidget
With those two to a play?

Sir Jasper
No, not with t'other, but with Mr. Horner; there can be no more scandal to go with him than with Mr. Tattle, or Master Limberham.

Lady Fidget
With that nasty fellow! no—no.

===Chinese===
**丹蒂小姐.** 呸！这路人跟才子们一样讨厌。

**斯奎米什太太.** 呸！

**菲吉特夫人.** 咱们换间屋子去。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 别走，别走；说真的，跟你们说句赤裸裸的实话——

**菲吉特夫人.** 呸，贾斯珀爵士！不许用「赤裸裸」这个字眼。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 好罢，好罢，长话短说：我白厅有公务，不能陪你们看戏去，所以想请你们去——

**菲吉特夫人.** 同那两位去看戏？

**贾斯珀爵士.** 不，不带另一位，就带霍纳先生；同他出门，不会比同塔特尔先生或林博汉姆少爷出门更招闲话。

**菲吉特夫人.** 同那个腌臜家伙！不——不。

===Original===
Sir Jasper
Nay, prithee, dear, hear me. *Whispers to **Lady Fidget**.*

Horner
Ladies—***Horner** and **Dorilant** draw near **Mrs. Squeamish** and **Mrs. Dainty Fidget**.*

Mrs. Dainty
Stand off.

Mrs. Squeamish
Do not approach us.

Mrs. Dainty
You herd with the wits, you are obscenity all over.

Mrs. Squeamish
And I would as soon look upon a picture of Adam and Eve, without fig-leaves, as any of you, if I could help it; therefore keep off, and do not make us sick.

Dorilant
What a devil are these?

Horner
Why, these are pretenders to honour, as critics to wit, only by censuring others; and as every raw, peevish, out-of-humoured, affected, dull, tea-drinking, arithmetical fop, sets up for a wit by railing at men of sense, so these for honour, by railing at the court, and ladies of as great honour as quality.

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 嗐，听我说，亲爱的。（向菲吉特夫人耳语。）

**霍纳.** 太太们——（*霍纳**与**多里兰特**凑近斯奎米什太太和丹蒂·菲吉特小姐。*）

**丹蒂小姐.** 站开些。

**斯奎米什太太.** 不许靠近我们。

**丹蒂小姐.** 你们同才子们混在一处，浑身上下都是淫词秽语。

**斯奎米什太太.** 要是能由我做主，我情愿看亚当夏娃不带无花果叶的画儿，也不情愿看你们二位；所以滚开些，别叫我们恶心。

**多里兰特.** 这两位是什么路数，见鬼？

**霍纳.** 嗐，这类人是名节的冒牌货，好比批评家是才情的冒牌货，全靠贬斥旁人充数；正如每个毛嫩、乖戾、闹脾气、装腔、乏味、喝茶的、打算盘的花花公子，靠骂明白人来冒充才子；她们也就靠骂宫廷、骂那些名节身份一样尊贵的太太们，来冒充名节。

===Original===
Sir Jasper
Come, Mr. Horner, I must desire you to go with these ladies to the play, sir.

Horner
I, sir?

Sir Jasper
Ay, ay, come, sir.

Horner
I must beg your pardon, sir, and theirs; I will not be seen in women's company in public again for the world.

Sir Jasper
Ha, ha, strange aversion!

Mrs. Squeamish
No, he's for women's company in private.

Sir Jasper
He—poor man—he—ha! ha! ha!

Mrs. Dainty
'Tis a greater shame amongst lewd fellows to be seen in virtuous women's company, than for the women to be seen with them.

Horner
Indeed, madam, the time was I only hated virtuous women, but now I hate the other too; I beg your pardon, ladies.

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 来罢，霍纳先生，我得劳驾您陪这几位太太去看戏，先生。

**霍纳.** 我，先生？

**贾斯珀爵士.** 是，是，来罢，先生。

**霍纳.** 我只能请您原谅，先生，也请她们原谅；打死我也不能再当众同女人们作伴了。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 哈，哈，好怪的毛病！

**斯奎米什太太.** 可不是怪，他喜欢私底下同女人们作伴。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 他呀——可怜人——他呀——哈！哈！哈！

**丹蒂小姐.** 在浪子们中间，叫人瞧见同贞洁的太太们作伴，比太太们叫人瞧见同浪子们作伴，更丢脸呢。

**霍纳.** 实话奉告，夫人，从前我只恨贞洁的太太，如今另一路我也恨上了；请诸位原谅，夫人们。

===Original===
Lady Fidget
You are very obliging, sir, because we would not be troubled with you.

Sir Jasper
In sober sadness, he shall go.

Dorilant
Nay, if he wo' not, I am ready to wait upon the ladies, and I think I am the fitter man.

Sir Jasper
You sir! no, I thank you for that. Master Horner is a privileged man amongst the virtuous ladies, 'twill be a great while before you are so; he! he! he! he's my wife's gallant; he! he! he! No, pray withdraw, sir, for as I take it, the virtuous ladies have no business with you.

Dorilant
And I am sure he can have none with them. 'Tis strange a man can't come amongst virtuous women now, but upon the same terms as men are admitted into the Great Turk's seraglio. But heavens keep me from being an ombre player with 'em!—But where is Pinchwife?

*Exit.*

===Chinese===
**菲吉特夫人.** 您真是「会做人」，先生，就为我们嫌您麻烦。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 一本正经地说，他去定了。

**多里兰特.** 嗐，他要是不肯，我随时乐意伺候太太们，何况我看我更够格。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 您哪，先生！不，这个可谢了。霍纳先生在贞洁太太们中间是有特权的，您要想熬到那一步可有得等呢；嘿！嘿！嘿！他是我太太的「骑士」；嘿！嘿！嘿！不，请您退开，先生，因为依我看，贞洁的太太们跟您没有业务往来。

**多里兰特.** 我也保险他同她们没有。如今一个男人想挨近贞洁的太太们，竟得按土耳其大皇帝后宫的规矩进门，真是咄咄怪事。老天保佑我别陪她们打翁布雷牌（ombre）！——咦，平奇怀夫呢？

*（下。）*

===Original===
Sir Jasper
Come, come, man; what, avoid the sweet society of womankind? that sweet, soft, gentle, tame, noble creature, woman, made for man's companion—

Horner
So is that soft, gentle, tame, and more noble creature a spaniel, and has all their tricks; can fawn, lie down, suffer beating, and fawn the more; barks at your friends when they come to see you, makes your bed hard, gives you fleas, and the mange sometimes. And all the difference is, the spaniel's the more faithful animal, and fawns but upon one master.

Sir Jasper
He! he! he!

Mrs. Squeamish
O the rude beast!

Mrs. Dainty
Insolent brute!

Lady Fidget
Brute! stinking, mortified, rotten French wether, to dare—

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 来罢，来罢，老兄；怎么，连女人家甜美的交际都要躲？女人，那甜美、温柔、和顺、驯良、高贵的造物，天生是给男人做伴的——

**霍纳.** 照这么说，那种温柔、和顺、驯良、更高贵的造物——垂耳猎狗——也一样，女人的把戏它全会：会摇尾乞怜，会趴下，挨了打反而摇得更欢；朋友来看你，它冲人家狂吠；搅得你床铺硌得慌，送你一身跳蚤，有时还送你疥疮。全部的分别只在：狗是更忠心的畜生，只对一位主人摇尾巴。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 嘿！嘿！嘿！

**斯奎米什太太.** 好个粗野的畜生！

**丹蒂小姐.** 放肆的蛮牛！

**菲吉特夫人.** 畜生！臭烘烘、干瘪、烂心烂肺的法国阉羊，竟敢——

===Original===
Sir Jasper
Hold, an't please your ladyship.—For shame, Master Horner! your mother was a woman—*Aside.* Now shall I never reconcile 'em.—*Aside to **Lady Fidget**.* Hark you, madam, take my advice in your anger. You know you often want one to make up your drolling pack of ombre players, and you may cheat him easily; for he's an ill gamester, and consequently loves play. Besides, you know you have but two old civil gentlemen (with stinking breaths too) to wait upon you abroad; take in the third into your service. The other are but crazy; and a lady should have a supernumerary gentleman-usher as a supernumerary coach-horse, lest sometimes you should be forced to stay at home.

Lady Fidget
But are you sure he loves play, and has money?

Sir Jasper
He loves play as much as you, and has money as much as I.

Lady Fidget
Then I am contented to make him pay for his scurrility. Money makes up in a measure all other wants in men.—Those whom we cannot make hold for gallants, we make fine. *Aside.*

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 息怒，请夫人息怒。——也不怕害臊，霍纳先生！令堂也是女人哪——（旁白）这下永远甭想劝和了。——（向菲吉特夫人旁白）听我说，夫人，趁您在火头上，听我一句劝。您知道，你们那桌插科打诨的翁布雷牌局常常三缺一；正好轻易赢他的钱——他牌艺差，偏偏就因为这个爱打牌。再者，您出门在外，身边拢共只有两位客客气气的老先生（还是一口臭气）伺候；把第三位收进班底罢。前两位都靠不住；一位太太该多备一位跟班绅士，就像多备一匹副马，免得有时只好困在家里。

**菲吉特夫人.** 可你拿得准他又爱打牌、又有钱么？

**贾斯珀爵士.** 他爱牌爱得跟您一样，有钱有得跟我一样。

**菲吉特夫人.** 那我情愿叫他拿钱赎他那张臭嘴。男人的短处，大都可以拿钱抵补。——做不成咱们情人的，就罚成财主。（旁白。）

===Original===
Sir Jasper
*Aside.* So, so; now to mollify, wheedle him.—*Aside to **Horner**.* Master Horner, will you never keep civil company? methinks 'tis time now, since you are only fit for them. Come, come, man, you must e'en fall to visiting our wives, eating at our tables, drinking tea with our virtuous relations after dinner, dealing cards to 'em, reading plays and gazettes to 'em, picking fleas out of their smocks for 'em, collecting receipts, new songs, women, pages, and footmen for 'em.

Horner
I hope they'll afford me better employment, sir.

Sir Jasper
He! he! he! 'tis fit you know your work before you come into your place. And since you are unprovided of a lady to flatter, and a good house to eat at, pray frequent mine, and call my wife mistress, and she shall call you gallant, according to the custom.

Horner
Who, I?

Sir Jasper
Faith, thou sha't for my sake; come, for my sake only.

Horner
For your sake—

Sir Jasper
Come, come, here's a gamester for you; let him be a little familiar sometimes; nay, what if a little rude? Gamesters may be rude with ladies, you know.

Lady Fidget
Yes; losing gamesters have a privilege with women.

Horner
I always thought the contrary, that the winning gamester had most privilege with women; for when you have lost your money to a man, you'll lose anything you have, all you have, they say, and he may use you as he pleases.

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.**（旁白）好，好，这就软下来哄他。——（向霍纳旁白）霍纳先生，您就永远不肯结交规矩的伴儿么？依我说，如今正是时候——反正您也就只配这个了。来罢，来罢，老兄，您索性就来登门拜望咱们的太太，上咱们的饭桌，饭后陪咱们贞洁的女眷喝茶，替她们发牌，给她们念剧本、念公报，替她们从衬衣里捉跳蚤，替她们张罗秘方、新歌谣，还有女人、小厮和跟班。

**霍纳.** 我指望她们能派我点更好的差事呢，先生。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 嘿！嘿！嘿！上岗之前先摸清活计，应该的。您如今既没有一位可奉承的太太，也没有一家可吃饭的好宅子，就请常来我家，称我太太为「女主人」，她照惯例称您「骑士」。

**霍纳.** 谁，我？

**贾斯珀爵士.** 真的，看在我的份上你就从了吧；来罢，就看我一个人的面子。

**霍纳.** 看您的面子——

**贾斯珀爵士.** 来，来，这不正是现成一位牌友；有时不妨同他亲热点；嗐，稍稍放肆点又何妨？您知道，赌客在太太们跟前是可以放肆的。

**菲吉特夫人.** 是；输钱的赌客在女人跟前更有特权。

**霍纳.** 我一向倒以为是反过来——赢钱的赌客在女人跟前特权最大；因为据说钱输给了哪个男人，你就什么都能输给他，家底儿都能输光，他爱怎么摆布你就怎么摆布。

===Original===
Sir Jasper
He! he! he! well, win or lose, you shall have your liberty with her.

Lady Fidget
As he behaves himself; and for your sake I'll give him admittance and freedom.

Horner
All sorts of freedom, madam?

Sir Jasper
Ay, ay, ay, all sorts of freedom thou canst take. And so go to her, begin thy new employment; wheedle her, jest with her, and be better acquainted one with another.

Horner
*Aside.* I think I know her already; therefore may venture with her my secret for hers. ***Horner** and **Lady Fidget** whisper.*

Sir Jasper
Sister cuz, I have provided an innocent playfellow for you there.

Mrs. Dainty
Who, he?

Mrs. Squeamish
There's a playfellow, indeed!

Sir Jasper
Yes sure.—What, he is good enough to play at cards, blindman's buff, or the fool with, sometimes!

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 嘿！嘿！嘿！好罢，输也罢赢也罢，您同她来往尽可自便。

**菲吉特夫人.** 也得看他守不守规矩；看您的面子，我准他进门，给他自由。

**霍纳.** 什么样的自由都给么，夫人？

**贾斯珀爵士.** 是，是，是，你能拿多少自由就给多少自由。这就过去罢，上你的新差；哄哄她，同她说笑，彼此多亲近亲近。

**霍纳.**（旁白）我看我本来就认得她；所以不妨拿我的秘密换她的秘密。（*霍纳**与**菲吉特夫人**耳语。*）

**贾斯珀爵士.** 表妹，我给你物色了一位无害的玩伴儿。

**丹蒂小姐.** 谁，他？

**斯奎米什太太.** 好一位「玩伴儿」，真的！

**贾斯珀爵士.** 当然。——怎么，陪打打牌、玩玩蒙眼捉人，偶尔再当当傻子，他还不够格么！

===Original===
Mrs. Squeamish
Foh! we'll have no such playfellows.

Mrs. Dainty
No, sir; you shan't choose playfellows for us, we thank you.

Sir Jasper
Nay, pray hear me. *Whispering to them.*

Lady Fidget
But, poor gentleman, could you be so generous, so truly a man of honour, as for the sakes of us women of honour, to cause yourself to be reported no man? No man! and to suffer yourself the greatest shame that could fall upon a man, that none might fall upon us women by your conversation? but, indeed, sir, as perfectly, perfectly the same man as before your going into France, sir? as perfectly, perfectly, sir?

Horner
As perfectly, perfectly, madam. Nay, I scorn you should take my word; I desire to be tried only, madam.

Lady Fidget
Well, that's spoken again like a man of honour: all men of honour desire to come to the test. But, indeed, generally you men report such things of yourselves, one does not know how or whom to believe; and it is come to that pass, we dare not take your words no more than your tailor's, without some staid servant of yours be bound with you. But I have so strong a faith in your honour, dear, dear, noble sir, that I'd forfeit mine for yours, at any time, dear sir.

===Chinese===
**斯奎米什太太.** 呸！我们不要这样的玩伴儿。

**丹蒂小姐.** 不要，先生；还劳不到您替我们挑玩伴儿，多谢了。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 嗐，务请听我说完。（向她们耳语。）

**菲吉特夫人.** 可是，可怜的先生，您竟能这样慷慨、这样真正讲荣誉，为了我们这些有名节的太太，肯让人把您传成「不是男人」？不是男人！甘愿领受一个男人所能蒙受的奇耻大辱，好叫您的亲近不给女人们带来半点损害？而且，先生，您当真、当真还同去法国之前一模一样地是个男人么，先生？一模一样，一模一样，先生？

**霍纳.** 一模一样，一模一样，夫人。嗐，空口无凭我不稀罕您信；只求验一验，夫人。

**菲吉特夫人.** 这才又是讲荣誉的男人的口吻：凡讲荣誉的男人都愿意受试。不过说真的，你们男人自吹自擂惯了，叫人不知信谁的、怎么信；世道落到这步田地：你们的话同你们裁缝的话一样不敢领受，非得有位持重的老家人一同作保不可。可我对您的荣誉信得这样足，亲亲的、尊贵的先生，随便什么时候，我都情愿拿我的名节换您的，亲爱的先生。

===Original===
Horner
No, madam, you should not need to forfeit it for me; I have given you security already to save you harmless, my late reputation being so well known in the world, madam.

Lady Fidget
But if upon any future falling-out, or upon a suspicion of my taking the trust out of your hands, to employ some other, you yourself should betray your trust, dear sir? I mean, if you'll give me leave to speak obscenely, you might tell, dear sir.

Horner
If I did, nobody would believe me. The reputation of impotency is as hardly recovered again in the world as that of cowardice, dear madam.

Lady Fidget
Nay, then, as one may say, you may do your worst, dear, dear sir.

Sir Jasper
Come, is your ladyship reconciled to him yet? have you agreed on matters? for I must be gone to Whitehall.

Lady Fidget
Why, indeed, Sir Jasper, Master Horner is a thousand, thousand times a better man than I thought him. Cousin Squeamish, sister Dainty, I can name him now. Truly, not long ago, you know, I thought his very name obscenity; and I would as soon have lain with him as have named him.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 不必，夫人，您用不着为我赔上名节；我早已交过了保，保管您万无一失——我近来那名声，天下谁人不知，夫人。

**菲吉特夫人.** 可要是日后咱们拌了嘴，或是您疑心我把这信托从您手里收回去、另托别人，您自己倒泄了密，亲爱的先生？我是说——恕我说句粗话——您兴许会讲出去，亲爱的先生。

**霍纳.** 我讲了也没人信。「不中用」的名声，跟「懦怯」的名声一样，一旦落地，天下再难翻身，亲爱的夫人。
