**菲吉特夫人.** 这么着，这么说罢，您就放开了干您的最坏罢，亲亲的先生。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 怎么样，夫人同他和好了没有？事情谈妥了么？我该去白厅了。

**菲吉特夫人.** 嗐，说真的，贾斯珀爵士，霍纳先生比我先前想的，好上了一千倍、一千倍。斯奎米什表妹，丹蒂妹妹，如今我连他的名字也叫得出口了。你们知道，就在不久前，我当他这个名字本身就是脏字眼；叫我念他的名字，同叫我陪他睡觉一样难。

===Original===
Sir Jasper
Very likely, poor madam.

Mrs. Dainty
I believe it.

Mrs. Squeamish
No doubt on't.

Sir Jasper
Well, well—that your ladyship is as virtuous as any she, I know, and him all the town knows—he! he! he! therefore now you like him, get you gone to your business together, go, go to your business, I say, pleasure, whilst I go to my pleasure, business.

Lady Fidget
Come, then, dear gallant.

Horner
Come away, my dearest mistress.

Sir Jasper
So, so; why, 'tis as I'd have it.

*Exit.*

Horner
And as I'd have it.

Lady Fidget
Who for his business from his wife will run,
Takes the best care to have her business done.

*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 很可能，可怜的夫人。

**丹蒂小姐.** 我信。

**斯奎米什太太.** 没跑儿。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 好罢，好罢——夫人您同任何太太一样贞洁，这个我知道；他嘛，全城都知道——嘿！嘿！嘿！所以如今你们两情相悦，就一道办你们的「正事」去罢，去，去办正事——我是说，乐事；我去办我的乐事——公务。

**菲吉特夫人.** 那就走罢，亲爱的骑士。

**霍纳.** 走罢，我最亲爱的情人。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 好，好；正合我意。

*（下。）*

**霍纳.** 也正合我意。

**菲吉特夫人.**
谁为「公务」撇下老婆拔腿奔，
谁就最会替她把「公务」办周全。

*（同下。）*

## Act III / 第三幕

### Scene I / 第一场

===Original===
A room in **Pinchwife's** house.

*Enter **Alithea** and **Mrs. Pinchwife**.*

Alithea
Sister, what ails you? you are grown melancholy.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Would it not make anyone melancholy to see you go every day fluttering about abroad, whilst I must stay at home like a poor lonely sullen bird in a cage?

Alithea
Ay, sister; but you came young, and just from the nest to your cage: so that I thought you liked it, and could be as cheerful in't as others that took their flight themselves early, and are hopping abroad in the open air.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Nay, I confess I was quiet enough till my husband told me what pure lives the London ladies live abroad, with their dancing, meetings, and junketings, and dressed every day in their best gowns; and I warrant you, play at ninepins every day of the week, so they do.

===Chinese===
平奇怀夫家中一室。

*（**阿莉西娅**与**平奇怀夫太太**上。）*

**阿莉西娅.** 妹妹，你怎么了？蔫蔫的，闷出病来了。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 天天瞧你在外头扑腾得欢，我倒得像只可怜的、孤零零、闷声不响的鸟儿关在笼子里——换了谁不闷出病来？

**阿莉西娅.** 是这样，妹妹；可你来得早，才出窝就进了笼子：我还当你喜欢呢，也能像那些早早自己飞出来的鸟儿一样自在，在外头露天里蹦跶。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 嗐，我认账，起先我倒是够安分的，直到我男人跟我说，伦敦的太太们在外头过的日子多滋润——又是跳舞，又是聚会，又是野餐出游，天天穿着顶好的衣裳；还保管一礼拜天天打九柱戏呢，真的。

===Original===
*Enter **Pinchwife**.*

Pinchwife
Come, what's here to do? you are putting the town-pleasures in her head, and setting her a-longing.

Alithea
Yes, after ninepins. You suffer none to give her those longings you mean but yourself.

Pinchwife
I tell her of the vanities of the town like a confessor.

Alithea
A confessor! just such a confessor as he that, by forbidding a silly ostler to grease the horse's teeth, taught him to do't.

Pinchwife
Come, Mrs. Flippant, good precepts are lost when bad examples are still before us: the liberty you take abroad makes her hanker after it, and out of humour at home. Poor wretch! she desired not to come to London; I would bring her.

===Chinese===
*（**平奇怀夫**上。）*

**平奇怀夫.** 喂，这是干什么？你们把城里的乐子往她脑袋里灌，勾得她眼馋起来了。

**阿莉西娅.** 是啊，馋九柱戏呢。你说的那种馋，除了你自个儿，你不许任何人勾她。

**平奇怀夫.** 我跟她讲城里的虚荣，就像听罪神父讲道似的。

**阿莉西娅.** 听罪神父！活像那个神父——不许傻马夫往马牙齿上抹油，反倒教会了他这一手。

**平奇怀夫.** 得了，轻嘴薄舌太太，眼前摆着坏榜样，好教训全白搭：你在外头的那份自由，勾得她眼馋心痒，在家里憋气。可怜的人儿！她本不想来伦敦；是我非要带来的。

===Original===
Alithea
Very well.

Pinchwife
She has been this week in town, and never desired till this afternoon to go abroad.

Alithea
Was she not at a play yesterday?

Pinchwife
Yes; but she ne'er asked me; I was myself the cause of her going.

Alithea
Then if she ask you again, you are the cause of her asking, and not my example.

Pinchwife
Well, tomorrow night I shall be rid of you; and the next day, before 'tis light, she and I'll be rid of the town, and my dreadful apprehensions.—Come, be not melancholy; for thou sha't go into the country after tomorrow, dearest.

Alithea
Great comfort!

Mrs. Pinchwife
Pish! what d'ye tell me of the country for?

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 很好。

**平奇怀夫.** 她进城才一个礼拜，直到今天下午才头一回想出门。

**阿莉西娅.** 她昨天不是看戏去了么？

**平奇怀夫.** 是去了；可她从没开过口；是我自个儿招她去的。

**阿莉西娅.** 那她这回开了口，招她开口的还是你，不是我带的头。

**平奇怀夫.** 好了，明晚我就送走你；后天一大早，天不亮，她跟我也就躲开这座城，躲开我这吓人的提心吊胆。——来，别蔫头耷脑的；后天你就能下乡了，最亲爱的。

**阿莉西娅.** 好大的安慰！

**平奇怀夫太太.** 啐！跟我说乡下做什么？

===Original===
Pinchwife
How's this! what, pish at the country?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Let me alone; I am not well.

Pinchwife
O, if that be all—what ails my dearest?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Truly, I don't know: but I have not been well since you told me there was a gallant at the play in love with me.

Pinchwife
Ha!—

Alithea
That's by my example too!

Pinchwife
Nay, if you are not well, but are so concerned, because a lewd fellow chanced to lie, and say he liked you, you'll make me sick too.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 这是怎么了！什么，对乡下啐？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 别理我；我不舒坦。

**平奇怀夫.** 哦，要是就为这个——我的心肝哪儿不舒坦？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 老实说，我也说不上来：可打你告诉我戏园子里有个公子爷爱上了我，我就一直不舒坦。

**平奇怀夫.** 哈！——

**阿莉西娅.** 这也是我带的头！

**平奇怀夫.** 嗐，你要是不舒坦，就因为一个浪子随口撒句谎、说喜欢你，你就这么牵肠挂肚，那你也要惹得我害病了。

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
Of what sickness?

Pinchwife
O, of that which is worse than the plague, jealousy.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Pish, you jeer! I'm sure there's no such disease in our receipt-book at home.

Pinchwife
No, thou never met'st with it, poor innocent.—Well, if thou cuckold me, 'twill be my own fault—for cuckolds and bastards are generally makers of their own fortune. *Aside.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
Well, but pray, bud, let's go to a play tonight.

Pinchwife
'Tis just done, she comes from it. But why are you so eager to see a play?

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 害什么病？

**平奇怀夫.** 哦，害那比瘟疫还凶的病——吃醋。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 啐，你逗我！咱们家那本药方书里，保管没有这么一号病。

**平奇怀夫.** 是没有，你从没碰上过它，可怜的天真人。——（旁白）唉，你要真叫我戴了绿帽，那也是我自作自受——绿帽汉和私生子，多半都是自个儿挣来的命。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 好了，可是求求你，乖乖，今晚咱们看戏去罢。

**平奇怀夫.** 戏刚散，她才从那儿回来。可你怎么这么急着看戏？

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
Faith, dear, not that I care one pin for their talk there; but I like to look upon the playermen, and would see, if I could, the gallant you say loves me: that's all, dear bud.

Pinchwife
Is that all, dear bud?

Alithea
This proceeds from my example!

Mrs. Pinchwife
But if the play be done, let's go abroad, however, dear bud.

Pinchwife
Come have a little patience and thou shalt go into the country on Friday.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Therefore I would see first some sights to tell my neighbours of. Nay, I will go abroad, that's once.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 说真的，亲爱的，倒不是他们台上说什么我爱听一个子儿；我就是爱瞧戏子爷们，还想瞅一眼——要能瞅着的话——你说的那位爱我的公子爷：就这么点事，亲乖乖。

**平奇怀夫.** 就这么点事么，亲乖乖？

**阿莉西娅.** 这也是我带的头！

**平奇怀夫太太.** 可戏既散了，咱们好歹出门逛逛去罢，亲乖乖。

**平奇怀夫.** 来，忍一忍，星期五就送你下乡。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 所以我想先逛几处景致，回去讲给街坊们听。不成的，我一定要出门，这回说定了。

===Original===
Alithea
I'm the cause of this desire too!

Pinchwife
But now I think on't, who, who was the cause of Horner's coming to my lodgings today? That was you.

Alithea
No, you, because you would not let him see your handsome wife out of your lodging.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Why, O Lord! did the gentleman come hither to see me indeed?

Pinchwife
No, no.—You are not the cause of that damned question too, Mistress Alithea?—*Aside.* Well, she's in the right of it. He is in love with my wife—and comes after her—'tis so—but I'll nip his love in the bud; lest he should follow us into the country, and break his chariot-wheel near our house, on purpose for an excuse to come to't. But I think I know the town.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 这份馋也是我惹的！

**平奇怀夫.** 可我想起来了，今天是谁、是谁招得霍纳上我家来的？是你。

**阿莉西娅.** 不，是你——谁叫你不许他到你家以外的地方瞧你那位标致太太呢。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 哎呀，老天爷！那位先生当真是上门来看我的么？

**平奇怀夫.** 不，不。——阿莉西娅小姐，这个该死的问题也是你惹的罢？——（旁白）唉，她说得在理。他爱上我老婆了——是追着她来的——就是这样——可我要把他的情掐死在芽里；免得他一路追到乡下，故意把马车轮子弄断在我家附近，好寻个由头进门。不过我自认懂得城里这一套。

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
Come, pray, bud, let's go abroad before 'tis late; for I will go, that's flat and plain.

Pinchwife
*Aside.* So! the obstinacy already of the town-wife; and I must, whilst she's here, humour her like one.—*Aloud.* Sister, how shall we do, that she may not be seen, or known?

Alithea
Let her put on her mask.

Pinchwife
Pshaw! a mask makes people but the more inquisitive, and is as ridiculous a disguise as a stage-beard: her shape, stature, habit will be known. And if we should meet with Horner, he would be sure to take acquaintance with us, must wish her joy, kiss her, talk to her, leer upon her, and the devil and all. No, I'll not use her to a mask, 'tis dangerous; for masks have made more cuckolds than the best faces that ever were known.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 来嘛，求你，乖乖，趁天没黑出门去；我一定要去，这话说定了，明明白白。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）瞧！城里娘们的犟劲儿这就上来了；她在这儿一天，我就得拿城里那一套哄她一天。——（高声）妹妹，怎么着才能叫她不叫人瞧见、不叫人认出来？

**阿莉西娅.** 叫她戴上面纱。

**平奇怀夫.** 啐！面纱反倒叫人更要打听，跟舞台上粘胡子一样可笑的伪装：她的身段、个头、装束都认得出。再说真撞上霍纳，他准要上来攀谈，非贺她不可，亲她，跟她搭话，拿眼睛瞟她，没完没了的鬼名堂。不，我不给她用面纱，太危险；面纱制造的绿帽，比天底下顶有名的脸蛋造的还多。

===Original===
Alithea
How will you do then?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Nay, shall we go? The Exchange will be shut, and I have a mind to see that.

Pinchwife
So—I have it—I'll dress her up in the suit we are to carry down to her brother, little Sir James; nay, I understand the town-tricks. Come, let's go dress her. A mask! no—a woman masked, like a covered dish, gives a man curiosity and appetite; when, it may be, uncovered, 'twould turn his stomach: no, no.

Alithea
Indeed your comparison is something a greasy one: but I had a gentle gallant used to say, A beauty masked, like the sun in eclipse, gathers together more gazers than if it shined out.

*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 那你打算怎么办？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 嗐，到底去不去呀？交易所要关门了，我还想瞧瞧那个呢。

**平奇怀夫.** 有了一—我把咱们要捎给她弟弟小詹姆斯爵士的那套衣裳给她穿上；嗐，城里这些门道我懂。来，去给她装扮。面纱！不用——蒙着面纱的女人，就像盖着盖儿的菜，勾人的好奇和馋虫；兴许一揭开，倒叫人反胃：不用，不用。

**阿莉西娅.** 你这比方可真够油腻的：不过我从前有位斯文的公子爱说：美人蒙纱，就像日蚀里的太阳，比朗朗照着更招人围观。

*（同下。）*

### Scene II / 第二场

===Original===
The New Exchange.

*Enter **Horner**, **Harcourt**, and **Dorilant**.*

Dorilant
Engaged to women, and not sup with us!

Horner
Ay, a pox on 'em all!

Harcourt
You were much a more reasonable man in the morning, and had as noble resolutions against 'em, as a widower of a week's liberty.

Dorilant
Did I ever think to see you keep company with women in vain!

Horner
In vain: no—'tis since I can't love 'em, to be revenged on 'em.

===Chinese===
新交易所。

*（**霍纳**、**哈考特**与**多里兰特**上。）*

**多里兰特.** 有妇人之约，就不跟我们吃夜宵了！

**霍纳.** 是啊，叫她们一齐遭瘟去！

**哈考特.** 你今天早上还像个明白事理的人，立下那么高贵的决心要抵制她们，跟一个只自由了一个礼拜的鳏夫似的。

**多里兰特.** 我何曾想到会看见你白陪女人的空！

**霍纳.** 白陪：不——如今我爱不成她们，正好报仇。

===Original===
Harcourt
Now your sting is gone, you looked in the box amongst all those women like a drone in the hive; all upon you, shoved and ill-used by 'em all, and thrust from one side to t'other.

Dorilant
Yet he must be buzzing amongst 'em still, like other beetle-headed liquorish drones. Avoid 'em, and hate 'em, as they hate you.

Horner
Because I do hate 'em, and would hate 'em yet more, I'll frequent 'em. You may see by marriage, nothing makes a man hate a woman more than her constant conversation. In short, I converse with 'em, as you do with rich fools, to laugh at 'em and use 'em ill.

Dorilant
But I would no more sup with women, unless I could lie with 'em, than sup with a rich coxcomb, unless I could cheat him.

Horner
Yes, I have known thee sup with a fool for his drinking; if he could set out your hand that way only, you were satisfied, and if he were a wine-swallowing mouth, 'twas enough.

===Chinese===
**哈考特.** 你如今没了螫刺，坐在包厢里混在一群女人当中，活像蜂巢里的雄蜂；谁都骑在你头上，个个作践你，把你从这边推到那边。

**多里兰特.** 可他还非得在她们当中嗡嗡不可，跟别的笨脑袋馋嘴雄蜂一样。躲开她们，恨她们，就像她们恨你一样。

**霍纳.** 就因为我恨她们，还想恨得更透，我才偏要常同她们来往。看看婚姻就知道：没什么比朝夕厮守更叫男人恨一个女人。一句话，我同她们交往，就像你们同有钱的傻子交往：为的是笑话他们、作践他们。

**多里兰特.** 可要我陪女人吃夜宵，就得能睡她们；就像陪有钱的草包吃夜宵，就得能骗他的钱——不然我一口也不去。

**霍纳.** 得了吧，我就见过你为着酒陪傻子吃夜宵；只要他单在这一项上衬你的手，你就满足，只要他是个灌酒的嗓子，就够本了。

===Original===
Harcourt
Yes, a man drinks often with a fool, as he tosses with a marker, only to keep his hand in use. But do the ladies drink?

Horner
Yes, sir; and I shall have the pleasure at least of laying 'em flat with a bottle, and bring as much scandal that way upon 'em as formerly t'other.

Harcourt
Perhaps you may prove as weak a brother among 'em that way as t'other.

Dorilant
Foh! drinking with women is as unnatural as scolding with 'em. But 'tis a pleasure of decayed fornicators, and the basest way of quenching love.

Harcourt
Nay, 'tis drowning love, instead of quenching it. But leave us for civil women too!

Dorilant
Ay, when he can't be the better for 'em. We hardly pardon a man that leaves his friend for a wench, and that's a pretty lawful call.

===Chinese===
**哈考特.** 是啊，人常同傻子对饮，就像同记分员掷骰子，只为活动活动手法。可这几位太太喝酒么？

**霍纳.** 喝，先生；我至少能得一样乐趣——一瓶酒把她们放平，在这一路上给她们招来的丑闻，同先前那一路一样多。

**哈考特.** 只怕你在这条路上，同那条路上一样，也是个不中用的角儿。

**多里兰特.** 呸！同女人喝酒，就跟同女人对骂一样不合天理。那是过气嫖客的乐子，是灭欲最下三滥的法子。

**哈考特.** 何止，那是把爱情淹死，不是浇灭。——竟还为「规矩女人」撇下我们！

**多里兰特.** 是啊，谁叫他占不着便宜呢。为个娘们撇下朋友，我们尚且难饶恕——那还算相当合法的由头。

===Original===
Horner
Faith, I would not leave you for 'em, if they would not drink.

Dorilant
Who would disappoint his company at Lewis's for a gossiping?

Harcourt
Foh! Wine and women, good apart, together are as nauseous as sack and sugar. But hark you, sir, before you go, a little of your advice; an old maimed general, when unfit for action, is fittest for counsel. I have other designs upon women than eating and drinking with them; I am in love with Sparkish's mistress, whom he is to marry tomorrow: now how shall I get her?

*Enter **Sparkish**, looking about.*

Horner
Why, here comes one will help you to her.

Harcourt
He! he, I tell you, is my rival, and will hinder my love.

Horner
No; a foolish rival and a jealous husband assist their rival's designs; for they are sure to make their women hate them, which is the first step to their love for another man.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 说真的，她们要是不灌酒，我也不至于撇下你们。

**多里兰特.** 谁会为一场娘们儿的闲磕牙，回掉刘易斯馆（Lewis's）一桌牌友？

**哈考特.** 呸！酒和女人，分开各有各的好，凑在一块就像白葡萄酒兑糖，腻得发呕。不过听我说，先生，你走之前讨你一句高见；残废的老将军上不得阵，正好出谋划策。我对女人另有一番图谋，不光是同她们吃喝；我爱上了斯帕基什的情人——他明天就要娶她：你说，我怎么把她弄到手？

*（**斯帕基什**东张西望地上。）*

**霍纳.** 瞧，这就来了个肯帮你到手的。

**哈考特.** 他！我告诉你，他正是我的情敌，只会坏事。

**霍纳.** 不；愚蠢的情敌和吃醋的丈夫，最成全情敌的好事；因为他们保管叫女人恨透自己，而恨，正是爱上旁人的头一步。

===Original===
Harcourt
But I cannot come near his mistress but in his company.

Horner
Still the better for you; for fools are most easily cheated when they themselves are accessories: and he is to be bubbled of his mistress as of his money, the common mistress, by keeping him company.

Sparkish
Who is that that is to be bubbled? Faith, let me snack; I han't met with a bubble since Christmas. 'Gad, I think bubbles are like their brother woodcocks, go out with the cold weather.

Harcourt
A pox! he did not hear all, I hope. *Apart to **Horner**.*

Sparkish
Come, you bubbling rogues you, where do we sup?—Oh, Harcourt, my mistress tells me you have been making fierce love to her all the play long: ha! ha!—But I—

Harcourt
I make love to her!

===Chinese===
**哈考特.** 可我只有搭着他才能挨近他的情人。

**霍纳.** 那对你更有利；傻子在自个儿搭伙当同谋的时候最容易上当：骗他的情人，同骗他的钱——大家共有的情妇——一样，都靠陪着他这一路。

**斯帕基什.** 谁要挨宰？说真的，算我一股；打圣诞节起就没遇上过冤大头了。老天，我看冤大头跟他们山鹬兄弟一个样，天一冷就绝迹。

**哈考特.**（向霍纳旁白）糟了！他没全听见罢，但愿。

**斯帕基什.** 来呀，你们这对坑人精，咱们上哪儿吃夜宵？——哦，哈考特，我情人告诉我，整场戏你都在死命向她求爱：哈！哈！——不过我——

**哈考特.** 我向她求爱！

===Original===
Sparkish
Nay, I forgive thee, for I think I know thee, and I know her; but I am sure I know myself.

Harcourt
Did she tell you so? I see all women are like these of the Exchange; who, to enhance the prize of their commodities, report to their fond customers offers which were never made 'em.

Horner
Ay, women are apt to tell before the intrigue, as men after it, and so show themselves the vainer sex. But hast thou a mistress, Sparkish? 'Tis as hard for me to believe it, as that thou ever hadst a bubble, as you bragged just now.

Sparkish
O, your servant, sir: are you at your raillery, sir? But we are some of us beforehand with you today at the play. The wits were something bold with you, sir; did you not hear us laugh?

Horner
Yes; but I thought you had gone to plays, to laugh at the poet's wit, not at your own.

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 嗐，我原谅你，我看我认得你，也认得她；可我保管认得我自个儿。

**哈考特.** 她这么跟你说的？我看天下的女人都跟交易所里这些一个样：为了抬高货价，向痴心的主顾谎报多少从没人出过的价。

**霍纳.** 是啊，女人爱在私情之前张扬，男人爱在私情之后吹嘘——足见哪一边更虚荣。可你当真有情人么，斯帕基什？叫我相信这个，比相信你方才吹的、你宰过冤大头还难。

**斯帕基什.** 哦，您哪，先生：又开您那俏皮玩笑来了，先生？可今天戏园子里，我们有些人先占了您一着。才子们对您可是放肆了些，先生；您没听见我们笑么？

**霍纳.** 听见了；只当我以为你们上戏园子，是笑诗人的俏皮，不是笑自个儿的。

===Original===
Sparkish
Your servant, sir: no, I thank you. 'Gad I go to a play as to a country treat; I carry my own wine to one, and my own wit to t'other, or else I'm sure I should not be merry at either. And the reason why we are so often louder than the players, is, because we think we speak more wit, and so become the poet's rivals in his audience: for to tell you the truth, we hate the silly rogues; nay, so much, that we find fault even with their bawdy upon the stage, whilst we talk nothing else in the pit as loud.

Horner
But why shouldst thou hate the silly poets? Thou hast too much wit to be one; and they, like whores, are only hated by each other: and thou dost scorn writing, I'm sure.

Sparkish
Yes; I'd have you to know I scorn writing: but women, women, that make men do all foolish things, make 'em write songs too. Everybody does it. 'Tis even as common with lovers, as playing with fans; and you can no more help rhyming to your Phillis, than drinking to your Phillis.

Harcourt
Nay, poetry in love is no more to be avoided than jealousy.

Dorilant
But the poets damned your songs, did they?

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 您哪，先生：不，谢谢。老天，我上戏园子就像赴乡下的酒席；那边自带酒，这边自带俏皮，不然保管两头都乐不起来。我们常比台上叫得还响，就因为我们觉得自个儿说的俏皮更多，就这么在台下当了诗人的对台：跟您说句实话，我们恨那些傻蛋恨得很；恨到连他们台上的荤话都要挑刺，可我们自个儿在池座里喊得震天响的，除了荤话没别的。

**霍纳.** 可你何必恨那些傻诗人？你俏皮得太多余，当不成他们那一路；他们呀，跟窑姐儿一样，只彼此相恨：再说，我拿准你是瞧不起动笔的。

**斯帕基什.** 是；叫你知道，我瞧不起动笔：可女人哪，女人——男人干的傻事全是她们撺掇的——连情歌也叫人写。人人都写。情人写诗，同摆弄扇子一样家常；你没法子不给你的菲莉斯押韵，就像没法子不为你的菲莉斯干杯。

**哈考特.** 可不是，恋爱中人逃不开做诗，就像逃不开吃醋。

**多里兰特.** 可诗人们把你的歌毙了，是不是？

===Original===
Sparkish
Damn the poets! they have turned 'em into burlesque, as they call it. That burlesque is a hocus-pocus trick they have got, which, by the virtue of *Hictius doctius topsy turvy*, they make a wise and witty man in the world, a fool upon the stage you know not how: and 'tis therefore I hate 'em too, for I know not but it may be my own case; for they'll put a man into a play for looking asquint. Their predecessors were contented to make serving-men only their stage-fools: but these rogues must have gentlemen, with a pox to 'em, nay, knights; and, indeed, you shall hardly see a fool upon the stage but he's a knight. And to tell you the truth, they have kept me these six years from being a knight in earnest, for fear of being knighted in a play, and dubbed a fool.

Dorilant
Blame 'em not, they must follow their copy, the age.

Harcourt
But why shouldst thou be afraid of being in a play, who expose yourself every day in the playhouses, and at public places?

Horner
'Tis but being on the stage, instead of standing on a bench in the pit.

Dorilant
Don't you give money to painters to draw you like? and are you afraid of your pictures at length in a playhouse, where all your mistresses may see you?

Sparkish
A pox! painters don't draw the smallpox or pimples in one's face. Come, damn all your silly authors whatever, all books and booksellers, by the world; and all readers, courteous or uncourteous!

Harcourt
But who comes here, Sparkish?

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 毙掉诗人还差不多！他们把我的歌翻成了「滑稽体」，他们管那叫滑稽体（burlesque）。那滑稽体是他们新学来的一套戏法，凭着「戏克图斯多克图斯、天翻地覆」（*Hictius doctius topsy turvy*）的咒语，把世上一个明白体面的人，稀里糊涂变成台上的傻子：所以我才也恨他们；谁知道哪天不轮到我头上；他们连斜眼看人的人都往戏里塞。他们的前辈知足，只拿仆人当台上的傻子：这帮家伙可非得拿爷们开涮，叫他们遭瘟，还拿爵士开涮；真的，你在台上难得见一个傻子不是爵士。跟你说实话，就为怕在戏里封爵、封个傻子，他们害得我这六年不敢当真受封爵士。

**多里兰特.** 怪不得他们，他们照着底本描——照的是这世道。

**哈考特.** 可你怕进戏做什么？你天天在戏园子里、在公共场所露你的怯。

**霍纳.** 无非是从池座的条凳上，挪到台上去站着。

**多里兰特.** 你不是花钱请画家照你的样儿画么？怎么倒怕你那全身画像挂进戏园子，叫你所有的情人都瞧得着？

**斯帕基什.** 遭瘟！画家可不画人脸上的天花和粉刺。来，叫你们那些傻作家统统遭瘟，还有所有的书、书贩，凭全世界起誓；还有所有的读者——管他客气不客气！

**哈考特.** 咦，这是谁来了，斯帕基什？

===Original===
*Enter **Pinchwife** and **Mrs. Pinchwife** in man's clothes, **Alithea** and **Lucy**.*

Sparkish
Oh, hide me! There's my mistress too. ***Sparkish** hides himself behind **Harcourt**.*

Harcourt
She sees you.

Sparkish
But I will not see her. 'Tis time to go to Whitehall, and I must not fail the drawing-room.

Harcourt
Pray, first carry me, and reconcile me to her.

Sparkish
Another time. Faith, the king will have supped.

Harcourt
Not with the worse stomach for thy absence. Thou art one of those fools that think their attendance at the king's meals as necessary as his physicians, when you are more troublesome to him than his doctors or his dogs.

Sparkish
Pshaw! I know my interest, sir. Prithee hide me.

Horner
Your servant, Pinchwife.—What, he knows us not!

Pinchwife
Come along. *To his **Wife** aside.*

===Chinese===
*（**平奇怀夫**与男装的**平奇怀夫太太**、**阿莉西娅**及**露西**（Lucy）上。）*

**斯帕基什.** 哦，把我藏起来！我情人也在。（*斯帕基什**躲到**哈考特**身后。*）

**哈考特.** 她瞧见你了。

**斯帕基什.** 可我不瞧她。该去白厅了，觐见室我误不得。

**哈考特.** 求你先领我去，让我同她和解。

**斯帕基什.** 改天罢。说真的，国王这会儿夜宵都要用完了。

**哈考特.** 少了你，他胃口只会更好。你就是那种蠢货，以为伺候国王用膳跟伺候他吃药一样必不可少；其实你比他的大夫、比他的狗还惹他嫌。

**斯帕基什.** 啐！我知道我的好处所在，先生。行行好，把我藏起来。

**霍纳.** 您安，平奇怀夫。——怎么，他不认得咱们了！

**平奇怀夫.** 走。（拉太太在旁低语。）

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
Pray, have you any ballads? give me sixpenny worth.

Bookseller
We have no ballads.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Then give me *Covent Garden Drollery*, and a play or two—Oh, here's *Tarugo's Wiles*, and *The Slighted Maiden*;7 I'll have them.

Pinchwife
No; plays are not for your reading. Come along; will you discover yourself? *Apart to her.*

Horner
Who is that pretty youth with him, Sparkish?

Sparkish
I believe his wife's brother, because he's something like her: but I never saw her but once.

Horner
Extremely handsome; I have seen a face like it too. Let us follow 'em.

*Exeunt **Pinchwife**, **Mrs. Pinchwife**, **Pinchwife**, **Alithea**, and **Lucy**; **Horner** and **Dorilant** following them.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 请问，有歌片儿卖么？给我来六便士的。

**书贩.** 我们不卖歌片儿。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 那给我一本《科文特花园俚曲》（*Covent Garden Drollery*），再来一两本戏文——哦，这儿有《塔鲁戈的诡计》（*Tarugo's Wiles*），还有《受冷落的小姐》（*The Slighted Maiden*）7；我要这几本。

**平奇怀夫.** 不行；戏文不是给你读的。走；你要自曝身份么？（拉她到一旁。）

**霍纳.** 跟他一处那个俊俏后生是谁，斯帕基什？

**斯帕基什.** 我看是他老婆的弟弟，眉眼有几分像她：不过她我也只见过一回。

**霍纳.** 俊得很；这张脸我也见过一张像的。咱们跟上他们。

*（平奇怀夫、平奇怀夫太太、平奇怀夫、阿莉西娅与露西下；霍纳与多里兰特尾随而去。）*

===Original===
Harcourt
Come, Sparkish, your mistress saw you, and will be angry you go not to her. Besides, I would fain be reconciled to her, which none but you can do, dear friend.

Sparkish
Well, that's a better reason, dear friend. I would not go near her now for hers or my own sake; but I can deny you nothing: for though I have known thee a great while, never go, if I do not love thee as well as a new acquaintance.

Harcourt
I am obliged to you indeed, dear friend. I would be well with her, only to be well with thee still; for these ties to wives usually dissolve all ties to friends. I would be contented she should enjoy you a-nights, but I would have you to myself a-days as I have had, dear friend.

Sparkish
And thou shalt enjoy me a-days, dear, dear friend, never stir: and I'll be divorced from her, sooner than from thee. Come along.

Harcourt
*Aside.* So, we are hard put to't, when we make our rival our procurer; but neither she nor her brother would let me come near her now. When all's done, a rival is the best cloak to steal to a mistress under, without suspicion; and when we have once got to her as we desire, we throw him off like other cloaks.

*Exit **Sparkish, Harcourt** following him.*

===Chinese===
**哈考特.** 来罢，斯帕基什，你情人瞧见你了，见你不过去要恼的。再说我一心想同她讲和，这事儿除了你没第二人办得到，亲爱的朋友。

**斯帕基什.** 嗐，这理由倒好些，亲爱的朋友。看在她的份上、我自个儿的份上，我这会儿都不想凑她跟前；可你就是叫我上天入地我也不推：虽说我认得你这么久了，可我要是不像喜欢新朋友一样喜欢你，叫我不得好死。

**哈考特.** 真真领情，亲爱的朋友。我同她要好，只为同你照旧要好；因为一旦拴上了老婆，通常就把朋友的交情全解了。她夜里享用你，我认了；可白天你得归我，像从前一样，亲爱的朋友。

**斯帕基什.** 白天你只管享用我，亲亲的朋友，决不挪窝：跟她离婚，也比跟你散伙早。走罢。

**哈考特.**（旁白）瞧瞧，把情敌当皮条客使，真是被逼到份上了；可眼下她和哥哥都不容我挨近她。说到底，情敌是溜到情人跟前去最好的一件斗篷，不招疑心；等如愿到了她跟前，再把他像旁的斗篷一样甩掉就是。

*（斯帕基什下，哈考特随下。）*

===Original===
*Reenter **Pinchwife** and **Mrs. Pinchwife**.*

Pinchwife
*To **Alithea**.* Sister, if you will not go, we must leave you.—*Aside.* The fool her gallant and she will muster up all the young saunterers of this place, and they will leave their dear sempstresses to follow us. What a swarm of cuckolds and cuckold-makers are here!—Come, let's be gone, Mistress Margery.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Don't you believe that; I han't half my bellyfull of sights yet.

Pinchwife
Then walk this way.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Lord, what a power of brave signs are here! stay—the Bull's-Head, the Ram's-Head, and the Stag's-Head, dear—

Pinchwife
Nay, if every husband's proper sign here were visible, they would be all alike.

===Chinese===
*（**平奇怀夫**与**平奇怀夫太太**又上。）*

**平奇怀夫.**（向阿莉西娅）妹妹，你要是不走，我们只好撇下你了。——（旁白）她那位蠢情人加上她，准会把这儿的闲汉全招拢来，撇下各自亲爱的女裁缝跟咱们跑。瞧这一大窝戴绿帽的和给人戴绿帽的！——走罢，玛格丽小姐。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 你才不信呢；我这点眼福还没饱个零头呢。

**平奇怀夫.** 那就走这边。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 天哪，这儿的招牌可真多、真气派！等等——「公牛头」、「公羊头」，还有「牡鹿头」，亲爱的——

**平奇怀夫.** 嗐，要是每个丈夫本命招牌都挂出来，这满街就都是一个样了。

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
What d'ye mean by that, bud?

Pinchwife
'Tis no matter—no matter, bud.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Pray tell me: nay, I will know.

Pinchwife
They would be all Bulls, Stags, and Rams-heads.

*Exeunt **Pinchwife** and **Mrs. Pinchwife**.*

*Reenter **Sparkish**, **Harcourt**, **Alithea**, and **Lucy**, at the other side.*

Sparkish
Come, dear madam, for my sake you shall be reconciled to him.

Alithea
For your sake I hate him.

Harcourt
That's something too cruel, madam, to hate me for his sake.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 那话怎么讲，乖乖？

**平奇怀夫.** 不打紧——不打紧，乖乖。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 求你告诉我：不成，我非知道不可。

**平奇怀夫.** 那就满街全是公牛头、牡鹿头、公羊头了。

*（平奇怀夫与平奇怀夫太太下。）*

*（**斯帕基什**、**哈考特**、**阿莉西娅**与**露西**从另一侧又上。）*

**斯帕基什.** 来，亲爱的夫人，看在我的份上，你就同他讲和罢。

**阿莉西娅.** 看在你的份上，我才恨他。

**哈考特.** 这就太狠了点，夫人——为他的缘故恨我。

===Original===
Sparkish
Ay indeed, madam, too, too cruel to me, to hate my friend for my sake.

Alithea
I hate him because he is your enemy; and you ought to hate him too, for making love to me, if you love me.

Sparkish
That's a good one! I hate a man for loving you! If he did love you, 'tis but what he can't help; and 'tis your fault, not his, if he admires you. I hate a man for being of my opinion! I'll n'er do't, by the world.

Alithea
Is it for your honour, or mine, to suffer a man to make love to me, who am to marry you tomorrow?

Sparkish
Is it for your honour, or mine, to have me jealous? That he makes love to you, is a sign you are handsome; and that I am not jealous, is a sign you are virtuous. That I think is for your honour.

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 可不是么，夫人，对我太狠、太狠了——为我的缘故恨我的朋友。

**阿莉西娅.** 我恨他，因为他是你的对头；你也该恨他——他向我求爱；你要是爱我。

**斯帕基什.** 这话新鲜！人家爱你，我倒恨人家！他真要爱你，那是他管不住的事；他倾慕你，也是你的错，不是他的。为着跟我一条心倒恨上人家了！打死我也不干，凭全世界起誓。

**阿莉西娅.** 我明天就要嫁你了，却由着一个人向我求爱——这于你的荣誉相干，还是于我的荣誉相干？

**斯帕基什.** 那我吃醋，就于你的荣誉、我的荣誉相干了？他向你求爱，是你标致的凭据；我不吃醋，是你贞洁的凭据。我看这才叫为你的荣誉着想。

===Original===
Alithea
But 'tis your honour too I am concerned for.

Harcourt
But why, dearest madam, will you be more concerned for his honour than he is himself? Let his honour alone, for my sake and his. He! he has no honour—

Sparkish
How's that?

Harcourt
But what my dear friend can guard himself.

Sparkish
O ho—that's right again.

Harcourt
Your care of his honour argues his neglect of it, which is no honour to my dear friend here. Therefore once more, let his honour go which way it will, dear madam.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 可我操心的还有你的荣誉。

**哈考特.** 可最亲爱的夫人，你何苦替他的荣誉操心，比他本人还上心？把他的荣誉放一放罢，看在我的份上，也看在他的份上。嘿！他才没有荣誉——

**斯帕基什.** 什么？

**哈考特.** ——我是说，那是我亲爱的朋友自个儿守得住的。

**斯帕基什.** 哦呵——这又对上了。

**哈考特.** 你这样替他的荣誉操心，正显得他自己不上心，这于我这位亲爱的朋友可没什么光彩。所以再说一遍：他的荣誉爱往哪儿去往哪儿去罢，亲爱的夫人。

===Original===
Sparkish
Ay, ay; were it for my honour to marry a woman whose virtue I suspected, and could not trust her in a friend's hands?

Alithea
Are you not afraid to lose me?

Harcourt
He afraid to lose you, madam! No, no—you may see how the most estimable and most glorious creature in the world is valued by him. Will you not see it?

Sparkish
Right, honest Frank, I have that noble value for her that I cannot be jealous of her.

Alithea
You mistake him. He means, you care not for me, nor who has me.

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 对，对；要是娶一位我疑心贞洁、连朋友的手里都信不过的女人，才于我的荣誉相干呢！

**阿莉西娅.** 你就不怕失掉我？

**哈考特.** 他怕失掉你，夫人！不，不——天下顶可贵、顶光彩的造物在他眼里值几个钱，你亲眼瞧见了。还要瞧么？

**斯帕基什.** 对，老实的弗兰克，我对她怀有的正是那种高贵的器重，器重得没法吃她的醋。

**阿莉西娅.** 你会错他的意了。他是说，你根本不把我放在心上，谁来占都随它去。

===Original===
Sparkish
Lord, madam, I see you are jealous! Will you wrest a poor man's meaning from his words?

Alithea
You astonish me, sir, with your want of jealousy.

Sparkish
And you make me giddy, madam, with your jealousy and fears, and virtue and honour. 'Gad, I see virtue makes a woman as troublesome as a little reading or learning.

Alithea
Monstrous!

Lucy
Well, to see what easy husbands these women of quality can meet with! a poor chambermaid can never have such ladylike luck. Besides, he's thrown away upon her. She'll make no use of her fortune, her blessing, none to a gentleman, for a pure cuckold; for it requires good breeding to be a cuckold. *Aside.*

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 天哪，夫人，我看你是吃醋了！怎么把可怜人的话往歪里掰呢？

**阿莉西娅.** 先生，你这副不吃醋的德行，叫我惊得说不出话。

**斯帕基什.** 夫人，你这份醋劲、这份提心吊胆，还有贞洁呀荣誉呀，也叫我头晕。老天，我看「贞洁」这东西叫女人招人嫌，跟「识几个字」「念过点书」一个样。

**阿莉西娅.** 骇人听闻！

**露西.**（旁白）嗐，瞧瞧这些有身份的太太，撞上的丈夫多省心！穷丫头一辈子也没这样的太太运。再说，他这人是白瞎在她手里了：她那份福气、那份造化，一点也不会便宜正经爷们，全填给一个纯粹的绿帽胚子；要晓得，当绿帽汉也是要讲究家教的。

===Original===
Alithea
I tell you then plainly, he pursues me to marry me.

Sparkish
Pshaw!

Harcourt
Come, madam, you see you strive in vain to make him jealous of me. My dear friend is the kindest creature in the world to me.

Sparkish
Poor fellow!

Harcourt
But his kindness only is not enough for me, without your favour, your good opinion, dear madam: 'tis that must perfect my happiness. Good gentleman, he believes all I say: would you would do so! Jealous of me! I would not wrong him nor you for the world.

Sparkish
Look you there. Hear him, hear him, and do not walk away so. ***Alithea** walks carelessly to and fro.*

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 那我就明说了：他追着我，为的是娶我。

**斯帕基什.** 啐！

**哈考特.** 得了，夫人，你要叫他为我吃醋，白费劲。我亲爱的朋友是天下顶厚道的人。

**斯帕基什.** 可怜的家伙！

**哈考特.** 可光有他的厚道，于我还不够，还得有您的眷顾、您的高看，亲爱的夫人：那才能叫我的福气圆满。多好的一个人，我说什么他信什么：但愿您也这样！吃我的醋！打死我也不会亏待他、亏待您。

**斯帕基什.** 听见没有。听他说，听他说，别走开嘛。（*阿莉西娅**心不在焉地来回踱步。*）

===Original===
Harcourt
I love you, madam, so—

Sparkish
How's that? Nay, now you begin to go too far indeed.

Harcourt
So much, I confess, I say, I love you, that I would not have you miserable, and cast yourself away upon so unworthy and inconsiderable a thing as what you see here. *Clapping his hand on his breast, points at **Sparkish**.*

Sparkish
No, faith, I believe thou wouldst not: now his meaning is plain; but I knew before thou wouldst not wrong me, nor her.

Harcourt
No, no, Heavens forbid the glory of her sex should fall so low, as into the embraces of such a contemptible wretch, the least of mankind—my friend here—I injure him! *Embracing **Sparkish**.*

===Chinese===
**哈考特.** 我爱你，夫人，爱得——

**斯帕基什.** 什么？嗐，这可当真有点过了。

**哈考特.** 爱到这个份上——我认了，我说我爱你爱到，不忍叫你遭殃，把自个儿扔给眼前这么一件不值钱、不上台面的东西。（一手按在自己胸口，指向斯帕基什。）

**斯帕基什.** 对，说真的，我也信你不忍：这下他意思明白了；不过我早知道，你不会亏待我，也不会亏待她。

**哈考特.** 不，不，老天不容她这女性中的光荣跌得这样低，低到投进这么个可怜虫、人类里顶不值一提的——我这朋友——的怀抱——我这是糟践他呀！（拥抱斯帕基什。）

===Original===
Alithea
Very well.

Sparkish
No, no, dear friend, I knew it.—Madam, you see he will rather wrong himself than me, in giving himself such names.

Alithea
Do not you understand him yet?

Sparkish
Yes: how modestly he speaks of himself, poor fellow!

Alithea
Methinks he speaks impudently of yourself, since—before yourself too; insomuch that I can no longer suffer his scurrilous abusiveness to you, no more than his love to me. *Offers to go.*

Sparkish
Nay, nay, madam, pray stay—his love to you! Lord, madam, has he not spoke yet plain enough?

Alithea
Yes, indeed, I should think so.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 好得很。

**斯帕基什.** 不，不，亲爱的朋友，我早知道。——夫人，你瞧，他宁肯糟践自个儿，也不肯糟践我，把这样的名目往自己头上安。

**阿莉西娅.** 你还没听懂他的话么？

**斯帕基什.** 懂了：他说自个儿说得多么谦虚，可怜的家伙！

**阿莉西娅.** 依我看，他说起「您」来倒是放肆得很——还当着「您」本人的面；以致他这种下作的糟践，我再也听不下去了，同他的求爱一样听不下去。（作势欲走。）

**斯帕基什.** 别呀，别呀，夫人，请留下——他对你的爱！天哪，夫人，他说得还不算够明白么？

**阿莉西娅.** 是，实在话，我也这么想。

===Original===
Sparkish
Well then, by the world, a man can't speak civilly to a woman now, but presently she says, he makes love to her. Nay, madam, you shall stay, with your pardon, since you have not yet understood him, till he has made an éclaircissement of his love to you, that is, what kind of love it is. Answer to thy catechism, friend; do you love my mistress here?

Harcourt
Yes, I wish she would not doubt it.

Sparkish
But how do you love her?

Harcourt
With all my soul.

Alithea
I thank him, methinks he speaks plain enough now.

Sparkish
*To **Alithea**.* You are out still.—But with what kind of love, Harcourt?

Harcourt
With the best and the truest love in the world.

Sparkish
Look you there then, that is with no matrimonial love, I'm sure.

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 好罢，凭全世界起誓，如今一个男人对女人客气一句，她马上就说人家向她求爱。不成，夫人，您得留下——您别见怪——既然您还没听懂他，就让他把他的爱跟您「挑明」（éclaircissement）了：是哪一种爱。朋友，回答你的教义问答：你爱我这儿的情人么？

**哈考特.** 爱，但愿她别再疑心。

**斯帕基什.** 可你是怎么个爱法？

**哈考特.** 用我的整个灵魂爱。

**阿莉西娅.** 承他的情，我看这回他说得够明白了。

**斯帕基什.**（向阿莉西娅）你还是没懂。——是哪一种爱，哈考特？

**哈考特.** 用天底下顶好、顶真的爱。

**斯帕基什.** 这不结了，我拿得准，那决不是「婚姻的爱」。

===Original===
Alithea
How's that? do you say matrimonial love is not best?

Sparkish
'Gad, I went too far ere I was aware. But speak for thyself, Harcourt, you said you would not wrong me nor her.

Harcourt
No, so, madam, e'en take him for Heaven's sake.

Sparkish
Look you there, madam.

Harcourt
Who should in all justice be yours, he that loves you most. *Claps his hand on his breast.*

Alithea
Look you there, Mr. Sparkish, who's that?

Sparkish
Who should it be?—Go on, Harcourt.

Harcourt
Who loves you more than women titles, or fortune fools. *Points at **Sparkish**.*

Sparkish
Look you there, he means me still, for he points at me.

Alithea
Ridiculous!

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 什么？你是说婚姻的爱不是顶好的爱？

**斯帕基什.** 老天，不留神又说漏了。你替自个儿说罢，哈考特，你说过你不亏待我、也不亏待她的。

**哈考特.** 正是为这个，夫人，看老天爷的份上，收下他罢。

**斯帕基什.** 听见没有，夫人。

**哈考特.** 天公地道，谁最爱你，谁才该归你。（一手按在胸口。）

**阿莉西娅.** 听见了么，斯帕基什先生，那是谁？

**斯帕基什.** 还能是谁？——接着说，哈考特。

**哈考特.** 是那个爱你赛过女人爱头衔、傻瓜爱钱财的人。（指向斯帕基什。）

**斯帕基什.** 瞧见没，他指的还是我——他冲我指呢。

**阿莉西娅.** 荒唐！

===Original===
Harcourt
Who can only match your faith and constancy in love.

Sparkish
Ay.

Harcourt
Who knows, if it be possible, how to value so much beauty and virtue.

Sparkish
Ay.

Harcourt
Whose love can no more be equalled in the world, than that heavenly form of yours.

Sparkish
No.

Harcourt
Who could no more suffer a rival, than your absence, and yet could no more suspect your virtue, than his own constancy in his love to you.

Sparkish
No.

Harcourt
Who, in fine, loves you better than his eyes, that first made him love you.

Sparkish
Ay—Nay, madam, faith, you shan't go till—

Alithea
Have a care, lest you make me stay too long.

Sparkish
But till he has saluted you; that I may be assured you are friends, after his honest advice and declaration. Come, pray, madam, be friends with him.

===Chinese===
**哈考特.** 是唯一配得上你的信义和坚贞的人。

**斯帕基什.** 对。

**哈考特.** 是那个懂得——只要人力做得到——怎样珍重这许多美貌与贞洁的人。

**斯帕基什.** 对。

**哈考特.** 是那份举世无双的爱，正如你那天仙般的身段。

**斯帕基什.** 没错。

**哈考特.** 是那个人：他容不下情敌，如同容不下与你分离；他疑你的贞洁，也决不多疑于他自个儿对你的坚贞。

**斯帕基什.** 没错。

**哈考特.** 总而言之，是那个比爱自己的眼睛更爱你的人——正是那双眼睛，最先叫他爱上了你。

**斯帕基什.** 对——别忙，夫人，说真的，你走不得，除非——

**阿莉西娅.** 留神，别留得我叫你后悔。

**斯帕基什.** 除非让他吻你一下；这样他出了这番忠告、这番表白之后，我好拿得准你们成了朋友。来，求你了，夫人，同他讲和罢。

===Original===
*Reenter **Pinchwife** and **Mrs. Pinchwife**.*

Alithea
You must pardon me, sir, that I am not yet so obedient to you.

Pinchwife
What, invite your wife to kiss men? Monstrous! are you not ashamed? I will never forgive you.

Sparkish
Are you not ashamed, that I should have more confidence in the chastity of your family than you have? You must not teach me, I am a man of honour, sir, though I am frank and free; I am frank, sir—

Pinchwife
Very frank, sir, to share your wife with your friends.

Sparkish
He is an humble, menial friend, such as reconciles the differences of the marriage bed; you know man and wife do not always agree; I design him for that use, therefore would have him well with my wife.

Pinchwife
A menial friend!—you will get a great many menial friends, by showing your wife as you do.

===Chinese===
*（**平奇怀夫**与**平奇怀夫太太**又上。）*

**阿莉西娅.** 请您恕罪，先生，我还没顺从到那个地步。

**平奇怀夫.** 什么，请你的老婆亲爷们？无法无天！你不害臊么？我永远不依你。

**斯帕基什.** 该害臊的是你——我对你们家门风的贞洁，比你还信得过！轮不到你来教我，先生，我是讲荣誉的人，虽然我爽朗大方；我爽朗着呢，先生——

**平奇怀夫.** 爽朗得很，先生——把老婆分给朋友们共享。

**斯帕基什.** 他是位低声下气的仆役朋友，专管调和婚床上的纠纷；你知道两口子未必事事投机；我就是派他这个用场的，所以要他同我老婆和睦。

**平奇怀夫.** 仆役朋友！——照你这么展览老婆，这样的仆役朋友你要多少有多少。

===Original===
Sparkish
What then? It may be I have a pleasure in't, as I have to show fine clothes at a playhouse, the first day, and count money before poor rogues.

Pinchwife
He that shows his wife or money, will be in danger of having them borrowed sometimes.

Sparkish
I love to be envied, and would not marry a wife that I alone could love; loving alone is as dull as eating alone. Is it not a frank age? and I am a frank person; and to tell you the truth, it may be, I love to have rivals in a wife, they make her seem to a man still but as a kept mistress; and so good night, for I must to Whitehall.—Madam, I hope you are now reconciled to my friend; and so I wish you a good night, madam, and sleep if you can: for tomorrow you know I must visit you early with a canonical gentleman. Good night, dear Harcourt.

*Exit.*

Harcourt
Madam, I hope you will not refuse my visit tomorrow, if it should be earlier with a canonical gentleman than Mr. Sparkish's?

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 那又怎样？我兴许就以此为乐呢，就像头一天穿新衣裳上戏园子、当着穷光蛋数钱一样。

**平奇怀夫.** 谁展览老婆、露白钱财，就保不齐哪天叫人「借」走。

**斯帕基什.** 我就爱叫人眼红，娶老婆也断不肯娶个只有我一个人爱得起的；一个人爱，跟一个人吃饭一样乏味。这难道不是个爽朗的时代？我又是爽朗人；跟你说实话也行：我就爱老婆身边围几个情敌，他们叫她在一个男人眼里，永远像个养着的情妇；好了，晚安，我得去白厅。——夫人，我希望您这会儿同我朋友讲和了；那么祝您晚安，夫人，睡得着就睡：您知道，明天一早我带着位穿法衣的先生（canonical gentleman）就要登门了。晚安，亲爱的哈考特。

*（下。）*

**哈考特.** 夫人，要是我明天带着位穿法衣的先生登门，比斯帕基什先生还早一步，您该不会闭门不纳罢？

===Original===
Pinchwife
This gentlewoman is yet under my care, therefore you must yet forbear your freedom with her, sir. *Coming between **Alithea** and **Harcourt**.*

Harcourt
Must, sir?

Pinchwife
Yes, sir, she is my sister.

Harcourt
'Tis well she is, sir—for I must be her servant, sir.—Madam—

Pinchwife
Come away, sister, we had been gone, if it had not been for you, and so avoided these lewd rake-hells, who seem to haunt us.

*Reenter **Horner** and **Dorilant**.*

Horner
How now, Pinchwife!

Pinchwife
Your servant.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 这位小姐还在我的监护之下，所以你对她还放肆不得，先生。（横身挡在阿莉西娅与哈考特之间。）

**哈考特.** 放肆不得，先生？

**平奇怀夫.** 是的，先生，她是我妹妹。

**哈考特.** 幸亏她是，先生——因为我非当她的「仆人」不可，先生。——夫人——

**平奇怀夫.** 走罢，妹妹，若不是等你，我们早走了，也就躲开了这帮放荡的泼皮——他们像是缠上我们了。

*（**霍纳**与**多里兰特**又上。）*

**霍纳.** 喂，平奇怀夫！

**平奇怀夫.** 您安。

===Original===
Horner
What! I see a little time in the country makes a man turn wild and unsociable, and only fit to converse with his horses, dogs, and his herds.

Pinchwife
I have business, sir, and must mind it; your business is pleasure, therefore you and I must go different ways.

Horner
Well, you may go on, but this pretty young gentleman—*Takes hold of **Mrs. Pinchwife**.*

Harcourt
The lady—

Dorilant
And the maid—

Horner
Shall stay with us; for I suppose their business is the same with ours, pleasure.

Pinchwife
'Sdeath, he knows her, she carries it so sillily! yet if he does not, I should be more silly to discover it first. *Aside.*

Alithea
Pray, let us go, sir.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 怎么！依我看，在乡下待上一阵子，人就变野了，不合群了，只配同他的马呀、狗呀、牛羊呀做伴。

**平奇怀夫.** 我有正事，先生，得操持；您的正事是行乐，所以你我只好各走各路。

**霍纳.** 好罢，你只管走，可这位俊俏的小相公——（拉住平奇怀夫太太。）

**哈考特.** 这位小姐——

**多里兰特.** 还有这位大姐——

**霍纳.** 要留下来陪我们；我料她们的正事同我们一样——行乐。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）该死，他认出她了，瞧她那副憨样！可他就算没认出来，我先自揭穿，那就更憨了。

**阿莉西娅.** 求您放我们走，先生。

===Original===
Pinchwife
Come, come—

Horner
*To **Mrs. Pinchwife**.* Had you not rather stay with us?—Prithee, Pinchwife, who is this pretty young gentleman?

Pinchwife
One to whom I'm a guardian.—*Aside.* I wish I could keep her out of your hands.

Horner
Who is he? I never saw anything so pretty in all my life.

Pinchwife
Pshaw! do not look upon him so much, he's a poor bashful youth, you'll put him out of countenance.—Come away, brother. *Offers to take her away.*

Horner
O, your brother!

Pinchwife
Yes, my wife's brother.—Come, come, she'll stay supper for us.

Horner
I thought so, for he is very like her I saw you at the play with, whom I told you I was in love with.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 走罢，走罢——

**霍纳.**（向平奇怀夫太太）您不想留下来陪我们么？——喂，平奇怀夫，这位俊俏的小相公是谁呀？

**平奇怀夫.** 我当监护人的一位。——（旁白）但愿我能把她从你手里保住。

**霍纳.** 他是谁？我平生没见过这么俊的。

**平奇怀夫.** 啐！别这么盯着他瞧，他是个脸嫩的穷后生，你要叫他挂不住了。——走罢，弟弟。（作势要拉她走。）

**霍纳.** 哦，是你小舅子！

**平奇怀夫.** 是，我老婆的弟弟。——走罢，走罢，她还要等我们回去吃晚饭呢。

**霍纳.** 我就说呢，他真像我那天在戏园子瞧见你身边那位——我不是说过我爱上她了么。

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
*Aside.* O jeminy! is that he that was in love with me? I am glad on't, I vow, for he's a curious fine gentleman, and I love him already, too.—*To **Pinchwife**.* Is this he, bud?

Pinchwife
Come away, come away. *To his **Wife**.*

Horner
Why, what haste are you in? why won't you let me talk with him?

Pinchwife
Because you'll debauch him; he's yet young and innocent, and I would not have him debauched for anything in the world.—*Aside.* How she gazes on him! the devil!

Horner
Harcourt, Dorilant, look you here, this is the likeness of that dowdy he told us of, his wife; did you ever see a lovelier creature? The rogue has reason to be jealous of his wife, since she is like him, for she would make all that see her in love with her.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.**（旁白）哎呀妈呀！这就是那位爱我的公子爷么？说真的，我可乐意了，他可真是个讲究的俊公子，这会儿我已经也爱上他啦。——（向平奇怀夫）就是他么，乖乖？

**平奇怀夫.** 走开，走开。（对太太。）

**霍纳.** 嗐，急什么？怎么不让我同他聊聊？

**平奇怀夫.** 因为你要勾坏他；他还年轻、还天真，天塌下来我也不能让他学坏。——（旁白）瞧她盯着他看的那个劲儿！见了鬼！

**霍纳.** 哈考特，多里兰特，你们瞧，这就是他念叨的那位黄脸婆的翻版——他老婆；见过这么可爱的人儿没有？这小子有理由看住他老婆：她既长得像他这份模样，见着她的人，保管个个都要爱上她。

===Original===
Harcourt
And, as I remember now, she is as like him here as can be.

Dorilant
She is indeed very pretty, if she be like him.

Horner
Very pretty? a very pretty commendation!—she is a glorious creature, beautiful beyond all things I ever beheld.

Pinchwife
So, so.

Harcourt
More beautiful than a poet's first mistress of imagination.

Horner
Or another man's last mistress of flesh and blood.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Nay, now you jeer, sir; pray don't jeer me.

Pinchwife
Come, come.—*Aside.* By Heavens, she'll discover herself!

===Chinese===
**哈考特.** 这么一提我也想起来了，她跟他眼前这位简直像得不能再像。

**多里兰特.** 她要真长他这样，那确实标致。

**霍纳.** 「确实标致」？这夸的可真大方！——她是件光彩夺目的造物，美得叫我生平所见都黯然失色。

**平奇怀夫.** 好了，好了。

**哈考特.** 比诗人想象中的初恋情人还美。

**霍纳.** 也比旁人血肉做的压轴情人美。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 嗐，先生，你们这是打趣了；可别拿我逗乐。

**平奇怀夫.** 走罢，走罢。——（旁白）皇天在上，她要自曝了！

===Original===
Horner
I speak of your sister, sir.

Pinchwife
Ay, but saying she was handsome, if like him, made him blush.—*Aside.* I am upon a rack!

Horner
Methinks he is so handsome he should not be a man.

Pinchwife
*Aside.* O, there 'tis out! he has discovered her! I am not able to suffer any longer.—*To his **Wife**.* Come, come away, I say.

Horner
Nay, by your leave, sir, he shall not go yet.—*Aside to them.* Harcourt, Dorilant, let us torment this jealous rogue a little.

Harcourt and Dorilant
How?

Horner
I'll show you.

Pinchwife
Come, pray let him go, I cannot stay fooling any longer; I tell you his sister stays supper for us.

Horner
Does she? Come then, we'll all go to sup with he and thee.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 我说的是你姐姐，先生。

**平奇怀夫.** 是，可说姐姐标致、又像他，臊得他脸都红了。——（旁白）我这心里跟上了刑架一样！

**霍纳.** 依我看，他俊得都不该是个男人。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）坏了，露馅了！他认出她了！我一刻也忍不下去了。——（对太太）走，这就走，我说。

**霍纳.** 慢来，恕我直言，先生，他还走不得。——（向他们旁白）哈考特，多里兰特，咱们来消遣消遣这个醋坛子。

**哈考特和多里兰特.** 怎么消遣？

**霍纳.** 看我的。

**平奇怀夫.** 行行好放他走罢，我没工夫再胡闹了；我跟你们说，他姐姐还等着我们吃晚饭呢。

**霍纳.** 是么？那好，我们都上你俩那儿吃去。

===Original===
Pinchwife
No, now I think on't, having stayed so long for us, I warrant she's gone to bed.—*Aside.* I wish she and I were well out of their hands.—*To his **Wife**.* Come, I must rise early tomorrow, come.

Horner
Well then, if she be gone to bed, I wish her and you a good night. But pray, young gentleman, present my humble service to her.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Thank you heartily, sir.

Pinchwife
*Aside.* 'Sdeath, she will discover herself yet in spite of me—*Aloud.* He is something more civil to you, for your kindness to his sister, than I am, it seems.

Horner
Tell her, dear sweet little gentleman, for all your brother there, that you have revived the love I had for her at first sight in the playhouse.

Mrs. Pinchwife
But did you love her indeed, and indeed?

Pinchwife
*Aside.* So, so.—*Aloud.* Away, I say.

Horner
Nay, stay.—Yes, indeed, and indeed, pray do you tell her so, and give her this kiss from me. *Kisses her.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 不成了，这会儿想起来，等了这么久，她保管睡下了。——（旁白）但愿她和我平安脱手。——（对太太）走，我明天还得早起，走。

**霍纳.** 好罢，她既睡下了，我祝她和你晚安。不过请托一声，小相公，替我向她致以卑微的敬意。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 多谢多谢，先生。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）该死，她还是要自曝，拦都拦不住——（高声）看样子，他承你照看他姐姐的情，倒比你哥哥我客气些呢。

**霍纳.** 亲爱的小甜相公，别管你哥哥在这儿，替我告诉她：你叫我死灰复燃了——我在戏园子里对她一见钟情的那份爱。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 可你当真爱她？当真的、当真的？

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）糟了，糟了。——（高声）走，我说。

**霍纳.** 别忙。——是，当真，千真万确；劳驾你就这么告诉她，再替我把这个吻捎给她。（吻她。）

===Original===
Pinchwife
*Aside.* O Heavens! what do I suffer? Now 'tis too plain he knows her, and yet—

Horner
And this, and this—*Kisses her again.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
What do you kiss me for? I am no woman.

Pinchwife
*Aside.* So, there, 'tis out.—*Aloud.* Come, I cannot, nor will stay any longer.

Horner
Nay, they shall send your lady a kiss too. Here, Harcourt, Dorilant, will you not? *They kiss her.*

Pinchwife
*Aside.* How! do I suffer this? Was I not accusing another just now for this rascally patience, in permitting his wife to be kissed before his face? Ten thousand ulcers gnaw away their lips.—*Aloud.* Come, come.

Horner
Good night, dear little gentleman; madam, good night: farewell, Pinchwife.—*Apart to **Harcourt** and **Dorilant**.* Did not I tell you I would raise his jealous gall?

*Exeunt **Horner**, **Harcourt** and **Dorilant**.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）老天爷！我受的这是什么罪！这下太明白了，他认得她，可——

**霍纳.** 还有这个，这个——（又吻她。）

**平奇怀夫太太.** 你亲我做什么？我又不是女人。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）得，露了。——（高声）走，我再也、再也不待了。

**霍纳.** 别忙，也得叫他们给你家太太捎个吻。来，哈考特，多里兰特，来不来？（二人吻她。）

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）什么！我竟受着这个？方才我不是还在数落别人这副没出息的忍性——眼看老婆当着自个儿的面叫人亲？叫一万个毒疮啃烂他们的嘴唇。——（高声）走，走。

**霍纳.** 晚安，亲爱的小相公；小姐，晚安；再会，平奇怀夫。——（向哈考特、多里兰特旁白）我说要勾出他一肚子醋火，没说错罢？

*（霍纳、哈考特、多里兰特下。）*

===Original===
Pinchwife
So, they are gone at last; stay, let me see first if the coach be at this door.

*Exit.*

*Reenter **Horner**, **Harcourt**, and **Dorilant**.*

Horner
What, not gone yet? Will you be sure to do as I desired you, sweet sir?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Sweet sir, but what will you give me then?

Horner
Anything. Come away into the next walk.

*Exit, haling away **Mrs. Pinchwife**.*

Alithea
Hold! hold! what d'ye do?

Lucy
Stay, stay, hold—

Harcourt
Hold, madam, hold, let him present him—he'll come presently; nay, I will never let you go till you answer my question.

Lucy
For God's sake, sir, I must follow 'em. ***Alithea** and **Lucy**, struggling with **Harcourt** and **Dorilant**.*

Dorilant
No, I have something to present you with too, you shan't follow them.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 好，他们总算走了；慢着，先瞧瞧马车在不在这门口。

*（下。）*

*（**霍纳**、**哈考特**与**多里兰特**又上。）*

**霍纳.** 怎么，还没走？您拿得准照我托的办么，甜甜的小相公？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 甜甜的先生，那您赏我点什么呢？

**霍纳.** 要什么给什么。跟我到旁边那条道上来说。

*（下，硬拽走平奇怀夫太太。）*

**阿莉西娅.** 住手！住手！你们干什么？

**露西.** 拦住，拦住，快拦——

**哈考特.** 别急，夫人，别急，由他去给他赠礼——他马上回来；不，你不回答我的问题，我决不放你走。

**露西.** 看上帝份上，先生，我非得跟上他们不可。（*阿莉西娅**与**露西**挣扎，与**哈考特**、**多里兰特**拉扯。*）

**多里兰特.** 不成，我也有东西要赠你呢，你跟不了他们。

===Original===
*Reenter **Pinchwife**.*

Pinchwife
Where?—how—what's become of?—gone!—whither?

Lucy
He's only gone with the gentleman, who will give him something, an't please your worship.

Pinchwife
Something!—give him something, with a pox!—where are they?

Alithea
In the next walk only, brother.

Pinchwife
Only, only! where, where?

*Exit and returns presently, then goes out again.*

Harcourt
What's the matter with him? why so much concerned? But, dearest madam—

Alithea
Pray let me go, sir; I have said and suffered enough already.

Harcourt
Then you will not look upon, nor pity, my sufferings?

Alithea
To look upon 'em, when I cannot help 'em, were cruelty, not pity; therefore, I will never see you more.

===Chinese===
*（**平奇怀夫**又上。）*

**平奇怀夫.** 人呢？——怎么——到哪儿去了？——没了！——去哪儿了？

**露西.** 回您的话，他不过同那位先生去了，先生要赏他点东西，您大人大量别见怪。

**平奇怀夫.** 东西！——赏他点东西，遭瘟的！——人在哪儿？

**阿莉西娅.** 就在旁边那条道上，哥哥。

**平奇怀夫.** 就在，就在！哪儿，哪儿？

*（下，旋即折回，复又奔出。）*

**哈考特.** 他这是怎么了？急成这样？可是，最亲爱的夫人——

**阿莉西娅.** 求您放我走，先生；我说够也受够了。

**哈考特.** 那么我的苦楚，您也不看一眼、不怜一丝了？

**阿莉西娅.** 帮不上忙却要看，那是残忍，不是怜悯；所以，我永远不要再见到你。

===Original===
Harcourt
Let me then, madam, have my privilege of a banished lover, complaining or railing, and giving you but a farewell reason why, if you cannot condescend to marry me, you should not take that wretch, my rival.

Alithea
He only, not you, since my honour is engaged so far to him, can give me a reason why I should not marry him; but if he be true, and what I think him to me, I must be so to him. Your servant, sir.

Harcourt
Have women only constancy when 'tis a vice, and are, like Fortune, only true to fools?

Dorilant
Thou sha't not stir, thou robust creature; you see I can deal with you, therefore you should stay the rather, and be kind. *To **Lucy**, who struggles to get from him.*

*Reenter **Pinchwife**.*

Pinchwife
Gone, gone, not to be found! quite gone! ten thousand plagues go with 'em! Which way went they?

===Chinese===
**哈考特.** 那就请夫人赐我一个流放情人的特权：许我抱怨，许我骂，只求临别赠你一句忠告——你既不肯屈尊嫁我，就千万别收那个可怜虫，我的情敌。

**阿莉西娅.** 能给我一个不嫁他的理由的，只有他本人，不是你——我的名节已经在他身上押了这么多；可他若待我如我所信的那样真，我也必同样待他。失陪了，先生。

**哈考特.** 莫非女人的坚贞只在它是恶德时才有？莫非她们像命运女神，只对傻子忠实？

**多里兰特.** 你一步也挪不得，你这健壮的小东西；瞧，我制得住你，所以你更该留下，乖一点。（对露西说；她挣扎着要脱身。）

*（**平奇怀夫**又上。）*

**平奇怀夫.** 没了，没了，找不着了！彻底没了！叫一万场瘟疫追着他们去！往哪边走了？

===Original===
Alithea
But into t'other walk, brother.

Lucy
Their business will be done presently sure, an't please your worship; it can't be long in doing, I'm sure on't.

Alithea
Are they not there?

Pinchwife
No, you know where they are, you infamous wretch, eternal shame of your family, which you do not dishonour enough yourself you think, but you must help her to do it too, thou legion of bawds!

Alithea
Good brother—

Pinchwife
Damned, damned sister!

Alithea
Look you here, she's coming.

*Reenter **Mrs. Pinchwife** running, with her hat full of oranges and dried fruit under her arm, **Horner** following.*

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 就在旁边那条道上，哥哥。

**露西.** 回您的话，他们的事这会儿保管就办完了；快得很，我拿得准。

**阿莉西娅.** 他们不在那儿？

**平奇怀夫.** 不在——你们知道他们在哪儿，你这不要脸的东西，你们家永远的耻辱：你自己丢这份人还嫌不够，还要帮她一块儿丢，你这成群结队的老鸨！

**阿莉西娅.** 好哥哥——

**平奇怀夫.** 该死的、该死的妹妹！

**阿莉西娅.** 你瞧，她这不是回来了。

*（**平奇怀夫太太**奔上，帽兜里满是橙子，胳膊下夹着果干；**霍纳**随后。）*

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
O dear bud, look you here what I have got, see!

Pinchwife
And what I have got here too, which you can't see! *Aside, rubbing his forehead.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
The fine gentleman has given me better things yet.

Pinchwife
Has he so?—*Aside.* Out of breath and coloured!—I must hold yet.

Horner
I have only given your little brother an orange, sir.

Pinchwife
*To **Horner**.* Thank you, sir.—*Aside.* You have only squeezed my orange, I suppose, and given it me again; yet I must have a city patience.—*To his **Wife**.* Come, come away.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Stay, till I have put up my fine things, bud.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 哎呀亲乖乖，你瞧瞧我捞着了什么，看！

**平奇怀夫.** 我这儿也有一样捞着的，你可瞧不见！（旁白，揉着额头。）

**平奇怀夫太太.** 那位俊公子还赏了我更好的东西呢。

**平奇怀夫.** 是么？——（旁白）气喘吁吁，脸红扑扑！——我还得忍。

**霍纳.** 我不过给了令弟一个橙子，先生。

**平奇怀夫.**（向霍纳）多谢，先生。——（旁白）想来你不过把我的橙子挤了，再还给我罢了；可我还得有城里人的耐性。——（对太太）走，这就走。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 等等，让我把好东西收拾起来，乖乖。

===Original===
*Enter **Sir Jasper Fidget**.*

Sir Jasper
O, Master Horner, come, come, the ladies stay for you; your mistress, my wife, wonders you make not more haste to her.

Horner
I have stayed this half hour for you here, and 'tis your fault I am not now with your wife.

Sir Jasper
But, pray, don't let her know so much; the truth on't is, I was advancing a certain project to his majesty about—I'll tell you.

Horner
No, let's go, and hear it at your house. Good night, sweet little gentleman; one kiss more, you'll remember me now, I hope. *Kisses her.*

Dorilant
What, Sir Jasper, will you separate friends? He promised to sup with us, and if you take him to your house, you'll be in danger of our company too.

Sir Jasper
Alas! gentlemen, my house is not fit for you; there are none but civil women there, which are not for your turn. He, you know, can bear with the society of civil women now, ha! ha! ha! besides, he's one of my family—he's—he! he! he!

Dorilant
What is he?

Sir Jasper
Faith, my eunuch, since you'll have it; he! he! he!

*Exeunt **Sir Jasper Fidget** and **Horner**.*

===Chinese===
*（**贾斯珀·菲吉特爵士**上。）*

**贾斯珀爵士.** 哦，霍纳先生，快走快走，太太们等着您呢；您的情人、我那位太太，直纳闷您怎么还不赶紧去她那儿。

**霍纳.** 我在这儿等了你半个钟头；我这会儿没在你太太身边，全怪你。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 可千万别叫她知道这个；实情是，我正为陛下一桩什么计划进言——我告诉你。

**霍纳.** 不必，路上去府上再听。晚安，甜甜的小相公；再亲一个，这回你总该记住我了吧，我希望。（吻她。）

**多里兰特.** 怎么，贾斯珀爵士，您要拆散朋友？他答应同我们吃夜宵的；您要是把他带回府上，我们这伙人也要赖上您家了。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 哎呀！先生们，寒舍可不敢屈尊二位；家里只有规矩太太们，不合二位的口味。他嘛，你们知道，如今受得了规矩太太们的交际了，哈！哈！哈！再说，他算是我们家里的一员了——他是——嘿！嘿！嘿！

**多里兰特.** 他是哪一号？

**贾斯珀爵士.** 说真的，我的阉人，既然你非要问；嘿！嘿！嘿！

*（贾斯珀·菲吉特爵士与霍纳同下。）*

===Original===
Dorilant
I rather wish thou wert his or my cuckold. Harcourt, what a good cuckold is lost there for want of a man to make him one? Thee and I cannot have Horner's privilege, who can make use of it.

Harcourt
Ay, to poor Horner 'tis like coming to an estate at threescore, when a man can't be the better for't.

Pinchwife
Come.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Presently, bud.

Dorilant
Come, let us go too.—*To **Alithea**.* Madam, your servant.—*To **Lucy**.* Good night, strapper.

Harcourt
Madam, though you will not let me have a good day or night, I wish you one; but dare not name the other half of my wish.

Alithea
Good night, sir, forever.

===Chinese===
**多里兰特.** 我倒宁愿你是他或者我的绿帽汉呢。哈考特，瞧那儿糟蹋了一个多好的绿帽坯子——只差个成全他的人！你我享不到霍纳那份特权，他享得了。

**哈考特.** 是啊，对可怜的霍纳，那就像六十岁上继承一笔家产——有福享不了。

**平奇怀夫.** 走。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 这就来，乖乖。

**多里兰特.** 走，咱们也走。——（向阿莉西娅）夫人，告辞。——（向露西）晚安，壮丫头。

**哈考特.** 夫人，您虽不许我有好日子、好夜晚，我却祝您有；只是我愿望的另一半，不敢说出口。

**阿莉西娅.** 晚安，先生，永远地晚安了。

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
I don't know where to put this here, dear bud, you shall eat it; nay, you shall have part of the fine gentleman's good things, or treat, as you call it, when we come home.

Pinchwife
Indeed, I deserve it, since I furnished the best part of it. *Strikes away the orange.*

The gallant treats presents, and gives the ball;
But 'tis the absent cuckold pays for all.

*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 这玩意儿我没处搁，亲乖乖，你吃了吧；不成，回头到了家，那俊公子的好东西、你说的什么「请客」，你也得有一份。

**平奇怀夫.** 说真的，我受之无愧——顶好的那一半还是我供应的呢。（把橙子打掉。）

公子爷设宴赠礼又开舞，
掏腰包的却是缺席的绿帽夫。

*（同下。）*

## Act IV / 第四幕

### Scene I / 第一场

===Original===
**Pinchwife’s** house in the morning.

*Enter **Alithea** dressed in new clothes, and **Lucy**.*

Lucy
Well—madam, now have I dressed you, and set you out with so many ornaments, and spent upon you ounces of essence and pulvillio;8 and all this for no other purpose but as people adorn and perfume a corpse for a stinking secondhand grave: such, or as bad, I think Master Sparkish’s bed.

Alithea
Hold your peace.

Lucy
Nay, madam, I will ask you the reason why you would banish poor Master Harcourt forever from your sight; how could you be so hard-hearted?

Alithea
’Twas because I was not hard-hearted.

Lucy
No, no; ’twas stark love and kindness, I warrant.

===Chinese===
早晨，平奇怀夫家。

*（**阿莉西娅**着新装与**露西**上。）*

**露西.** 好了——夫人，如今我总算把您装扮停当了，周身插戴了这许多首饰，还往您身上使了好几盎司的香精香粉8；可这一切图的什么呢？不过像人们妆点一具尸首、给它熏香，好送进一座发臭的二手坟：斯帕基什先生的床，依我看，就是这样的坟，或者一样地糟。

**阿莉西娅.** 住嘴。

**露西.** 不成，夫人，我倒要问问您，为什么要把可怜的哈考特先生永远逐出您的眼皮；您怎么下得去这个狠心？

**阿莉西娅.** 正因为我下不去狠心。

**露西.** 是啊，是啊；保管是十足的恩爱情分。

===Original===
Alithea
It was so; I would see him no more because I love him.

Lucy
Hey day, a very pretty reason!

Alithea
You do not understand me.

Lucy
I wish you may yourself.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 正是；就因为我爱他，才不再见他。

**露西.** 嘿，好一个漂亮的理由！

**阿莉西娅.** 你没懂我的意思。

**露西.** 但愿您自个儿懂。

===Original===
Alithea
I was engaged to marry, you see, another man, whom my justice will not suffer me to deceive or injure.

Lucy
Can there be a greater cheat or wrong done to a man than to give him your person without your heart? I should make a conscience of it.

Alithea
I’ll retrieve it for him after I am married a while.

Lucy
The woman that marries to love better, will be as much mistaken as the wencher that marries to live better. No, madam, marrying to increase love is like gaming to become rich; alas! you only lose what little stock you had before.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 你瞧，我已许给了另一个人；我的公道心容不得我欺骗他、亏负他。

**露西.** 把身子给人、心却留着不给，天下还有比这更欺人、更亏心的么？换了我要当作良心上一桩亏欠。

**阿莉西娅.** 等我嫁过去些时日，再替他把心收回来给他。

**露西.** 指望越嫁越爱而嫁的女人，同指望越娶越好活而娶的嫖客，一样要大失所望。不，夫人，指望嫁人来添情，就像指望赌钱来发财；唉！原本那一点点本钱，反倒要赔个精光。

===Original===
Alithea
I find by your rhetoric you have been bribed to betray me.

Lucy
Only by his merit, that has bribed your heart, you see, against your word and rigid honour. But what a devil is this honour! ’tis sure a disease in the head, like the megrim or falling-sickness, that always hurries people away to do themselves mischief. Men lose their lives by it; women, what’s dearer to ’em, their love, the life of life.

Alithea
Come, pray talk you no more of honour, nor Master Harcourt; I wish the other would come to secure my fidelity to him and his right in me.

Lucy
You will marry him then?

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 听你这番口才，你是叫人贿买了，来出卖我。

**露西.** 贿我的不过是他的人品——就是他，您瞧，也贿动了您的心，叫您背了诺言、撇了那铁面的荣誉。可这「荣誉」到底是个什么鬼！它保管是脑袋里的一种病，像偏头痛、羊癫疯，一味撺掇人去自讨苦吃。男人为它送命；女人为它赔上更心疼的东西——爱情，命根子里的命根子。

**阿莉西娅.** 得了，求你别再提荣誉，也别再提哈考特先生；我巴望那一位快来，好坐实我对他的坚贞、他对我的名分。

**露西.** 那么您是要嫁他了？

===Original===
Alithea
Certainly, I have given him already my word, and will my hand too, to make it good, when he comes.

Lucy
Well, I wish I may never stick pin more, if he be not an arrant natural, to t’other fine gentleman.

Alithea
I own he wants the wit of Harcourt, which I will dispense withal for another want he has, which is want of jealousy, which men of wit seldom want.

Lucy
Lord, madam, what should you do with a fool to your husband? You intend to be honest, don’t you? then that husbandly virtue, credulity, is thrown away upon you.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 当然；话我已经给了他，等他来，手再给他，好说话作数。

**露西.** 嗐，比起那另一位俊公子来，他若不是个十足的低能儿，叫我从此一辈子别不上别针。

**阿莉西娅.** 我承认他缺哈考特那份机智；可这份「缺」我甘愿将就，因为他另有一「缺」——缺醋劲，而机智男人难得缺这个。

**露西.** 天哪，夫人，您要个傻瓜当丈夫做什么？您是打算守规矩的，是不是？那么「做丈夫的美德」——轻信——在您身上可就白瞎了。

===Original===
Alithea
He only that could suspect my virtue should have cause to do it; ’tis Sparkish’s confidence in my truth that obliges me to be so faithful to him.

Lucy
You are not sure his opinion may last.

Alithea
I am satisfied, ’tis impossible for him to be jealous after the proofs I have had of him. Jealousy in a husband—Heaven defend me from it! it begets a thousand plagues to a poor woman, the loss of her honour, her quiet, and her—

Lucy
And her pleasure.

Alithea
What d’ye mean, impertinent?

Lucy
Liberty is a great pleasure, madam.

Alithea
I say, loss of her honour, her quiet, nay, her life sometimes; and what’s as bad almost, the loss of this town; that is, she is sent into the country, which is the last ill-usage of a husband to a wife, I think.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 只有疑心我贞洁的人，才配叫这份疑心有着落；正是斯帕基什信得过我的操守，我才不能不守这份信义。

**露西.** 您可拿不准他这份见识别撑得长久。

**阿莉西娅.** 我放心得很；凭我在他身上见过的那些凭据，他要吃醋，断无可能。丈夫吃醋——老天保佑我躲开它！它给一个可怜女人添一千桩祸：叫她失了荣誉、失了安宁，还失了她的——

**露西.** 还有她的乐子。

**阿莉西娅.** 什么意思，多嘴的？

**露西.** 自由可是一大乐子，夫人。

**阿莉西娅.** 我说的是：失了荣誉、失了安宁，有时连性命也失了；还有一样几乎同样糟——失了这座城；也就是说，给打发下乡去；我看，这是丈夫虐待妻子最毒的一手。

===Original===
Lucy
*Aside.* O, does the wind lie there?—*Aloud.* Then of necessity, madam, you think a man must carry his wife into the country, if he be wise. The country is as terrible, I find, to our young English ladies, as a monastery to those abroad; and on my virginity, I think they would rather marry a London jailer, than a high sheriff of a county, since neither can stir from his employment. Formerly women of wit married fools for a great estate, a fine seat, or the like; but now ’tis for a pretty seat only in Lincoln’s-Inn-Fields, St. James’s-Fields, or the Pall-Mall.

*Enter **Sparkish**, and **Harcourt**, dressed like a Parson.*

Sparkish
Madam, your humble servant, a happy day to you, and to us all.

Harcourt
Amen.

Alithea
Who have we here?

Sparkish
My chaplain, faith—O madam, poor Harcourt remembers his humble service to you; and, in obedience to your last commands, refrains coming into your sight.

Alithea
Is not that he?

Sparkish
No, fy, no; but to show that he ne’er intended to hinder our match, has sent his brother here to join our hands. When I get me a wife, I must get her a chaplain, according to the custom; that is his brother, and my chaplain.

===Chinese===
**露西.**（旁白）哦，风打这边刮呢？——（高声）照这么说，夫人，您是一口咬定：做男人的但凡明白事理，就得把老婆带下乡了。我看，乡下在我们英格兰的年轻小姐眼里，同外洋小姐眼里的修道院一样可怕；凭我的贞洁起誓，我看她们宁可嫁伦敦的狱卒，也不肯嫁一郡的高等郡长——横竖两个都离不得本职。从前有脑子的女人嫁傻瓜，图的是大宗家产、华厦庄园之类；如今图的无非是林肯律师学院广场（Lincoln’s-Inn-Fields）、圣詹姆斯广场（St. James’s-Fields）或蓓尔美尔街（the Pall-Mall）上一处小巧宅子。

*（**斯帕基什**与扮作牧师的**哈考特**上。）*

**斯帕基什.** 夫人，您卑微的仆人；祝您一个良辰吉日，也祝我们大家。

**哈考特.** 阿们。

**阿莉西娅.** 这来的是谁？

**斯帕基什.** 我的随行牧师，说真的——哦，夫人，可怜的哈考特托我向您致以卑微的敬意；他谨遵您上回的号令，克制着不到您跟前来。

**阿莉西娅.** 这不就是他本人么？

**斯帕基什.** 不，嗐，不是；他为了表明从没有半点拦咱俩亲事的意思，特意打发他兄弟来给咱们携手成礼。按着规矩，我娶了媳妇，就得给她配一名随行牧师；这位就是他兄弟，我的牧师。

===Original===
Alithea
His brother!

Lucy
And your chaplain, to preach in your pulpit then—*Aside.*

Alithea
His brother!

Sparkish
Nay, I knew you would not believe it.—I told you, sir, she would take you for your brother Frank.

Alithea
Believe it!

Lucy
His brother! ha! ha! he! he has a trick left still, it seems. *Aside.*

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 他兄弟！

**露西.** 兼您的牧师，往后好在您的讲坛上布道呢——（旁白）

**阿莉西娅.** 他兄弟！

**斯帕基什.** 瞧，我就知道您不肯信。——我告诉过您了，先生，她要把您当成您兄弟弗兰克了。

**阿莉西娅.** 信这个！

**露西.** 他兄弟！哈！哈！嘿！看来他到底还留着一手。（旁白）

===Original===
Sparkish
Come, my dearest, pray let us go to church before the canonical hour is past.

Alithea
For shame, you are abused still.

Sparkish
By the world, ’tis strange now you are so incredulous.

Alithea
’Tis strange you are so credulous.

Sparkish
Dearest of my life, hear me. I tell you this is Ned Harcourt of Cambridge, by the world; you see he has a sneaking college look. ’Tis true he’s something like his brother Frank; and they differ from each other no more than in their age, for they were twins.

Lucy
Ha! ha! ha!

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 来，我的最亲爱的，趁圣职时辰还没过，求您这就同我上教堂去。

**阿莉西娅.** 不害臊么，你还在叫人耍。

**斯帕基什.** 凭全世界起誓，您可真叫人纳闷，这么不肯信人。

**阿莉西娅.** 你这么肯信人，才叫人纳闷。

**斯帕基什.** 我命根子里顶亲爱的，听我说。我告诉您，这是剑桥的内德·哈考特，凭全世界起誓；您瞧他那副缩头缩脑的书院气。不假，他是有几分像他兄弟弗兰克；哥俩彼此的分别，也超不出年纪上头，因为他们生下来是双胞胎。

**露西.** 哈！哈！哈！

===Original===
Alithea
Your servant, sir; I cannot be so deceived, though you are. But come, let’s hear, how do you know what you affirm so confidently?

Sparkish
Why, I’ll tell you all. Frank Harcourt coming to me this morning to wish me joy, and present his service to you, I asked him if he could help me to a parson. Whereupon he told me, he had a brother in town who was in orders; and he went straight away, and sent him, you see there, to me.

Alithea
Yes, Frank goes and puts on a black coat, then tells you he is Ned; that’s all you have for’t.

Sparkish
Pshaw! pshaw! I tell you, by the same token, the midwife put her garter about Frank’s neck, to know ’em asunder, they were so like.

Alithea
Frank tells you this too?

Sparkish
Ay, and Ned there too: nay, they are both in a story.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 您安，先生；您尽管叫人哄，我可受哄不成。不过来，倒要听听，您说得这么笃定，是打哪儿知道的？

**斯帕基什.** 怎么，我原原本本说给您。今儿一早，弗兰克·哈考特来给我道喜，替他向您请安，我就问他能不能替我寻位牧师。他当下说他有个兄弟在城里，受过圣职；说着就去了，把这位给您打发来了，您瞧。

**阿莉西娅.** 是啊，弗兰克跑去披一件黑袍子，回头就告诉你他叫内德；你到手的凭据就这么一条。

**斯帕基什.** 啐！啐！我告诉您，还有个铁证：当年接生婆往弗兰克脖子上系了条吊袜带，好分得清哥俩，他们像到那个地步。

**阿莉西娅.** 这也是弗兰克告诉你的？

**斯帕基什.** 是，那边那位内德也这么说：嗐，哥俩口供一致。

===Original===
Alithea
So, so; very foolish.

Sparkish
Lord, if you won’t believe one, you had best try him by your chambermaid there; for chambermaids must needs know chaplains from other men, they are so used to ’em.

Lucy
Let’s see: nay, I’ll be sworn he has the canonical smirk, and the filthy clammy palm of a chaplain.

Alithea
Well, most reverend doctor, pray let us make an end of this fooling.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 好了，好了；蠢得可以。

**斯帕基什.** 天哪，您既然谁的话也不信，最好叫您那位贴身丫头来验一验；侍女们认牧师，保管道行比别人深，她们同牧师们惯熟得很。

**露西.** 我来瞧瞧：嗐，我可以起誓，他生就一副圣职的假笑，还有牧师那只又脏又黏的手心。

**阿莉西娅.** 好了，最可敬的博士，求你叫这场胡闹收了场罢。

===Original===
Harcourt
With all my soul, divine heavenly creature, when you please.

Alithea
He speaks like a chaplain indeed.

Sparkish
Why, was there not soul, divine, heavenly, in what he said?

===Chinese===
**哈考特.** 万分愿意，神圣的天国尤物，全凭您的便。

**阿莉西娅.** 他说话倒真像个牧师。

**斯帕基什.** 怎么，他方才的话里，不是又有灵魂、又神圣、又天国么？

===Original===
Alithea
Once more, most impertinent black coat, cease your persecution, and let us have a conclusion of this ridiculous love.

Harcourt
I had forgot, I must suit my style to my coat, or I wear it in vain. *Aside.*

Alithea
I have no more patience left; let us make once an end of this troublesome love, I say.

Harcourt
So be it, seraphic lady, when your honour shall think it meet and convenient so to do.

Sparkish
’Gad I’m sure none but a chaplain could speak so, I think.

Alithea
Let me tell you, sir, this dull trick will not serve your turn; though you delay our marriage, you shall not hinder it.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 再说一遍，最放肆的黑袍子，收了你的迫害罢，快叫这段可笑的爱情有个收梢。

**哈考特.** 我倒忘了，文风得配袍子，不然这袍子就白穿了。（旁白）

**阿莉西娅.** 我的耐性一丝也不剩了；我说，这就叫这段纠缠不清的爱情了结算了。

**哈考特.** 遵命，炽天使般的夫人，只等您的荣誉认为合宜、妥当，便如此施行。

**斯帕基什.** 老天，我拿得准，除了牧师，谁也说不出这样的话。

**阿莉西娅.** 告诉你，先生，这套笨把戏成不了你的事；你拖得了我们的婚礼，拦不住。

===Original===
Harcourt
Far be it from me, munificent patroness, to delay your marriage; I desire nothing more than to marry you presently, which I might do, if you yourself would; for my noble, good-natured, and thrice generous patron here would not hinder it.

Sparkish
No, poor man, not I, faith.

Harcourt
And now, madam, let me tell you plainly nobody else shall marry you; by Heavens! I’ll die first, for I’m sure I should die after it.

Lucy
How his love has made him forget his function, as I have seen it in real parsons!

Alithea
That was spoken like a chaplain too? now you understand him, I hope.

Sparkish
Poor man, he takes it heinously to be refused; I can’t blame him, ’tis putting an indignity upon him, not to be suffered; but you’ll pardon me, madam, it shan’t be; he shall marry us; come away, pray madam.

===Chinese===
**哈考特.** 岂敢岂敢，慷慨的女施主，岂敢耽误您的婚事；我只巴不得立刻娶您——只要您自个儿肯；因为我这位高贵、厚道、慷慨到三倍有余的施主绝不会拦。

**斯帕基什.** 不拦，可怜的人，我决不拦，真的。

**哈考特.** 再者，夫人，我不妨明明白白告诉您：除了我，谁也不许娶您；皇天在上！我宁可先死一步，因为我拿得准，他娶了您，我随后也活不成。

**露西.** 瞧，爱情叫他连圣职都忘了——我在真牧师身上也见过这样的！

**阿莉西娅.** 这话也像牧师说的？这回你总该听懂他了吧，我希望。

**斯帕基什.** 可怜人，遭了回绝，恼得不轻；这也难怪他，这是往他脸上安怠慢，谁也忍不下；可您别见怪，夫人，断不能这么办；他非得给咱们主婚不可；走罢，求您了，夫人。

===Original===
Lucy
Ha! ha! he! more ado! ’tis late.

Alithea
Invincible stupidity! I tell you, he would marry me as your rival, not as your chaplain.

Sparkish
Come, come, madam. *Pulling her away.*

Lucy
I pray, madam, do not refuse this reverend divine the honour and satisfaction of marrying you; for I dare say, he has set his heart upon’t, good doctor.

Alithea
What can you hope or design by this?

Harcourt
I could answer her, a reprieve for a day only, often revokes a hasty doom. At worst, if she will not take mercy on me, and let me marry her, I have at least the lover’s second pleasure, hindering my rival’s enjoyment, though but for a time. *Aside.*

Sparkish
Come, madam, ’tis e’en twelve o’clock, and my mother charged me never to be married out of the canonical hours. Come, come; Lord, here’s such a deal of modesty, I warrant, the first day.

Lucy
Yes, an’t please your worship, married women show all their modesty the first day, because married men show all their love the first day.

*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
**露西.** 哈！哈！嘿！越闹越热闹了！天不早了。

**阿莉西娅.** 无可救药的蠢笨！告诉你，他要娶我，是以你情敌的身份，不是以你牧师的身份。

**斯帕基什.** 来罢，来罢，夫人。（拉她走。）

**露西.** 求您了，夫人，别驳了这位可敬的神学博士的脸面和心愿——他可是一心一意要娶您呢，好博士，我说得对不对？

**阿莉西娅.** 你到底指望什么、图什么？

**哈考特.**（旁白）我原可以答她：只缓刑一日，也常能勾销一桩仓促的判决。退一万步，她若不肯开恩嫁我，我至少还剩情人第二等的乐子——叫情敌一时享不成，哪怕只拖得一晌。

**斯帕基什.** 来，夫人，可就十二点了；我母亲叮嘱过我，万万不可在圣职时辰之外成婚。来，来；天哪，好大的一堆娇羞，我保管，头一天都这样。

**露西.** 是，回您的话：做太太的头一天亮出全部的娇羞，正因为做丈夫的头一天亮出全部的恩爱。

*（同下。）*

### Scene II / 第二场

===Original===
A bedchamber in **Pinchwife’s** house.

***Pinchwife** and **Mrs. Pinchwife** discovered.*

Pinchwife
Come, tell me, I say.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Lord! han’t I told it a hundred times over?

Pinchwife
*Aside.* I would try, if in the repetition of the ungrateful tale, I could find her altering it in the least circumstance; for if her story be false, she is so too.—*Aloud.* Come, how was’t, baggage?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Lord, what pleasure you take to hear it sure!

===Chinese===
平奇怀夫家中一卧房。

*（**平奇怀夫**与**平奇怀夫太太**已在场上。）*

**平奇怀夫.** 来，我说，你给我讲。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 天哪！我不是讲过一百遍了么？

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）我要试一试，这段倒胃口的旧事叫她再讲一遍，能不能讲出半点出入；因为她的故事若是假的，她这个人也是假的。——（高声）来，怎么一回事，小贱人？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 天哪，你听这个可真爱听，真的！

===Original===
Pinchwife
No, you take more in telling it I find; but speak, how was’t?

Mrs. Pinchwife
He carried me up into the house next to the Exchange.

Pinchwife
So, and you two were only in the room!

Mrs. Pinchwife
Yes, for he sent away a youth that was there, for some dried fruit, and China oranges.

Pinchwife
Did he so? Damn him for it—and for—

Mrs. Pinchwife
But presently came up the gentlewoman of the house.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 不，我看你讲起来的瘾头更大；说罢，怎么一回事？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 他把我带上交易所隔壁那座屋子的楼上。

**平奇怀夫.** 好啊，屋里就你们两个！

**平奇怀夫太太.** 是啊，他把屋里一个小后生打发走了，去买果干和中国橙子。

**平奇怀夫.** 他倒会打发！叫这狗东西遭瘟——还为了——

**平奇怀夫太太.** 可不一会儿，屋子的女主人就上来了。

===Original===
Pinchwife
O, ’twas well she did; but what did he do whilst the fruit came?

Mrs. Pinchwife
He kissed me a hundred times, and told me he fancied he kissed my fine sister, meaning me, you know, whom he said he loved with all his soul, and bid me be sure to tell her so, and to desire her to be at her window, by eleven of the clock this morning, and he would walk under it at that time.

Pinchwife
And he was as good as his word, very punctual; a pox reward him for’t. *Aside.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
Well, and he said if you were not within, he would come up to her, meaning me, you know, bud, still.

Pinchwife
*Aside.* So—he knew her certainly; but for this confession, I am obliged to her simplicity.—*Aloud.* But what, you stood very still when he kissed you?

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 哦，亏得她上来；可等果子那工夫，他都干了什么？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 他亲了我一百下，还说，他只当他亲的是我那标致姐姐——你知道，说的其实就是我——又说他拿整个灵魂爱她，叫我千万把这话捎到，还求她今儿早上十一点整守在窗口，他到那钟点要打她窗底下走。

**平奇怀夫.** 他倒说话算话，准时得很；叫瘟病赏他罢。（旁白）

**平奇怀夫太太.** 还有，他说，要是你不在家，他就上楼到她那儿去——你知道，乖乖，说的还是我。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）好啊——他分明认得她；不过这番口供，倒亏了她这份实心眼。——（高声）可是怎么，他亲你那会儿，你站得倒安分？

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
Yes, I warrant you; would you have had me discovered myself?

Pinchwife
But you told me he did some beastliness to you, as you call it; what was’t?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Why, he put—

Pinchwife
What?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Why, he put the tip of his tongue between my lips, and so mousled me—and I said, I’d bite it.

Pinchwife
An eternal canker seize it, for a dog!

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 可不是么，保管安分；难道你要我自个儿露馅？

**平奇怀夫.** 可你跟我说过，他对你使了些畜生勾当——你是这么叫的；是什么勾当？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 嗐，他把——

**平奇怀夫.** 把什么？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 嗐，他把舌尖塞进我两片嘴唇当中，就这么咂了我——我说了，我要咬它。

**平奇怀夫.** 叫它烂上万年溃疡，这条狗！

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
Nay, you need not be so angry with him neither, for to say truth, he has the sweetest breath I ever knew.

Pinchwife
The devil! you were satisfied with it then, and would do it again?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Not unless he should force me.

Pinchwife
Force you, changeling! I tell you, no woman can be forced.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Yes, but she may sure, by such a one as he, for he’s a proper, goodly, strong man; ’tis hard, let me tell you, to resist him.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 嗐，你也犯不着这么恼他；说句实话，他那口气，是我知道的顶香甜的一口气。

**平奇怀夫.** 见鬼！这么说，你受用了，还想再来一回？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 除非他强来，那另说。

**平奇怀夫.** 强来，傻丫头！告诉你，天下的女人，没一个能叫人强来。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 能，真能的——碰上他这号人物就真能；他是位齐整、雄壮、有膂力的爷们；告诉你罢，要顶住他，可不容易。

===Original===
Pinchwife
*Aside.* So, ’tis plain she loves him, yet she has not love enough to make her conceal it from me; but the sight of him will increase her aversion for me and love for him; and that love instruct her how to deceive me and satisfy him, all idiot as she is. Love! ’twas he gave women first their craft, their art of deluding. Out of Nature’s hands they came plain, open, silly, and fit for slaves, as she and Heaven intended ’em; but damned Love—well—I must strangle that little monster whilst I can deal with him.—*Aloud.* Go fetch pen, ink, and paper out of the next room.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Yes, bud.

*Exit.*

Pinchwife
Why should women have more invention in love than men? It can only be, because they have more desires, more soliciting passions, more lust, and more of the devil.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）好，明摆着她爱他；好在这份爱还不够，不够叫她瞒我。可再照上一面，她对我的嫌恶、对他的情爱都要见长；那份情爱自会教她——傻归傻——怎么哄我、怎么满足他。爱情！是它头一个教给女人心眼，教会她们哄人的手艺。她们从造化手里出来时，本是坦白、敞亮、憨傻，天生当奴隶的坯子，正合造化和老天的本意；可该死的爱情——好罢——趁我还有力气对付，我得掐死那小怪物。——（高声）去，到隔壁屋里把纸笔墨拿来。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 好嘞，乖乖。

*（下。）*

**平奇怀夫.** 为什么女人在情爱上的巧思比男人多？缘故只能有一个：她们欲念更多、勾人的情热更多、淫心更多、魔鬼的成分也更多。

===Original===
*Reenter **Mrs. Pinchwife**.*

Come, minx, sit down and write.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Ay, dear bud, but I can’t do’t very well.

Pinchwife
I wish you could not at all.

Mrs. Pinchwife
But what should I write for?

Pinchwife
I’ll have you write a letter to your lover.

===Chinese===
*（**平奇怀夫太太**又上。）*

**平奇怀夫.** 来，小狐狸精，坐下写。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 哎，亲乖乖，可我写不大好。

**平奇怀夫.** 我倒巴望你压根儿不会。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 可要我写什么呀？

**平奇怀夫.** 我要你给你的情人写封信。

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
O Lord, to the fine gentleman a letter!

Pinchwife
Yes, to the fine gentleman.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Lord, you do but jeer: sure you jest.

Pinchwife
I am not so merry: come, write as I bid you.

Mrs. Pinchwife
What, do you think I am a fool?

Pinchwife
*Aside.* She’s afraid I would not dictate any love to him, therefore she’s unwilling.—*Aloud.* But you had best begin.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 哎呀老天，给那位俊公子写信！

**平奇怀夫.** 对，给那位俊公子。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 天哪，你逗我呢：你准是说笑。

**平奇怀夫.** 我没那份闲心：来，照我吩咐的写。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 怎么，你当我是傻子？

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）她怕我念出来的不带情意，所以不情愿。——（高声）可你顶好这就动笔。

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
Indeed, and indeed, but I won’t, so I won’t.

Pinchwife
Why?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Because he’s in town; you may send for him if you will.

Pinchwife
Very well, you would have him brought to you; is it come to this? I say, take the pen and write, or you’ll provoke me.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Lord, what d’ye make a fool of me for? Don’t I know that letters are never writ but from the country to London, and from London into the country? Now he’s in town, and I am in town too; therefore I can’t write to him, you know.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 真的，真的，我不写，就是不写。

**平奇怀夫.** 为什么？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 因为他在城里；你要乐意，打发人去叫他来就是。

**平奇怀夫.** 好得很，你想叫他送到你跟前来；事情到这一步了？我说，拿笔，写，不然你要惹恼我。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 天哪，你拿我当傻子耍做什么？信这种东西，不都是从乡下写到伦敦、从伦敦写到乡下的么？如今他在城里，我也在城里；所以我没法子写给他，你知道的。

===Original===
Pinchwife
*Aside.* So, I am glad it is no worse; she is innocent enough yet.—*Aloud.* Yes, you may, when your husband bids you, write letters to people that are in town.

Mrs. Pinchwife
O, may I so? then I’m satisfied.

Pinchwife
Come, begin:—“Sir”—*Dictates.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
Shan’t I say, “Dear Sir?”—You know one says always something more than bare “sir.”

Pinchwife
Write as I bid you, or I will write whore with this penknife in your face.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Nay, good bud—“Sir”—*Writes.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）好，还好，没更糟；她眼下的天真还够用。——（高声）可以的；丈夫叫你写，你就可以写信给城里的人。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 哦，可以的么？那我就服了。

**平奇怀夫.** 来，起头：——「先生」——（口授。）

**平奇怀夫太太.** 我不写「亲爱的先生」么？——你知道，总得比光秃秃一个「先生」多写点什么。

**平奇怀夫.** 照我吩咐的写，不然我就拿这把削笔刀，在你脸上写下「婊子」两个字。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 别呀，好乖乖——「先生」——（写。）

===Original===
Pinchwife
“Though I suffered last night your nauseous, loathed kisses and embraces”—Write!

Mrs. Pinchwife
Nay, why should I say so? You know I told you he had a sweet breath.

Pinchwife
Write!

Mrs. Pinchwife
Let me but put out “loathed.”

Pinchwife
Write, I say!

Mrs. Pinchwife
Well then. *Writes.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 「昨夜我忍受了你的令人作呕、叫人憎恶的亲吻和搂抱」——写！

**平奇怀夫太太.** 嗐，干吗要这么写？你知道，我告诉过你，他口气香甜。

**平奇怀夫.** 写！

**平奇怀夫太太.** 容我把「叫人憎恶的」去掉罢。

**平奇怀夫.** 我说，写！

**平奇怀夫太太.** 好罢。（写。）

===Original===
Pinchwife
Let’s see, what have you writ?—*Takes the paper and reads.* “Though I suffered last night your kisses and embraces”—Thou impudent creature! where is “nauseous” and “loathed?”

Mrs. Pinchwife
I can’t abide to write such filthy words.

Pinchwife
Once more write as I’d have you, and question it not, or I will spoil thy writing with this. I will stab out those eyes that cause my mischief. *Holds up the penknife.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
O Lord! I will.

Pinchwife
So—so—let’s see now.—*Reads.* “Though I suffered last night your nauseous, loathed kisses and embraces”—go on—“yet I would not have you presume that you shall ever repeat them”—so—*She writes.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
I have writ it.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 瞧瞧，你写了些什么？——（取过信纸念。）「昨夜我忍受了你的亲吻和搂抱」——好个厚脸皮的东西！「令人作呕」「叫人憎恶」呢？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 这种脏字眼儿，我写不来。

**平奇怀夫.** 再照我的话写一遍，休得多问，不然我就用这个毁了你的字。我要把那双招祸的眼睛剜出来。（举起削笔刀。）

**平奇怀夫太太.** 哎呀老天！我写。

**平奇怀夫.** 好了——好了——再瞧瞧。——（念。）「昨夜我忍受了你的令人作呕、叫人憎恶的亲吻和搂抱」——往下——「但你休得放肆，以为还能有下回」——好——（她写。）

**平奇怀夫太太.** 写好了。

===Original===
Pinchwife
On, then—“I then concealed myself from your knowledge, to avoid your insolencies.”—*She writes.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
So—

Pinchwife
“The same reason, now I am out of your hands—” *She writes.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
So—

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 那么接着——「我当日瞒下身份，为的是躲你的无礼。」——（她写。）

**平奇怀夫太太.** 好了——

**平奇怀夫.** 「如今我既脱出你的掌心，也是同样的缘故——」（她写。）

**平奇怀夫太太.** 好了——

===Original===
Pinchwife
“Makes me own to you my unfortunate, though innocent frolic, of being in man’s clothes”—*She writes.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
So—

Pinchwife
“That you may forevermore cease to pursue her, who hates and detests you”—*She writes on.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
So—heigh! *Sighs.*

Pinchwife
What, do you sigh?—“detests you—as much as she loves her husband and her honour—”

Mrs. Pinchwife
I vow, husband, he’ll ne’er believe I should write such a letter.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 「叫我把那桩不幸、却无恶意的男装胡闹，向你招认了」——（她写。）

**平奇怀夫太太.** 好了——

**平奇怀夫.** 「好叫你从此永远死了心，别再缠那个恨你、嫌恶你的人」——（她接着写。）

**平奇怀夫太太.** 好了——唉！（叹气。）

**平奇怀夫.** 怎么，叹气？——「嫌恶你——正如她爱她的丈夫、爱她的荣誉——」

**平奇怀夫太太.** 我起誓，当家的，他死也不会信，这样的信是我写的。

===Original===
Pinchwife
What, he’d expect a kinder from you? Come, now your name only.

Mrs. Pinchwife
What, shan’t I say “Your most faithful humble servant till death?”

Pinchwife
No, tormenting fiend!—*Aside.* Her style, I find, would be very soft.—*Aloud.* Come, wrap it up now whilst I go fetch wax and a candle; and write on the backside, “For Mr. Horner.” *Exit.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 怎么，他还指望你写封更亲热的？来，光落个名字。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 怎么，不写「你到死最忠心、最卑微的仆人」么？

**平奇怀夫.** 不写，磨人的鬼东西！——（旁白）瞧这口气，她要自个儿写，保管软得很。——（高声）来，这就折起来，我去取火漆和蜡烛；背面写上「霍纳先生亲启」。（下。）

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
“For Mr. Horner.”—So, I am glad he has told me his name. Dear Mr. Horner! but why should I send thee such a letter that will vex thee, and make thee angry with me?—Well, I will not send it.—Ay, but then my husband will kill me—for I see plainly he won’t let me love Mr. Horner—but what care I for my husband?—I won’t, so I won’t, send poor Mr. Horner such a letter—But then my husband—but oh, what if I writ at bottom my husband made me write it?—Ay, but then my husband would see’t—Can one have no shift? ah, a London woman would have had a hundred presently. Stay—what if I should write a letter, and wrap it up like this, and write upon’t too? Ay, but then my husband would see’t—I don’t know what to do.—But yet evads I’ll try, so I will—for I will not send this letter to poor Mr. Horner, come what will on’t.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 「霍纳先生亲启」。——好了，亏得他告诉了我那位先生的名字。亲爱的霍纳先生！可我干吗要给你寄这么一封信，惹你气恼、叫你冲我发火呢？——好，我不寄。——可是，那当家的要杀了我的——我看得明明白白，他不肯叫我爱霍纳先生——可我管他做什么？——我不寄，就是不寄，说什么也不能叫可怜的霍纳先生收这么封信——可再一想，当家的——哦，有了，要是我信尾添一句「当家的逼我写的」呢？——可他一瞧就瞧见了——就没个遮掩的法子么？唉，换个伦敦女人，眼面前就能生出一百个来。慢着——要是我也写上一封，照这封一模一样折好，也写上这么一行呢？可他照样要瞧见——我没主意了。——不过躲得过就躲，我偏要试试——因为说什么我也不能把这么封信寄给可怜的霍纳先生，天塌下来也不寄。

===Original===
