“Dear, sweet Mr. Horner”—*Writes and repeats what she writes.*—so—“my husband would have me send you a base, rude, unmannerly letter; but I won’t”—so—“and would have me forbid you loving me; but I won’t”—so—“and would have me say to you, I hate you, poor Mr. Horner; but I won’t tell a lie for him”—there—“for I’m sure if you and I were in the country at cards together”—so—“I could not help treading on your toe under the table”—so—“or rubbing knees with you, and staring in your face, till you saw me”—very well—“and then looking down, and blushing for an hour together”—so—“but I must make haste before my husband comes: and now he has taught me to write letters, you shall have longer ones from me, who am, dear, dear, poor, dear Mr. Horner, your most humble friend, and servant to command till death—Margery Pinchwife.”

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 「亲爱的、甜甜的霍纳先生」——（边写边念。）——好了——「我当家的逼我给你寄一封下作、粗鲁、没规矩的信；可我不肯」——好了——「还逼我不许你爱我；可我不肯」——好了——「还逼我对你说，我恨你，可怜的霍纳先生；可我不肯替他撒这个谎」——成了——「因为我拿得准，你我要是一块儿在乡下打牌」——好了——「我准忍不住要在桌子底下踩你的脚尖」——好了——「不然就同你蹭膝盖，直勾勾盯着你的脸，盯到你瞧见我为止」——很好——「然后低下头去，一臊臊上一个钟头」——好了——「可我得赶紧，当家的要回来了：如今他教会了我写信，往后你收我的信，一封要比一封长。你那亲爱的、亲爱的、可怜的、亲爱的霍纳先生，你最卑微的朋友、到死听凭差遣的仆人——玛格丽·平奇怀夫。」

===Original===
Stay, I must give him a hint at bottom—so—now wrap it up just like t’other—so—now write “For Mr. Horner”—But oh now, what shall I do with it? for here comes my husband.

*Reenter **Pinchwife**.*

Pinchwife
*Aside.* I have been detained by a sparkish coxcomb, who pretended a visit to me; but I fear ’twas to my wife—*Aloud.* What, have you done?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Ay, ay, bud, just now.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 慢着，还得在信尾给他递个暗号——好了——这就照那封一模一样折好——好了——写上「霍纳先生亲启」——可这会儿糟了，这信怎么送出去呢？当家的来了。

*（**平奇怀夫**又上。）*

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）方才叫个花花公子草包缠住了，他假模假式登门拜访我；只怕醉翁之意在我老婆——（高声）怎么，写完了？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 写完了，写完了，乖乖，刚刚写完。

===Original===
Pinchwife
Let’s see’t: what d’ye tremble for? what, you would not have it go?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Here—*Aside.* No, I must not give him that: so I had been served if I had given him this. *He opens and reads the first letter.*

Pinchwife
Come, where’s the wax and seal?

Mrs. Pinchwife
*Aside.* Lord, what shall I do now? Nay, then I have it—*Aloud.* Pray let me see’t. Lord, you think me so arrant a fool, I cannot seal a letter; I will do’t, so I will. *Snatches the letter from him, changes it for the other, seals it, and delivers it to him.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 拿来我瞧：抖什么？怎么，你还舍不得它寄出去？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 给你——（旁白）不，那封可不能落到他手里；方才要给了他那封，就有好瞧的了。（他拆开念那第一封信。）

**平奇怀夫.** 来，火漆和印章呢？

**平奇怀夫太太.**（旁白）天哪，这可怎么办？——有了——（高声）求你给我瞧瞧。天哪，你当我是十足的低能儿，连封信都不会封；我会封，当然会。（从他手里夺过信，换成另一封，封好，交给他。）

===Original===
Pinchwife
Nay, I believe you will learn that, and other things too, which I would not have you.

Mrs. Pinchwife
So, han’t I done it curiously?9—*Aside.* I think I have; there’s my letter going to Mr. Horner, since he’ll needs have me send letters to folks.

Pinchwife
’Tis very well; but I warrant, you would not have it go now?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Yes, indeed, but I would, bud, now.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 是啊，我看这个你学得会，旁的你也一样要学——偏偏都是我不要你学的。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 瞧，我办得巧不巧？9——（旁白）我看是巧；他既然非要我给人写信不可，我那封信这就去霍纳先生那儿了。

**平奇怀夫.** 很好；可我拿得准，你这会儿又舍不得它寄了？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 舍不得才怪呢，我巴望它走呢，乖乖，这会儿就巴望。

===Original===
Pinchwife
Well, you are a good girl then. Come, let me lock you up in your chamber, till I come back; and be sure you come not within three strides of the window when I am gone, for I have a spy in the street.—*Exit **Mrs. Pinchwife**, **Pinchwife** locks the door.* At least, ’tis fit she think so. If we do not cheat women, they’ll cheat us, and fraud may be justly used with secret enemies, of which a wife is the most dangerous; and he that has a handsome one to keep, and a frontier town, must provide against treachery, rather than open force. Now I have secured all within, I’ll deal with the foe without, with false intelligence.

*Holds up the letter. Exit.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 好，那才是乖孩子。来，我把你锁在屋里，等我回来；我走了，你千万离窗口三大步开外，因为街上我安了眼线。——（**平奇怀夫太太**下，**平奇怀夫**落锁。）至少，叫她这么想是应当的。我们不算计女人，女人就算计我们；对暗地里的敌人使诈，名正言顺——而妻子正是顶危险的那种；谁要看住一个标致的，又守一座边关城池，就得防内奸甚于防明枪。如今里面都锁牢了，我这就去对付外头的敌人——使假情报。

*（举起信，下。）*

### Scene III / 第三场

===Original===
**Horner’s** lodging

*Enter **Horner** and **Quack**.*

Quack
Well, sir, how fadges10 the new design? have you not the luck of all your brother projectors, to deceive only yourself at last?

Horner
No, good domine doctor, I deceive you, it seems, and others too; for the grave matrons, and old, rigid husbands think me as unfit for love, as they are; but their wives, sisters, and daughters know, some of ’em, better things already.

Quack
Already!

Horner
Already, I say. Last night I was drunk with half-a-dozen of your civil persons, as you call ’em, and people of honour, and so was made free of their society and dressing-rooms forever hereafter; and am already come to the privileges of sleeping upon their pallets, warming smocks, tying shoes and garters, and the like, doctor, already, already, doctor.

===Chinese===
霍纳的寓所。

*（**霍纳**与**庸医**上。）*

**庸医.** 好啊，先生，新计谋的成色如何？10天底下搞空想的同行，到头来个个只骗着了自个儿，你莫非偏偏逃得掉这份运气？

**霍纳.** 不，好一位牧师博士，看来骗着的是你，还有旁人；因为那些端着架子的主妇们、又老又硬的丈夫们，当我不中用得同他们一个样；可他们的太太、小姨、闺女们，有的早见识过更妙的了。

**庸医.** 早？

**霍纳.** 早，我说。昨晚我同你所谓的六位「规矩人」、几位荣誉人物，喝了个烂醉，就此永远获准入他们的交际圈和梳妆室了；而且已经升到那份特权——睡她们的榻，焐她们的睡衣，替她们系鞋带、绑袜带，诸如此类，大夫，已经了，已经了，大夫。

===Original===
Quack
You have made good use of your time, sir.

Horner
I tell thee, I am now no more interruption to ’em, when they sing, or talk bawdy, than a little squab French page who speaks no English.

Quack
But do civil persons and women of honour drink, and sing bawdy songs?

Horner
O, amongst friends, amongst friends. For your bigots in honour are just like those in religion; they fear the eye of the world more than the eye of Heaven; and think there is no virtue, but railing at vice, and no sin, but giving scandal. They rail at a poor, little, kept player, and keep themselves some young, modest pulpit comedian to be privy to their sins in their closets, not to tell ’em of them in their chapels.

===Chinese===
**庸医.** 先生，您这时间用得可真值。

**霍纳.** 我告诉你，如今她们唱歌也好、说荤话也好，有我在跟前，比一个不懂英文的矮墩墩法国小听差还碍不着事。

**庸医.** 可规矩人、荣誉的太太们，也喝酒、也唱荤歌么？

**霍纳.** 哦，朋友堆里么，朋友堆里。荣誉的偏执狂同宗教的偏执狂一个德行：怕世人的眼，甚于怕上天的眼；以为只有痛骂恶习才算德行，只有闹出丑闻才算罪过。她们骂一个可怜的、小小被人包养的戏子，自家却养个年轻腼腆的「布道坛戏子」，在密室里同他私底下知罪——而不是在礼拜堂里叫他数落罪。

===Original===
Quack
Nay, the truth on’t is, priests, amongst the women now, have quite got the better of us lay-confessors, physicians.

Horner
And they are rather their patients; but—

*Enter **Lady Fidget**, looking about her.*

Now we talk of women of honour, here comes one. Step behind the screen there, and but observe, if I have not particular privileges with the women of reputation already, doctor, already. ***Quack** retires.*

===Chinese===
**庸医.** 可不是，实情是：如今在女人堆里，教士们把我们这些在俗的听罪神父——也就是大夫们——彻底比下去了。

**霍纳.** 他们倒不如说是她们的病人；不过——

*（**菲吉特夫人**东张西望地上。）*

**霍纳.** 说荣誉女人，荣誉女人就到。到那边屏风后头去，只管瞧着，看我在这些有名声的太太跟前，是不是已经享上了特别的特权，大夫，已经了。（庸医退下。）

===Original===
Lady Fidget
Well, Horner, am not I a woman of honour? you see, I’m as good as my word.

Horner
And you shall see, madam, I’ll not be behindhand with you in honour; and I’ll be as good as my word too, if you please but to withdraw into the next room.

Lady Fidget
But first, my dear sir, you must promise to have a care of my dear honour.

Horner
If you talk a word more of your honour, you’ll make me incapable to wrong it. To talk of honour in the mysteries of love, is like talking of Heaven or the Deity, in an operation of witchcraft, just when you are employing the devil: it makes the charm impotent.

Lady Fidget
Nay, fy! let us not be smutty. But you talk of mysteries and bewitching to me; I don’t understand you.

Horner
I tell you, madam, the word money in a mistress’s mouth, at such a nick of time, is not a more disheartening sound to a younger brother, than that of honour to an eager lover like myself.

===Chinese===
**菲吉特夫人.** 好啊，霍纳，我难道不是个讲荣誉的女人？你瞧，我说到做到。

**霍纳.** 您也会看得到，夫人，论荣誉我不落您的后尘；我也说到做到——只消请您移步隔壁屋子。

**菲吉特夫人.** 可先得依我一桩，亲爱的先生：您得答应我，好生看住我宝贵的荣誉。

**霍纳.** 您再提一个字的荣誉，就要叫我没本事亏负它了。在爱情的秘仪里谈荣誉，就像行巫做法的当口谈天国、谈神明——您正驱使着魔鬼呢：这一谈，符咒就不灵了。

**菲吉特夫人.** 嗐，去你的！别说脏话。可你同我讲什么秘仪、什么迷惑；我听不懂你。

**霍纳.** 告诉你，夫人，在那样的节骨眼上，情妇嘴里蹦出个「钱」字，对分不着家产的小儿子有多泄气，「荣誉」二字对我这样心热的情人，就有多泄气。

===Original===
Lady Fidget
But you can’t blame a lady of my reputation to be chary.

Horner
Chary! I have been chary of it already, by the report I have caused of myself.

Lady Fidget
Ay, but if you should ever let other women know that dear secret, it would come out. Nay, you must have a great care of your conduct; for my acquaintance are so censorious, (oh, ’tis a wicked, censorious world, Mr. Horner!) I say, are so censorious, and detracting, that perhaps they’ll talk to the prejudice of my honour, though you should not let them know the dear secret.

Horner
Nay, madam, rather than they shall prejudice your honour, I’ll prejudice theirs; and, to serve you, I’ll lie with ’em all, make the secret their own, and then they’ll keep it. I am a Machiavel in love, madam.

===Chinese===
**菲吉特夫人.** 可我这样身份的夫人爱惜羽毛，您总不能怪。

**霍纳.** 爱惜！我早替您爱惜过了——瞧我给自个儿散出去的名声。

**菲吉特夫人.** 是啊，可您哪天走漏给别的女人知道那个宝贝秘密，它就露馅了。不，您的行事得千万仔细；因为我那些相识个个长舌，（哦，这世道，又坏、又爱嚼舌，霍纳先生！）我说，个个长舌、个个刻薄，保不齐您一句不走漏，她们也要嚼出些有损我荣誉的话来。

**霍纳.** 夫人，与其叫她们糟蹋您的荣誉，不如我去糟蹋她们的；为给您效劳，我把她们统统睡一遍，叫秘密成了她们自家的私产，她们自然守口如瓶。我是情场上的马基雅维利（Machiavel），夫人。

===Original===
Lady Fidget
O, no sir, not that way.

Horner
Nay, the devil take me, if censorious women are to be silenced any other way.

Lady Fidget
A secret is better kept, I hope, by a single person than a multitude; therefore pray do not trust anybody else with it, dear, dear Mr. Horner. *Embracing him.*

*Enter **Sir Jasper Fidget**.*

Sir Jasper
How now!

===Chinese===
**菲吉特夫人.** 哦，不，先生，不是这么个效劳法。

**霍纳.** 嗐，叫魔鬼抓了我去——长舌女人要封口，再没第二条路。

**菲吉特夫人.** 我指望，一个人守秘密，总比一大群人守得牢；所以求您千万别再托付第二个人，亲爱的、亲爱的霍纳先生。（拥抱他。）

*（**贾斯珀·菲吉特爵士**上。）*

**贾斯珀爵士.** 喂，这是怎么了！

===Original===
Lady Fidget
*Aside.* O my husband!—prevented—and what’s almost as bad, found with my arms about another man—that will appear too much—what shall I say?—*Aloud.* Sir Jasper, come hither: I am trying if Mr. Horner were ticklish, and he’s as ticklish as can be. I love to torment the confounded toad; let you and I tickle him.

Sir Jasper
No, your ladyship will tickle him better without me, I suppose. But is this your buying china? I thought you had been at the china-house.

Horner
*Aside.* China-house! that’s my cue, I must take it.—*Aloud.* A pox! can’t you keep your impertinent wives at home? Some men are troubled with the husbands, but I with the wives; but I’d have you to know, since I cannot be your journeyman by night, I will not be your drudge by day, to squire your wife about, and be your man of straw, or scarecrow only to pies and jays, that would be nibbling at your forbidden fruit; I shall be shortly the hackney gentleman-usher of the town.

===Chinese===
**菲吉特夫人.**（旁白）哎呀，我丈夫！——误了好事——而且几乎同样糟的是，叫他撞见我搂着另一个男人——这可太扎眼了——怎么交代呢？——（高声）贾斯珀爵士，过来：我在试霍纳先生怕不怕痒，他呀，怕痒怕到了家。我就爱折磨这只该死的癞蛤蟆；咱们俩来胳肢他。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 不，夫人胳肢他，没我在跟前更得法，想必。可这就是您买瓷器？我还当您上瓷器铺去了呢。

**霍纳.**（旁白）瓷器铺！这是给我的提词，得接住。——（高声）遭瘟！你们就不能把自家那些放肆的太太圈在家里？有的男人苦于丈夫们的纠缠，我却苦于太太们；可我把话说在明处：我夜里既当不成您的雇工，白天也就不当您的苦差，不陪您太太前呼后拥，不给您当傀儡、当稻草人，光去吓唬那些想偷啄您禁果的喜鹊和松鸦；再这么下去，我不久就要当上全城雇用的绅士前导了。

===Original===
Sir Jasper
*Aside.* He! he! he! poor fellow, he’s in the right on’t, faith. To squire women about for other folks is as ungrateful an employment, as to tell money for other folks.—*Aloud.* He! he! he! be’n’t angry, Horner.

Lady Fidget
No, ’tis I have more reason to be angry, who am left by you, to go abroad indecently alone; or, what is more indecent, to pin myself upon such ill-bred people of your acquaintance as this is.

Sir Jasper
Nay, prithee, what has he done?

Lady Fidget
Nay, he has done nothing.

Sir Jasper
But what d’ye take ill, if he has done nothing?

Lady Fidget
Ha! ha! ha! faith, I can’t but laugh however; why, d’ye think the unmannerly toad would come down to me to the coach? I was fain to come up to fetch him, or go without him, which I was resolved not to do; for he knows china very well, and has himself very good, but will not let me see it, lest I should beg some; but I will find it out, and have what I came for yet.

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.**（旁白）嘿！嘿！嘿！可怜的家伙，他说得在理，真的。替别人陪着太太们逛，同替别人数钱一样，是件不落好的差事。——（高声）嘿！嘿！嘿！别恼，霍纳。

**菲吉特夫人.** 不，更有理由恼的是我：撇下我，叫我一个人出门，不成体统；或者更不成体统的，硬叫我去贴着你相识的这种没教养的家伙。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 嗐，说真的，他怎么了？

**菲吉特夫人.** 嗐，他倒没怎么。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 可他没怎么，你又恼的哪一门？

**菲吉特夫人.** 哈！哈！哈！说真的，我还是忍不住要笑；怎么，你当那只没规矩的癞蛤蟆肯下楼陪我上马车么？没奈何，我只得自个儿上来请他，不然就不带他——那我是断断不肯的；因为他顶懂瓷器，自家也藏着顶好的，偏不肯给我看，怕我开口讨；可我偏要把它翻出来，把我此行要的东西弄到手。

===Original===
Horner
*Apart to **Lady Fidget**, as he follows her to the door.* Lock the door, madam.—

*Exit **Lady Fidget**, and locks the door.*

*Aloud.*—So, she has got into my chamber and locked me out. Oh the impertinency of womankind! Well, Sir Jasper, plain-dealing is a jewel; if ever you suffer your wife to trouble me again here, she shall carry you home a pair of horns; by my lord mayor she shall; though I cannot furnish you myself, you are sure, yet I’ll find a way.

Sir Jasper
Ha! ha! he!—*Aside.* At my first coming in, and finding her arms about him, tickling him it seems, I was half jealous, but now I see my folly.—*Aloud.* He! he! he! poor Horner.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.**（向菲吉特夫人旁白，随她走向门口）把门锁上，夫人。—

*（菲吉特夫人下，落了锁。）*

**霍纳.**（高声）——瞧，她进了我的屋子，倒把我锁在外头。哦，女人家这份放肆！好吧，贾斯珀爵士，实话是块珍宝；您要是再容您太太上这儿搅扰我，她就该给您捎一对犄角回家；凭市长大人起誓，一定捎得成；虽说我本人是供不上货的，这个您放心，可我总会寻出条道来。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 哈！哈！嘿！——（旁白）我刚进门，见她搂着他——说是胳肢他——我险些吃了半截醋，如今才见出我的糊涂。——（高声）嘿！嘿！嘿！可怜的霍纳。

===Original===
Horner
Nay, though you laugh now, ’twill be my turn ere long. Oh women, more impertinent, more cunning, and more mischievous than their monkeys, and to me almost as ugly!—Now is she throwing my things about and rifling all I have; but I’ll get into her the back way, and so rifle her for it.

Sir Jasper
Ha! ha! ha! poor angry Horner.

Horner
Stay here a little, I’ll ferret her out to you presently, I warrant.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 嗐，你们尽管这会儿笑，回头就轮到我笑了。哦，这些女人，比她们的猴子还放肆、还狡猾、还会祸害人，而且依我看，也快同猴子一样丑了！——这会儿她正翻我的东西，把我洗劫一空；可我要从后门进她那儿去，照样把她劫个痛快。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 哈！哈！哈！可怜的霍纳，气成这样。

**霍纳.** 在这儿稍待，我拿雪貂把她给您撵出来，保管马上。

===Original===
*Exit at the other door. **Sir Jasper** talks through the door to his **Wife**, she answers from within.*

Sir Jasper
Wife! my Lady Fidget! wife! he is coming in to you the back way.

Lady Fidget
Let him come, and welcome, which way he will.

Sir Jasper
He’ll catch you, and use you roughly, and be too strong for you.

Lady Fidget
Don’t you trouble yourself, let him if he can.

Quack
*Aside.* This indeed I could not have believed from him, nor any but my own eyes.

===Chinese===
*（从另一道门下。贾斯珀爵士隔门同太太说话，她在屋里应答。）*

**贾斯珀爵士.** 太太！菲吉特夫人！太太！他要从后门进来找你了。

**菲吉特夫人.** 让他来好了，欢迎之至，爱走哪条道走哪条道。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 他要逮住你，狠狠摆布你，你可抵不过他。

**菲吉特夫人.** 您就别操心了，他有本事就来罢。

**庸医.**（旁白）这样的事，不是他说的我不信，谁说的我都不信——除非凭我这双眼。

===Original===
*Enter **Mrs. Squeamish**.*

Mrs. Squeamish
Where’s this woman-hater, this toad, this ugly, greasy, dirty sloven?

Sir Jasper
*Aside.* So, the women all will have him ugly; methinks he is a comely person, but his wants make his form contemptible to ’em; and ’tis e’en as my wife said yesterday, talking of him, that a proper handsome eunuch was as ridiculous a thing as a gigantic coward.

Mrs. Squeamish
Sir Jasper, your servant: where is the odious beast?

Sir Jasper
He’s within in his chamber, with my wife; she’s playing the wag with him.

Mrs. Squeamish
Is she so? and he’s a clownish beast, he’ll give her no quarter, he’ll play the wag with her again, let me tell you: come, let’s go help her—What, the door’s locked?

===Chinese===
*（**斯奎米什太太**上。）*

**斯奎米什太太.** 那个恨女人的家伙在哪儿？那只癞蛤蟆，那个又丑、又油腻、又邋遢的懒汉？

**贾斯珀爵士.**（旁白）瞧，女人们一口咬定他丑；依我看，他一表人才，可他那「缺了的东西」叫这副相貌在她们眼里一钱不值；这也正像我太太昨天议论他时说的：一个齐齐整整的漂亮阉人，同一个身长丈二的懦夫一样，都是天下的笑柄。

**斯奎米什太太.** 贾斯珀爵士，您安：那只讨厌的畜生在哪儿？

**贾斯珀爵士.** 他在里头屋里，同我太太在一起；她正跟他闹着玩呢。

**斯奎米什太太.** 是么？他可是头粗坯畜生，不会留情面，听我说，他也要跟她闹个够的：来，咱们去帮她——咦，门怎么锁了？

===Original===
Sir Jasper
Ay, my wife locked it.

Mrs. Squeamish
Did she so? let’s break it open then.

Sir Jasper
No, no, he’ll do her no hurt.

Mrs. Squeamish
*Aside.* But is there no other way to get in to ’em? whither goes this? I will disturb ’em.

*Exit at another door.*

*Enter **Old Lady Squeamish**.*

Lady Squeamish
Where is this harlotry, this impudent baggage, this rambling tomrigg?11 O Sir Jasper, I’m glad to see you here; did you not see my vile grandchild come in hither just now?

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 是，我太太锁的。

**斯奎米什太太.** 她锁的？那咱们把门砸开。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 别，别，他伤不着她。

**斯奎米什太太.**（旁白）可还有别的道进他们那儿去么？这道门通哪儿？我偏要去搅了他们。

*（从另一道门下。）*

*（**老斯奎米什夫人**（Old Lady Squeamish）上。）*

**老斯奎米什夫人.** 我那个小贱人呢？那个不要脸的蹄子、那个满街野跑的野丫头？11——哦，贾斯珀爵士，真高兴在这儿见着您；方才您没瞧见我那可恶的孙女进这儿来么？

===Original===
Sir Jasper
Yes.

Lady Squeamish
Ay, but where is she then? where is she? Lord, Sir Jasper, I have e’en rattled myself to pieces in pursuit of her: but can you tell what she makes here? they say below, no woman lodges here.

Sir Jasper
No.

Lady Squeamish
No! what does she here then? say, if it be not a woman’s lodging, what makes she here? But are you sure no woman lodges here?

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 瞧见了。

**老斯奎米什夫人.** 是啊，可她人呢？人在哪儿？天哪，贾斯珀爵士，为追她，我这一路把浑身骨头都颠散了：可您说，她上这儿做什么？楼底下人说，这儿没住女人。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 没住。

**老斯奎米什夫人.** 没住！那她在这儿做什么？您说，这既不是个女人住的地方，她来做什么？可您拿得准这儿没住女人？

===Original===
Sir Jasper
No, nor no man neither, this is Mr. Horner’s lodging.

Lady Squeamish
Is it so, are you sure?

Sir Jasper
Yes, yes.

Lady Squeamish
So; then there’s no hurt in’t, I hope. But where is he?

Sir Jasper
He’s in the next room with my wife.

Lady Squeamish
Nay, if you trust him with your wife, I may with my Biddy. They say, he’s a merry harmless man now, e’en as harmless a man as ever came out of Italy with a good voice, and as pretty, harmless company for a lady, as a snake without his teeth.

Sir Jasper
Ay, ay, poor man.

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 拿得准，男人也没住；这是霍纳先生的寓所。

**老斯奎米什夫人.** 是么，您拿得准？

**贾斯珀爵士.** 拿得准，拿得准。

**老斯奎米什夫人.** 这就好；那么，我但愿没什么妨害。可他在哪儿？

**贾斯珀爵士.** 他在隔壁屋里，同我太太在一起。

**老斯奎米什夫人.** 是啊，您既敢把太太托给他，我也敢把我的小碧迪（Biddy）托给他。都说他如今是个快活无害的人了，同那些从意大利出来的、有副好嗓子的爷们一样无害；陪一位淑女解闷，再没有比他更妥帖、更无害的了——好比一条拔了牙的蛇。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 是，是，可怜的人。

===Original===
*Reenter **Mrs. Squeamish**.*

Mrs. Squeamish
I can’t find ’em.—Oh, are you here, grandmother? I followed, you must know, my Lady Fidget hither; ’tis the prettiest lodging, and I have been staring on the prettiest pictures—

*Reenter **Lady Fidget** with a piece of china in her hand, and **Horner** following.*

Lady Fidget
And I have been toiling and moiling for the prettiest piece of china, my dear.

Horner
Nay, she has been too hard for me, do what I could.

===Chinese===
*（**斯奎米什太太**又上。）*

**斯奎米什太太.** 我找不着他们。——哦，奶奶，您也在这儿？不瞒您说，我是跟着菲吉特夫人一道来的；这寓所漂亮极了，我方才一直在看那些顶漂亮的画儿——

*（**菲吉特夫人**手持一件瓷器又上，**霍纳**随后。）*

**菲吉特夫人.** 我呢，亲爱的，又出力又操劳，才挣下这件顶漂亮的瓷器。

**霍纳.** 可不是，任我使尽解数，还是她占了上风。

===Original===
Mrs. Squeamish
Oh, lord, I’ll have some china too. Good Mr. Horner, don’t think to give other people china, and me none; come in with me too.

Horner
Upon my honour, I have none left now.

Mrs. Squeamish
Nay, nay, I have known you deny your china before now, but you shan’t put me off so. Come.

Horner
This lady had the last there.

===Chinese===
**斯奎米什太太.** 哦，天哪，我也要瓷器。好霍纳先生，别光想着给别人瓷器、独不给我；也陪我进去一趟。

**霍纳.** 凭我的荣誉，这会儿真的一件不剩了。

**斯奎米什太太.** 得了，得了，您从前赖瓷器的账我是领教过的，这回可别想这么打发我。来。

**霍纳.** 这位夫人把最后一件拿走了。

===Original===
Lady Fidget
Yes indeed, madam, to my certain knowledge, he has no more left.

Mrs. Squeamish
O, but it may be he may have some you could not find.

Lady Fidget
What, d’ye think if he had had any left, I would not have had it too? for we women of quality never think we have china enough.

Horner
Do not take it ill, I cannot make china for you all, but I will have a roll-wagon for you too, another time.

===Chinese===
**菲吉特夫人.** 确实不假，夫人，我拿得准，他一件也不剩了。

**斯奎米什太太.** 哦，保不齐还有您没搜出来的呢。

**菲吉特夫人.** 什么，您以为他要是还剩下一件，我会不拿下？我们上等女人，从来不嫌瓷器够多。

**霍纳.** 别见怪，我没法子给你们人人烧出瓷器来；改日我再给你备一只罗尔瓦根大酒杯（roll-wagon）罢。

===Original===
Mrs. Squeamish
Thank you, dear toad.

Lady Fidget
What do you mean by that promise? *Aside to **Horner**.*

Horner
Alas, she has an innocent, literal understanding. *Aside to **Lady Fidget**.*

===Chinese===
**斯奎米什太太.** 多谢了，亲爱的癞蛤蟆。

**菲吉特夫人.**（向霍纳旁白）你这许诺是什么意思？

**霍纳.**（向菲吉特夫人旁白）唉，她这人心眼实，只认得字面儿。

===Original===
Lady Squeamish
Poor Mr. Horner! he has enough to do to please you all, I see.

Horner
Ay, madam, you see how they use me.

Lady Squeamish
Poor gentleman, I pity you.

Horner
I thank you, madam: I could never find pity, but from such reverend ladies as you are; the young ones will never spare a man.

===Chinese===
**老斯奎米什夫人.** 可怜的霍纳先生！瞧，要讨好你们全体，他真够忙的。

**霍纳.** 是啊，夫人，您瞧她们是怎么使唤我的。

**老斯奎米什夫人.** 可怜的先生，我真怜惜您。

**霍纳.** 多谢您，夫人：肯怜惜我的，从来只有您这样年高德劭的夫人；年轻的，是从来不肯饶人的。

===Original===
Mrs. Squeamish
Come, come, beast, and go dine with us; for we shall want a man at ombre after dinner.

Horner
That’s all their use of me, madam, you see.

Mrs. Squeamish
Come, sloven, I’ll lead you, to be sure of you. *Pulls him by the cravat.*

Lady Squeamish
Alas, poor man, how she tugs him! Kiss, kiss her; that’s the way to make such nice women quiet.

Horner
No, madam, that remedy is worse than the torment; they know I dare suffer anything rather than do it.

Lady Squeamish
Prithee kiss her, and I’ll give you her picture in little, that you admired so last night; prithee do.

===Chinese===
**斯奎米什太太.** 来罢，来罢，丑八怪，跟我们去用饭；饭后打奥姆布雷牌（ombre），还缺一位男士呢。

**霍纳.** 夫人您瞧，她们要我，用处全在这上头了。

**斯奎米什太太.** 来，懒汉，我牵着你走，免得你溜了。（拽住他的领巾。）

**老斯奎米什夫人.** 哎呀，可怜的人，她拽得好狠！亲她，亲她一个；对付这种娇滴滴的女人，一亲就安生了。

**霍纳.** 不行，夫人，那剂药比病还凶；她们知道我什么苦都受得，就是不肯干这个。

**老斯奎米什夫人.** 求你亲她罢，我就把她那张袖珍小像送您，您昨晚不是爱得不得了么；求您了。

===Original===
Horner
Well, nothing but that could bribe me: I love a woman only in effigy, and good painting as much as I hate them.—I’ll do’t, for I could adore the devil well painted. *Kisses **Mrs. Squeamish**.*

Mrs. Squeamish
Foh, you filthy toad! nay, now I’ve done jesting.

Lady Squeamish
Ha! ha I ha! I told you so.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 好罢，能说动我的，也就这一样了：我爱女人，只爱画里的；好丹青我有多少爱，活女人我就有多少恨。——我亲；画得好的魔鬼，我都肯拜。（吻斯奎米什太太。）

**斯奎米什太太.** 呸，你这脏癞蛤蟆！好了，玩笑我可开够了。

**老斯奎米什夫人.** 哈！哈！哈！我说什么来着。

===Original===
Mrs. Squeamish
Foh! a kiss of his—

Sir Jasper
Has no more hurt in’t than one of my spaniel’s.

Mrs. Squeamish
Nor no more good neither.

Quack
I will now believe anything he tells me. *Aside.*

===Chinese===
**斯奎米什太太.** 呸！他这个吻——

**贾斯珀爵士.** 同我那条西班牙猎犬舔一口，多不出半点害处。

**斯奎米什太太.** 也多不出半点好处。

**庸医.**（旁白）从这会儿起，他跟我说什么我都信了。

===Original===
*Enter **Pinchwife**.*

Lady Fidget
O lord, here’s a man! Sir Jasper, my mask, my mask! I would not be seen here for the world.

Sir Jasper
What, not when I am with you?

Lady Fidget
No, no, my honour—let’s be gone.

Mrs. Squeamish
Oh grandmother, let’s be gone; make haste, make haste, I know not how he may censure us.

Lady Fidget
Be found in the lodging of anything like a man!—Away.

*Exeunt **Sir Jasper Fidget**, **Lady Fidget**, **Old Lady Squeamish**, and **Mrs. Squeamish**.*

===Chinese===
*（**平奇怀夫**上。）*

**菲吉特夫人.** 哎呀，来了个男人！贾斯珀爵士，我的面纱，我的面纱！打死我也不能叫人瞧见我在这儿。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 怎么，有我陪着也不成？

**菲吉特夫人.** 不成，不成，我的荣誉——快走。

**斯奎米什太太.** 哦，奶奶，快走；赶紧，赶紧，天知道他会怎么编排咱们。

**菲吉特夫人.** 叫人撞见待在一个还算得上男人的窝里！——走。

*（贾斯珀·菲吉特爵士、菲吉特夫人、老斯奎米什夫人与斯奎米什太太同下。）*

===Original===
Quack
What’s here? another cuckold? he looks like one, and none else sure have any business with him. *Aside.*

Horner
Well, what brings my dear friend hither?

Pinchwife
Your impertinency.

Horner
My impertinency!—why, you gentlemen that have got handsome wives, think you have a privilege of saying anything to your friends, and are as brutish as if you were our creditors.

Pinchwife
No, sir, I’ll ne’er trust you anyway.

Horner
But why not, dear Jack? why diffide in me thou know’st so well?

===Chinese===
**庸医.**（旁白）这又是哪位？又一个绿帽汉？瞧那模样就像；再说，除了绿帽汉，谁还会有事找他这儿来。

**霍纳.** 喂，什么风把我的亲爱的朋友吹来了？

**平奇怀夫.** 您的放肆。

**霍纳.** 我的放肆！——嗐，你们这些娶了标致太太的爷们，自以为对朋友开口闭口都有特权，粗鲁得像是我们的债主。

**平奇怀夫.** 不，先生，我怎么也不信您。

**霍纳.** 可为什么不呢，亲爱的杰克？你这么知我的底细，怎么倒信不过我？

===Original===
Pinchwife
Because I do know you so well.

Horner
Han’t I been always thy friend, honest Jack, always ready to serve thee, in love or battle, before thou wert married, and am so still?

Pinchwife
I believe so, you would be my second now, indeed.

Horner
Well then, dear Jack, why so unkind, so grum, so strange to me? Come, prithee kiss me, dear rogue: gad I was always, I say, and am still as much thy servant as—

Pinchwife
As I am yours, sir. What, you would send a kiss to my wife, is that it?

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 就因为我太知您的底细。

**霍纳.** 我难道不一直是你的朋友，老实的杰克？你没成亲那会儿，情场也罢战场也罢，我随时听你差遣；如今不也一样？

**平奇怀夫.** 我信：如今你倒真乐意给我当决斗副手了。

**霍纳.** 好了，亲爱的杰克，干吗这么不客气，这么闷沉沉，这么见外？来，亲我一个，亲爱的家伙：老天，我一直、我说、如今也还是你的忠仆，就像——

**平奇怀夫.** 就像我当您的忠仆一样，先生。怎么，您想往我太太那儿捎个吻，是这意思罢？

===Original===
Horner
So, there ’tis—a man can’t show his friendship to a married man, but presently he talks of his wife to you. Prithee, let thy wife alone, and let thee and I be all one, as we were wont. What, thou art as shy of my kindness, as a Lombard-street alderman of a courtier’s civility at Locket’s!12

Pinchwife
But you are over-kind to me, as kind as if I were your cuckold already; yet I must confess you ought to be kind and civil to me, since I am so kind, so civil to you, as to bring you this: look you there, sir. *Delivers him a letter.*

Horner
What is’t?

Pinchwife
Only a love-letter, sir.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 瞧，来了——对结了婚的人表示点交情，他立马跟你提他太太。行行好，撇开你太太，你我照旧亲如一人。嗐，你躲我的好意，躲得比朗伯德街（Lombard-street）的市政官在洛克特馆（Locket’s）躲朝臣的客套还急！12

**平奇怀夫.** 可您对我亲热过了头，亲热得像我已然是您的绿帽汉了；不过我承认，您对我确实该又亲又敬，因为我也够亲够敬，给您捎来了这个：您请过目，先生。（递给他一封信。）

**霍纳.** 这是什么？

**平奇怀夫.** 不过是一封情书，先生。

===Original===
Horner
From whom?—how! this is from your wife—hum—and hum—*Reads.*

Pinchwife
Even from my wife, sir: am I not wondrous kind and civil to you now too?—*Aside.* But you’ll not think her so.

Horner
Ha! is this a trick of his or hers? *Aside.*

Pinchwife
The gentleman’s surprised I find.—What, you expected a kinder letter?

Horner
No faith, not I, how could I?

Pinchwife
Yes, yes, I’m sure you did. A man so well made as you are, must needs be disappointed, if the women declare not their passion at first sight or opportunity.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 谁写的？——咦！这是你太太的笔迹——嗯——嗯——（读。）

**平奇怀夫.** 正是我太太的，先生：瞧我这会儿对您，还不够亲、不够敬么？——（旁白）不过您就不会这么看她了。

**霍纳.** 哈！这是他捣的鬼，还是她捣的鬼？（旁白）

**平奇怀夫.** 瞧这位先生愣了。——怎么，您原指望一封更温存的？

**霍纳.** 说真的，不，我哪能？

**平奇怀夫.** 是，是，我拿得准您指望过。像您这样一副好皮囊的男人，女人们若不趁初见或一有机会就表明热情，您准得失望。

===Original===
Horner
*Aside.* But what should this mean? Stay, the postscript.—*Reads aside.* “Be sure you love me, whatsoever my husband says to the contrary, and let him not see this, lest he should come home and pinch me, or kill my squirrel.”—It seems he knows not what the letter contains.

Pinchwife
Come, ne’er wonder at it so much.

Horner
Faith, I can’t help it.

Pinchwife
Now, I think I have deserved your infinite friendship and kindness, and have showed myself sufficiently an obliging kind friend and husband; am I not so, to bring a letter from my wife to her gallant?

Horner
Ay, the devil take me, art thou, the most obliging, kind friend and husband in the world, ha! ha!

===Chinese===
**霍纳.**（旁白）可这是什么意思？慢着，有附言。——（旁白读信。）「你千万爱我，不管我丈夫说什么反话；也别叫他瞧见这信，免得他回家来掐我，或者弄死我的松鼠。」——看来信里写了什么，他还蒙在鼓里。

**平奇怀夫.** 得了，别这么大惊小怪。

**霍纳.** 说真的，忍不住。

**平奇怀夫.** 这下，我想我不愧领您那份无穷的友爱与厚谊了，也足以表明我是个多够意思的朋友兼丈夫了；把太太的情书亲手捎给她的情人，难道还不是么？

**霍纳.** 是啊，叫我给魔鬼抓去，你真是天底下顶够意思、顶和气的朋友兼丈夫，哈！哈！

===Original===
Pinchwife
Well, you may be merry, sir; but in short I must tell you, sir, my honour will suffer no jesting.

Horner
What dost thou mean?

Pinchwife
Does the letter want a comment? Then, know, sir, though I have been so civil a husband, as to bring you a letter from my wife, to let you kiss and court her to my face, I will not be a cuckold, sir, I will not.

Horner
Thou art mad with jealousy. I never saw thy wife in my life but at the play yesterday, and I know not if it were she or no. I court her, kiss her!

Pinchwife
I will not be a cuckold, I say; there will be danger in making me a cuckold.

Horner
Why, wert thou not well cured of thy last clap?

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 你尽可以乐，先生；可长话短说，先生，我的荣誉开不得玩笑。

**霍纳.** 你这话什么意思？

**平奇怀夫.** 信还需要注解么？那么，先生，听着：我纵然客气到把太太的信给您送来、当着我的面任您亲她追她，我也不当绿帽汉，先生，我不当。

**霍纳.** 你醋疯了。我平生除昨天戏园子里那回，压根没见过你太太；就连那是她不是她，我也说不准。我追她，亲她！

**平奇怀夫.** 我再说一遍，我不当绿帽汉；谁要叫我当上，可得担着风险。

**霍纳.** 怎么，你上回那场淋病没治利索？

===Original===
Pinchwife
I wear a sword.

Horner
It should be taken from thee, lest thou shouldst do thyself a mischief with it; thou art mad, man.

Pinchwife
As mad as I am, and as merry as you are, I must have more reason from you ere we part. I say again, though you kissed and courted last night my wife in man’s clothes, as she confesses in her letter—

Horner
Ha! *Aside.*

Pinchwife
Both she and I say, you must not design it again, for you have mistaken your woman, as you have done your man.

Horner
*Aside.* O—I understand something now—*Aloud.* Was that thy wife! Why wouldst thou not tell me ’twas she? Faith, my freedom with her was your fault, not mine.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 我身上带着剑呢。

**霍纳.** 该叫人把剑收走，免得你拿它伤了自己；你疯了，老兄。

**平奇怀夫.** 我再疯，你再乐，分手之前，你也得给我个说法。我再说一遍：昨晚你亲了、追了那男装的我太太——她信里也招认了——

**霍纳.** 哈！（旁白）

**平奇怀夫.** 她和我都说了：这种念头，您趁早收了；您认错了女人，正如您认错了男人。

**霍纳.**（旁白）哦——我这会儿明白了几分——（高声）那是你太太！你怎么不早告诉我？说真的，我对她放肆，怪你，不怪我。

===Original===
Pinchwife
Faith, so ’twas. *Aside.*

Horner
Fy! I’d never do’t to a woman before her husband’s face, sure.

Pinchwife
But I had rather you should do’t to my wife before my face, than behind my back; and that you shall never do.

Horner
No—you will hinder me.

Pinchwife
If I would not hinder you, you see by her letter she would.

Horner
Well, I must e’en acquiesce then, and be contented with what she writes.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 说真的，是怪我。（旁白）

**霍纳.** 呸！当着丈夫的面，我对女人可干不出那个，真的。

**平奇怀夫.** 可你当着我的面对我太太干，总好过背着我干；背着我，你永远干不成。

**霍纳.** 是啊——有你拦着呢。

**平奇怀夫.** 我就算不拦，你瞧她那信，她也要拦。

**霍纳.** 好罢，那我只有俯首遵命，她信里怎么写，我就怎么受着。

===Original===
Pinchwife
I’ll assure you ’twas voluntarily writ; I had no hand in’t you may believe me.

Horner
I do believe thee, faith.

Pinchwife
And believe her too, for she’s an innocent creature, has no dissembling in her: and so fare you well, sir.

Horner
Pray, however, present my humble service to her, and tell her, I will obey her letter to a tittle, and fulfil her desires, be what they will, or with what difficulty soever I do’t; and you shall be no more jealous of me, I warrant her, and you.

Pinchwife
Well then, fare you well; and play with any man’s honour but mine, kiss any man’s wife but mine, and welcome.

*Exit.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 我向您担保，这信是她自个儿情愿写的；我半个字没沾，您信我。

**霍纳.** 我信，说真的。

**平奇怀夫.** 也信她罢，她是个天真的造物，心里没有一点虚饰：那么再会了，先生。

**霍纳.** 只求您替我向她致以卑微的敬意，告诉她：她信上的吩咐，我一字不差地遵从；她的心愿我都照办，不管是什么心愿，也不管有多难办；叫她放心，也叫您放心，您再不会为我吃醋了，我担保。

**平奇怀夫.** 好罢，那么再会；谁的荣誉您都尽管玩去，只别玩我的；谁的太太您都尽管亲去，欢迎之至，只别亲我的。

*（下。）*

===Original===
Horner
Ha! ha! ha! doctor.

Quack
It seems, he has not heard the report of you, or does not believe it.

Horner
Ha! ha!—now, doctor, what think you?

Quack
Pray let’s see the letter—hum—“for—dear—love you—” *Reads the letter.*

Horner
I wonder how she could contrive it! What say’st thou to’t? ’tis an original.

Quack
So are your cuckolds too originals: for they are like no other common cuckolds, and I will henceforth believe it not impossible for you to cuckold the Grand Signior amidst his guards of eunuchs, that I say.

Horner
And I say for the letter, ’tis the first love-letter that ever was without flames, darts, fates, destinies, lying and dissembling in’t.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 哈！哈！哈！大夫。

**庸医.** 看来满城传您的那个话，他要么没听见，要么不信。

**霍纳.** 哈！哈！——怎么样，大夫，您有何高见？

**庸医.** 劳驾，信给我瞧瞧——嗯——「为——亲爱的——爱你——」（读信。）

**霍纳.** 我真纳闷，她怎么想出的这个主意！你说如何？独一份的原创。

**庸医.** 您那些绿帽汉，也都是原创：他们同寻常的绿帽汉没一个相像的；从今往后，您就是当着满朝阉人卫队，给苏丹大王（Grand Signior）戴上绿帽，我也信您办得到——我说的。

**霍纳.** 我呢，说这信是有史以来头一封里头不带火焰、飞箭、命数、宿缘、谎话和虚饰的情书。

===Original===
*Enter **Sparkish** pulling in **Pinchwife**.*

Sparkish
Come back, you are a pretty brother-in-law, neither go to church nor to dinner with your sister bride!

Pinchwife
My sister denies her marriage, and you see is gone away from you dissatisfied.

Sparkish
Pshaw! upon a foolish scruple, that our parson was not in lawful orders, and did not say all the common-prayer; but ’tis her modesty only I believe. But let all women be never so modest the first day, they’ll be sure to come to themselves by night, and I shall have enough of her then. In the meantime, Harry Horner, you must dine with me: I keep my wedding at my aunt’s in the Piazza.13

===Chinese===
*（**斯帕基什**拽着**平奇怀夫**上。）*

**斯帕基什.** 回来回来，你这算哪门子内兄！妹妹做了新娘，你教堂也不进，喜酒也不吃！

**平奇怀夫.** 我妹妹不认这门亲事，你瞧，她这就撂下你走了。

**斯帕基什.** 啐！为个糊涂疑心，嫌咱们的牧师圣职不合法，《公祷书》没念周全；依我看，不过是她害臊罢了。可随她们头一天多害臊，到夜里保管就回过神来，那时节有我受用的。眼下嘛，哈利·霍纳，你务必来陪我吃饭：喜酒摆在我姑母家，就在皮亚扎广场（the Piazza）13。

===Original===
Horner
Thy wedding! what stale maid has lived to despair of a husband, or what young one of a gallant?

Sparkish
O, your servant, sir—this gentleman’s sister then—no stale maid.

Horner
I’m sorry for’t.

Pinchwife
How comes he so concerned for her? *Aside.*

Sparkish
You sorry for’t? why, do you know any ill by her?

Horner
No, I know none but by thee; ’tis for her sake, not yours, and another man’s sake that might have hoped, I thought.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 你的喜酒！是哪一位老姑娘活到今天、对嫁人死了心，还是哪一位小娇娘对情人死了心？

**斯帕基什.** 哦，您哪，先生——是这位先生的妹妹——可不是老姑娘。

**霍纳.** 那我可要可惜了。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）他怎么这么惦记她？

**斯帕基什.** 你可惜？怎么，你知道她什么短处？

**霍纳.** 不，短处我只知道你的；我可惜是为她，不是为你——还为另一个本来有指望的人，我这么想。

===Original===
Sparkish
Another man! another man! what is his name?

Horner
Nay, since ’tis past, he shall be nameless.—*Aside.* Poor Harcourt! I am sorry thou hast missed her.

Pinchwife
He seems to be much troubled at the match. *Aside.*

Sparkish
Prithee, tell me—Nay, you shan’t go, brother.

Pinchwife
I must of necessity, but I’ll come to you to dinner.

*Exit.*

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 另一个人！另一个人！他叫什么？

**霍纳.** 嗐，事已至此，就不提名了。——（旁白）可怜的哈考特！可惜你错过了她。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）瞧他，为这门亲事好不懊恼。

**斯帕基什.** 求你告诉我——别忙，内兄，你走不得。

**平奇怀夫.** 我实在有事；可我准来吃你的喜酒。

*（下。）*

===Original===
Sparkish
But, Harry, what, have I a rival in my wife already? But with all my heart, for he may be of use to me hereafter; for though my hunger is now my sauce, and I can fall on heartily without, the time will come, when a rival will be as good sauce for a married man to a wife, as an orange to veal.

Horner
O thou damned rogue! thou hast set my teeth on edge with thy orange.

Sparkish
Then let’s to dinner—there I was with you again. Come.

Horner
But who dines with thee?

Sparkish
My friends and relations, my brother Pinchwife, you see, of your acquaintance.

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 可是，哈利，怎么，我老婆还没进门，我就先有了情敌？不过我求之不得，往后他于我大有用处：眼下肚子饿就是我的调料，不用调料也吃得香；可将来有一天，对一个娶了老婆的男人，情敌就是一味好调料，就像小牛肉配橙子。

**霍纳.** 哦，你这该死的家伙！你这橙子，把我一口牙都酸倒了。

**斯帕基什.** 那就吃饭去——瞧，这下又同你平了一局。来。

**霍纳.** 可都有谁陪你吃？

**斯帕基什.** 我的亲友呗；还有内兄平奇怀夫，你瞧，也是你相熟的。

===Original===
Horner
And his wife?

Sparkish
No, ’gad, he’ll ne’er let her come amongst us good fellows; your stingy country coxcomb keeps his wife from his friends, as he does his little firkin of ale, for his own drinking, and a gentleman can’t get a smack on’t; but his servants, when his back is turned, broach it at their pleasures, and dust it away, ha! ha! ha!—’Gad, I am witty, I think, considering I was married today, by the world; but come—

Horner
No, I will not dine with you, unless you can fetch her too.

Sparkish
Pshaw! what pleasure canst thou have with women now, Harry?

Horner
My eyes are not gone; I love a good prospect yet, and will not dine with you unless she does too; go fetch her, therefore, but do not tell her husband ’tis for my sake.

Sparkish
Well, I’ll go try what I can do; in the meantime, come away to my aunt’s lodging, ’tis in the way to Pinchwife’s.

Horner
The poor woman has called for aid, and stretched forth her hand, doctor; I cannot but help her over the pale out of the briars.

*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 还有他太太？

**斯帕基什.** 不，老天，他才不肯放她到我们这些好哥们儿中间来呢；你那号小气的乡下草包，护着老婆不叫朋友沾边，就像护着他自饮的那一小桶麦酒，爷们连一口都蹭不着；可他前脚一转背，仆人们后脚就开了桶，喝个底朝天，哈！哈！哈！——老天，瞧我今天成的亲，居然还有这份俏皮，凭全世界起誓；来罢——

**霍纳.** 不成，你把她也请来，我才去吃。

**斯帕基什.** 啐！你如今还能从女人身上得什么乐子，哈利？

**霍纳.** 我的眼睛还没瞎；我还爱看好景致；她不来，我就不去；去请她罢，只是别告诉她男人，说是为我。

**斯帕基什.** 好，我去试试我的能耐；这会儿你先到我姑母的寓所来，顺路，正挨着去平奇怀夫家的道。

**霍纳.** 那可怜的女人既然呼了救、伸出了手，大夫，我不能不拉她翻过栅栏，逃出荆棘丛了。

*（同下。）*

### Scene IV / 第四场

===Original===
A room in **Pinchwife’s** house.

***Mrs. Pinchwife** alone, leaning on her elbow.—A table, pen, ink and paper.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
Well, ’tis e’en so, I have got the London disease they call love; I am sick of my husband, and for my gallant. I have heard this distemper called a fever, but methinks ’tis like an ague; for when I think of my husband, I tremble, and am in a cold sweat, and have inclinations to vomit; but when I think of my gallant, dear Mr. Horner, my hot fit comes, and I am all in a fever indeed; and, as in other fevers, my own chamber is tedious to me, and I would fain be removed to his, and then methinks I should be well. Ah, poor Mr. Horner! Well, I cannot, will not stay here; therefore I’ll make an end of my letter to him, which shall be a finer letter than my last, because I have studied it like anything. Oh sick, sick! *Takes the pen and writes.*

===Chinese===
平奇怀夫家中一室。

*（**平奇怀夫太太**独在场上，支着手肘。——桌上有笔、墨、纸。）*

**平奇怀夫太太.** 嗐，事到如今，就是这么回事了：我染上了他们唤作「爱情」的伦敦病；我男人叫我发腻，我的情人叫我惦记。听人把这毛病叫热病，可我看它像疟疾：一想到我男人，我就打颤、冒冷汗、直想吐；可一想到我的情人、亲爱的霍纳先生，热劲儿就上来，当真浑身滚烫；而且同别的热病一个样，自个儿屋里待着嫌闷，一心想挪到他的屋里去，一挪过去，我想就百病全消了。啊，可怜的霍纳先生！好了，我不能、也不肯再守在这儿；这就把给他的信写完罢；这回要写得比上封还漂亮，因为我下死劲琢磨过的。哎，难受，难受！（执笔写。）

===Original===
*Enter **Pinchwife**, who seeing her writing, steals softly behind her and looking over her shoulder, snatches the paper from her.*

Pinchwife
What, writing more letters?

Mrs. Pinchwife
O Lord, bud, why d’ye fright me so? *She offers to run out; he stops her, and reads.*

===Chinese===
*（**平奇怀夫**上，见她写信，蹑手蹑脚绕到她身后，越过她肩头一把抽走信纸。）*

**平奇怀夫.** 怎么，又写信？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 哎呀老天，乖乖，你吓死我了！（她要夺门而出；他拦住，读信。）

===Original===
Pinchwife
How’s this? nay, you shall not stir, madam:—“Dear, dear, dear Mr. Horner”—very well—I have taught you to write letters to good purpose—but let us see’t. “First, I am to beg your pardon for my boldness in writing to you, which I’d have you to know I would not have done, had not you said first you loved me so extremely, which if you do, you will never suffer me to lie in the arms of another man whom I loathe, nauseate, and detest.”—Now you can write these filthy words. But what follows?—“Therefore, I hope you will speedily find some way to free me from this unfortunate match, which was never, I assure you, of my choice, but I’m afraid ’tis already too far gone; however, if you love me, as I do you, you will try what you can do; but you must help me away before tomorrow, or else, alas! I shall be forever out of your reach, for I can defer no longer our—our—” what is to follow “our”?—speak, what—our journey into the country I suppose—Oh woman, damned woman! and Love, damned Love, their old tempter! for this is one of his miracles; in a moment he can make those blind that could see, and those see that were blind, those dumb that could speak, and those prattle who were dumb before; nay, what is more than all, make these dough-baked, senseless, indocile animals, women, too hard for us their politic lords and rulers, in a moment. But make an end of your letter, and then I’ll make an end of you thus, and all my plagues together. *Draws his sword.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 这是怎么说的？慢着，夫人，你一步也别想挪：——「亲爱的、亲爱的、亲爱的霍纳先生」——好得很——我教你写信，教得真有出息——咱们接着瞧。「第一，我要请您原谅我写信的冒昧；您得知道，若不是您先说了爱我爱到极处，我原不肯写的；您要真爱我，就断不能忍心叫我躺在另一个男人的怀里——那男人我厌恶、恶心、憎恨。」——这种脏字眼儿，你倒写得出。底下还有什么？——「所以，我盼望您快快寻个法子，把我从这门不幸的亲事里解救出去；您放心，这门亲事从来不是我自个儿挑的；只怕已经闹得太深，不好回头了；可您要是爱我，像我爱您一样，总要想法子试试；您得赶明天以前带我逃走，不然，唉！我就永远够不着您了，因为我再拖不得了，咱们的——咱们的——」「咱们的」底下是什么？——说，什么——我猜是「咱们的下乡行程」罢——哦，女人，该死的女人！还有爱神，该死的爱神，他们家祖传的教唆犯！这是他的奇迹之一：一眨眼，他叫明眼的瞎了，瞎眼的明了；会说话的哑了，原先哑的倒叽叽喳喳；还不止呢，顶厉害的是，一眨眼，他叫这些半生不熟、没头没脑、教不会的畜生——女人——反倒斗赢了咱们这些老谋深算的君主和主宰。快把信收了尾，我好这样把你——连同我这一身祸害——一并了断。（拔剑。）

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
O Lord, O Lord, you are such a passionate man, bud!

*Enter **Sparkish**.*

Sparkish
How now, what’s here to do?

Pinchwife
This fool here now!

Sparkish
What! drawn upon your wife? You should never do that, but at night in the dark, when you can’t hurt her. This is my sister-in-law, is it not? ay, faith, e’en our country Margery; *Pulls aside her handkerchief* one may know her. Come, she and you must go dine with me; dinner’s ready, come. But where’s my wife? is she not come home yet? where is she?

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 哎呀老天，哎呀老天，你这人火气也太大了，乖乖！

*（**斯帕基什**上。）*

**斯帕基什.** 喂，这是怎么了？

**平奇怀夫.** 这傻子偏偏挑这时候来！

**斯帕基什.** 什么！拔剑对着你太太？这种事千万做不得——除非夜里摸黑，那还伤她不着。这位是咱们那位姻嫂，是不是？可不是嘛，真真是咱们乡下的玛格丽；（掀开她的头巾）一搭眼就认得。来，她同你都得上我那儿吃饭；饭都齐了，走。可我太太呢？还没到家？人在哪儿？

===Original===
Pinchwife
Making you a cuckold; ’tis that they all do, as soon as they can.

Sparkish
What, the wedding-day? no, a wife that designs to make a cully of her husband will be sure to let him win the first stake of love, by the world. But come, they stay dinner for us: come, I’ll lead down our Margery.

Pinchwife
No—sir, go, we’ll follow you.

Sparkish
I will not wag without you.

Pinchwife
This coxcomb is a sensible torment to me amidst the greatest in the world. *Aside.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 正给你戴绿帽呢；她们个个都这样，一得空就干。

**斯帕基什.** 什么，成亲头一天？不会；存心叫丈夫当冤大头的老婆，头一注总保管叫丈夫赢，凭全世界起誓。来，他们还等着咱们开席呢：来，我牵咱们的玛格丽下楼。

**平奇怀夫.** 不——先生，你先去，我们随后就来。

**斯帕基什.** 你们不动身，我也不挪窝。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）在我天大的苦难中间，这草包又是一桩活受罪。

===Original===
Sparkish
Come, come, Madam Margery.

Pinchwife
No; I’ll lead her my way: what, would you treat your friends with mine, for want of your own wife?—*Leads her to the other door, and locks her in and returns.* I am contented my rage should take breath—*Aside.*

Sparkish
I told Horner this.

Pinchwife
Come now.

Sparkish
Lord, how shy you are of your wife! but let me tell you, brother, we men of wit have amongst us a saying, that cuckolding, like the smallpox, comes with a fear; and you may keep your wife as much as you will out of danger of infection, but if her constitution incline her to’t, she’ll have it sooner or later, by the world, say they.

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 来嘛，来嘛，玛格丽小姐。

**平奇怀夫.** 不成；要牵，也由我打我这边牵：怎么，自家老婆脱了手，就拿我的人去款待你的朋友么？——（把她领到另一道门，锁进屋去，转身回来。）正好叫我这腔怒火喘口气——（旁白）

**斯帕基什.** 我跟霍纳就是这么说的。

**平奇怀夫.** 这就走。

**斯帕基什.** 天哪，你可真把老婆看得紧！不过我告诉你，内兄，我们这些才子中间有句行话：戴绿帽这东西，同天花一样，越怕越来；你尽可以把老婆隔得远远的，不叫她沾着传染的边儿；可她那体质里要是带着，早晚要发出来，凭全世界起誓——他们这么说。

===Original===
Pinchwife
*Aside.* What a thing is a cuckold, that every fool can make him ridiculous!—*Aloud.* Well, sir—but let me advise you, now you are come to be concerned, because you suspect the danger, not to neglect the means to prevent it, especially when the greatest share of the malady will light upon your own head, for

Hows’e’er the kind wife’s belly comes to swell,
The husband breeds for her, and first is ill.

*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）绿帽汉算个什么东西！连个个傻子都能拿他打趣。——（高声）好了，先生——不过听我一句劝：你既起了疑心、担上了这风险，就别疏忽了防备的法子；何况这病的大头，总要落在你自个儿头上，因为——

不管贤妻肚皮为谁鼓，
怀胎害病的，先是她丈夫。

*（同下。）*

## Act V / 第五幕

### Scene I / 第一场

===Original===
**Pinchwife’s** House.

*Enter **Pinchwife** and **Mrs. Pinchwife**. A table and candle.*

Pinchwife
Come, take the pen and make an end of the letter, just as you intended; if you are false in a tittle, I shall soon perceive it, and punish you as you deserve.—*Lays his hand on his sword.* Write what was to follow—let’s see—“You must make haste, and help me away before tomorrow, or else I shall be forever out of your reach, for I can defer no longer our”—What follows “our”?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Must all out, then, bud?—Look you there, then. ***Mrs. Pinchwife** takes the pen and writes.*

Pinchwife
Let’s see—“For I can defer no longer our—wedding—Your slighted Alithea.”—What’s the meaning of this? my sister’s name to’t? speak, unriddle.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Yes, indeed, bud.

===Chinese===
平奇怀夫家。

*（**平奇怀夫**与**平奇怀夫太太**上。一桌一烛。）*

**平奇怀夫.** 来，拿笔，把信写完，一个字也照你原先的意思写；你但凡有一丝虚饰，我立刻瞧得出来，照你应得的罚你。（手按剑柄。）把底下该写的写出来——我瞧瞧——「你得赶紧，赶明天以前带我逃走，不然我就永远够不着你了，因为我再拖不得了，咱们的」——「咱们的」底下是什么？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 都得抖出来么，乖乖？——那您就瞧罢。（平奇怀夫太太执笔写。）

**平奇怀夫.** 我瞧瞧——「因为我再拖不得了，咱们的——婚礼——你那受了冷落的阿莉西娅。」——这是什么意思？署的是我妹妹的名字？说，给我把这谜解开。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 是，真的是，乖乖。

===Original===
Pinchwife
But why her name to’t? speak—speak, I say.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Ay, but you’ll tell her then again. If you would not tell her again—

Pinchwife
I will not:—I am stunned, my head turns round.—Speak.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Won’t you tell her, indeed, and indeed?

Pinchwife
No; speak, I say.

Mrs. Pinchwife
She’ll be angry with me; but I had rather she should be angry with me than you, bud; and, to tell you the truth, ’twas she made me write the letter, and taught me what I should write.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 可为什么署她的名字？说——我说，说。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 是呀，可你回头又要去告诉她。你要是不告诉她——

**平奇怀夫.** 不告诉：——我懵了，头直打转。——说。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 你真不告诉她？真的真的？

**平奇怀夫.** 不告诉；我说，说。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 她要恼我的；可我宁可她恼我，也不愿你恼我，乖乖；况且说句实话，正是她叫我写的这信，还一句一句教了我。

===Original===
Pinchwife
*Aside.* Ha!—I thought the style was somewhat better than her own.—*Aloud.* Could she come to you to teach you, since I had locked you up alone?

Mrs. Pinchwife
O, through the keyhole, bud.

Pinchwife
But why should she make you write a letter for her to him, since she can write herself?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Why, she said because—for I was unwilling to do it—

Pinchwife
Because what—because?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Because, lest Mr. Horner should be cruel, and refuse her; or be vain afterwards, and show the letter, she might disown it, the hand not being hers.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）哈！——我原觉得这文笔比她自个儿的高明些。——（高声）我把你独个儿锁在屋里，她怎么能进来教你？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 哦，打钥匙孔里教的，乖乖。

**平奇怀夫.** 可她自个儿会写字，干吗要你替她给他写信？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 嗐，她说因为——我原本是不肯写的——

**平奇怀夫.** 因为什么——因为？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 因为，怕霍纳先生狠心回绝了她；或是日后得意忘形，拿信给人看，她还能不认账——笔迹反正不是她的。

===Original===
Pinchwife
*Aside.* How’s this? Ha!—then I think I shall come to myself again.—This changeling could not invent this lie: but if she could, why should she? she might think I should soon discover it.—Stay—now I think on’t too, Horner said he was sorry she had married Sparkish; and her disowning her marriage to me makes me think she has evaded it for Horner’s sake: yet why should she take this course? But men in love are fools; women may well be so—*Aloud.* But hark you, madam, your sister went out in the morning, and I have not seen her within since.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Alack-a-day, she has been crying all day above, it seems, in a corner.

Pinchwife
Where is she? let me speak with her.

Mrs. Pinchwife
*Aside.* O Lord, then she’ll discover all!—*Aloud.* Pray hold, bud; what, d’ye mean to discover me? she’ll know I have told you then. Pray, bud, let me talk with her first.

Pinchwife
I must speak with her, to know whether Horner ever made her any promise, and whether she be married to Sparkish or no.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Pray, dear bud, don’t, till I have spoken with her, and told her that I have told you all; for she’ll kill me else.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）这是怎么说的？哈！——那我看我该回魂了。——这个傻丫头编不出这样的谎：可她要真编得出，又图什么呢？她该想得到我很快就会戳穿。——慢着——这会儿我也想起来了，霍纳说过，他可惜她嫁了斯帕基什；她在我跟前不认这门亲，叫我想，她是为了霍纳才躲掉的：可她何必走这条路子？不过恋爱中的男人是傻子；女人自然更可以是——（高声）不过听着，夫人，你妹妹一早出的门，打那以后我就没在屋里见过她。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 哎哟天，她哭了一整天呢，听说，在楼上一个犄角里。

**平奇怀夫.** 她在哪儿？叫她来见我。

**平奇怀夫太太.**（旁白）哎呀老天，那她可要全露馅了！——（高声）求你慢着，乖乖；怎么，你要把我抖搂出去？她就知道我把话都告诉你了。求你，乖乖，容我先同她说。

**平奇怀夫.** 我必须同她说话，好知道霍纳到底许没许过她什么诺言，她到底同斯帕基什成没成亲。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 求你了，好乖乖，先别去，等我同她说过、告诉她我把什么都告诉你了再去；不然她要杀了我的。

===Original===
Pinchwife
Go then, and bid her come out to me.

Mrs. Pinchwife
Yes, yes, bud.

Pinchwife
Let me see—*Pausing.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
*Aside.* I’ll go, but she is not within to come to him: I have just got time to know of Lucy her maid, who first set me on work, what lie I shall tell next; for I am e’en at my wit’s end.

*Exit.*

Pinchwife
Well, I resolve it, Horner shall have her: I’d rather give him my sister than lend him my wife; and such an alliance will prevent his pretensions to my wife, sure. I’ll make him of kin to her, and then he won’t care for her.

*Reenter **Mrs. Pinchwife**.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
O Lord, bud! I told you what anger you would make me with my sister.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 那去罢，叫她出来见我。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 好嘞，好嘞，乖乖。

**平奇怀夫.** 容我想想——（沉吟。）

**平奇怀夫太太.**（旁白）去是去，可她压根儿不在屋里，出不来见他：我刚好来得及去问问露西——她的贴身丫头，也是头一个撺掇我干这营生的——下一句谎该怎么编；我实在山穷水尽了。

*（下。）*

**平奇怀夫.** 好，我拿定主意了，霍纳就娶她罢：宁可以把妹妹送他，不把老婆借他；结上这么门亲，保管断了他对我老婆的想头。我叫他成了她的亲戚，他自然不把她放在心上了。

*（**平奇怀夫太太**又上。）*

**平奇怀夫太太.** 哎呀老天，乖乖！我说什么来着——你叫我惹下我姐姐了。

===Original===
Pinchwife
Won’t she come hither?

Mrs. Pinchwife
No, no. Lack-a-day, she’s ashamed to look you in the face: and she says, if you go in to her, she’ll run away downstairs, and shamefully go herself to Mr. Horner, who has promised her marriage, she says; and she will have no other, so she won’t.

Pinchwife
Did he so?—promise her marriage!—then she shall have no other. Go tell her so; and if she will come and discourse with me a little concerning the means, I will about it immediately. Go.—

*Exit **Mrs. Pinchwife**.*

Pinchwife
His estate is equal to Sparkish’s, and his extraction as much better than his, as his parts are; but my chief reason is, I’d rather be akin to him by the name of brother-in-law than that of cuckold.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 她不肯上这儿来？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 不来，不来。可惜哟，她臊得不敢瞧你的脸：还说，你要是进屋去，她就跑下楼，索性自个儿丢人现眼地上霍纳先生那儿去——照她的说法，霍纳先生许过她婚事；她非他不可，说什么也不肯另嫁。

**平奇怀夫.** 他这么样了？——许过她婚事！——那她就非他不可。去这样告诉她；她要是肯来，同我稍稍商量一下办法，我立马着手去办。去。——

*（**平奇怀夫太太**下。）*

**平奇怀夫.** 他的家产同斯帕基什的相当，出身却比他强得多，才具也一样强得多；可我头一层的理由是：同他结亲，我宁可占「内兄」这个名分，也不占「绿帽汉」那个名分。

===Original===
*Reenter **Mrs. Pinchwife**.*

Pinchwife
Well, what says she now?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Why, she says, she would only have you lead her to Horner’s lodging; with whom she first will discourse the matter before she talks with you, which yet she cannot do; for alack, poor creature, she says she can’t so much as look you in the face, therefore she’ll come to you in a mask. And you must excuse her, if she make you no answer to any question of yours, till you have brought her to Mr. Horner; and if you will not chide her, nor question her, she’ll come out to you immediately.

Pinchwife
Let her come: I will not speak a word to her, nor require a word from her.

===Chinese===
*（**平奇怀夫太太**又上。）*

**平奇怀夫.** 好，她这会儿怎么说？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 嗐，她说，只消你把她领到霍纳的寓所；她想先同他把事情谈妥，再同你谈——这会儿还谈不成；因为，可怜见的，她说她连瞧你一眼都不敢，所以要蒙着面纱来见你。还有，你把她带到霍纳先生那儿之前，你问什么她都不答，这个你得多担待；你只要不骂她、也不盘问她，她立马就出来见你。

**平奇怀夫.** 叫她来罢：我不对她说一个字，也不向她讨一个字。

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
Oh, I forgot: besides she says, she cannot look you in the face, though through a mask; therefore would desire you to put out the candle.

Pinchwife
I agree to all. Let her make haste.—There, ’tis out—

*Puts out the candle. Exit **Mrs. Pinchwife**.*

Pinchwife
My case is something better: I’d rather fight with Horner for not lying with my sister, than for lying with my wife; and of the two, I had rather find my sister too forward than my wife. I expected no other from her free education, as she calls it, and her passion for the town. Well, wife and sister are names which make us expect love and duty, pleasure and comfort; but we find ’em plagues and torments, and are equally, though differently, troublesome to their keeper; for we have as much ado to get people to lie with our sisters as to keep ’em from lying with our wives.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 哦，我忘了：她还说，就算隔着面纱，她也不敢瞧你的脸；所以求你把蜡烛灭了。

**平奇怀夫.** 我全依。叫她快些。——喏，灭了——

*（吹灭蜡烛。**平奇怀夫太太**下。）*

**平奇怀夫.** 我这情形算是好些了：宁可为了霍纳不肯睡我妹妹同他动剑，也别为了他睡了我老婆同他动剑；两下里比较，我宁可撞见妹妹太浪，也别撞见老婆不贞。她受的那种「自由教育」——她这么叫它——还有她迷这座城的那股热乎劲，早叫我料定如此了。唉，「妻子」和「妹妹」这两个名目，原叫人指望恩爱和恭顺、享乐和安慰；到头来却发现是灾殃和苦刑，虽各有不同，看守它们的人一样不得安生；因为叫人去睡咱们的妹妹，同拦着人别睡咱们的老婆，一样的费劲。

===Original===
*Reenter **Mrs. Pinchwife** masked, and in hoods and scarfs, and a nightgown and petticoat of **Alithea’s**.*

Pinchwife
What, are you come, sister? let us go then.—But first, let me lock up my wife. Mrs. Margery, where are you?

Mrs. Pinchwife
Here, bud.

Pinchwife
Come hither, that I may lock you up: get you in.—*Locks the door.* Come, sister, where are you now? ***Mrs. Pinchwife** gives him her hand; but when he lets her go, she steals softly on to the other side of him, and is led away by him for his Sister, **Alithea**.*

===Chinese===
*（**平奇怀夫太太**戴面纱又上，裹着头巾披肩，身上是**阿莉西娅**的晨衣和衬裙。）*

**平奇怀夫.** 怎么，妹妹，来了？那就走罢。——先容我把老婆锁起来。玛格丽太太，你在哪儿？

**平奇怀夫太太.** 在这儿呢，乖乖。

**平奇怀夫.** 过来，好锁你起来：进去罢。——（锁门。）来，妹妹，你这会儿在哪儿呢？（平奇怀夫太太把手递给他；可他一撒手，她就蹑手蹑脚溜到他的另一边，被他当作妹妹**阿莉西娅**领下场去。）

### Scene II / 第二场

===Original===
**Horner’s** Lodging.

***Horner** and **Quack**.*

Quack
What, all alone? not so much as one of your cuckolds here, nor one of their wives! They use to take their turns with you, as if they were to watch you.

Horner
Yes, it often happens that a cuckold is but his wife’s spy, and is more upon family duty when he is with her gallant abroad, hindering his pleasure, than when he is at home with her playing the gallant. But the hardest duty a married woman imposes upon a lover is keeping her husband company always.

Quack
And his fondness wearies you almost as soon as hers.

Horner
A pox! keeping a cuckold company, after you have had his wife, is as tiresome as the company of a country squire to a witty fellow of the town, when he has got all his money.

===Chinese===
霍纳的寓所。

*（**霍纳**与**庸医**在场上。）*

**庸医.** 怎么，独个儿？连你哪一位绿帽汉也不在，连他们哪一位太太也不在！他们惯常轮流守着你，倒像要看住你似的。

**霍纳.** 是啊，常有这样的事：绿帽汉不过是太太的探子；他陪着太太的情人外出行走、搅了人家的乐子，那才算当真尽家务职分；在家陪着太太充情人，倒在其次。可一个已婚女人派给情人的顶苦的差事，莫过于时时刻刻陪着她的丈夫。

**庸医.** 再说他那份热乎劲，同她那份一样，很快就叫你腻烦。

**霍纳.** 遭瘟！睡过了他的太太，再陪着他，就像城里的伶俐人榨干了乡下乡绅的钱、再陪着他一样，腻烦死人。

===Original===
Quack
And as at first a man makes a friend of the husband to get the wife, so at last you are fain to fall out with the wife to be rid of the husband.

Horner
Ay, most cuckold-makers are true courtiers; when once a poor man has cracked his credit for ’em, they can’t abide to come near him.

Quack
But at first, to draw him in, are so sweet, so kind, so dear! just as you are to Pinchwife. But what becomes of that intrigue with his wife?

Horner
A pox! he’s as surly as an alderman that has been bit; and since he’s so coy, his wife’s kindness is in vain, for she’s a silly innocent.

===Chinese===
**庸医.** 再说，起初为了弄到手太太，先同丈夫交朋友；到末了，为了甩掉丈夫，又不得不同太太翻脸。

**霍纳.** 是啊，制造绿帽的行家，多半是真正的朝臣：可怜人一旦为他们坏了信用，他们就再不忍走近他半步。

**庸医.** 可当初钓他上钩的时候，又那么甜、那么亲、那么热！就同你待平奇怀夫一个样。不过你同他太太那段私情，后来怎么样了？

**霍纳.** 遭瘟！他愠怒得像上了当的市政官；他既然这么拿班做势，他太太那份情意也白搭，因为她是个傻天真。

===Original===
Quack
Did she not send you a letter by him?

Horner
Yes; but that’s a riddle I have not yet solved. Allow the poor creature to be willing, she is silly too, and he keeps her up so close—

Quack
Yes, so close, that he makes her but the more willing, and adds but revenge to her love; which two, when met, seldom fail of satisfying each other one way or other.

Horner
What! here’s the man we are talking of, I think.

*Enter **Pinchwife**, leading in his **Wife** masked, muffled, and in her Sister’s gown.*

Horner
Pshaw!

Quack
Bringing his wife to you is the next thing to bringing a love-letter from her.

===Chinese===
**庸医.** 她不是托他捎给你一封信么？

**霍纳.** 是；可那是个我还没解开的谜。就算这可怜人是有意的罢，她也傻，何况他把她圈得那么死——

**庸医.** 是，圈得那么死，倒把她圈得越发有意了，还给她的情爱添了一把复仇心；这两样凑到一处，少不得要你依我顺，用这样那样的法子彼此称心。

**霍纳.** 咦！说曹操曹操到，我想。

*（**平奇怀夫**领着蒙面裹身的**太太**上，穿的是她姐姐的衣裳。）*

**霍纳.** 啐！

**庸医.** 把老婆亲领到你门上，同替她捎一封情书来，只差一步了。

===Original===
Horner
What means this?

Pinchwife
The last time, you know, sir, I brought you a love-letter; now, you see, a mistress; I think you’ll say I am a civil man to you.

Horner
Ay, the devil take me, will I say thou art the civilest man I ever met with; and I have known some. I fancy I understand thee now better than I did the letter. But, hark thee, in thy ear—

Pinchwife
What?

Horner
Nothing but the usual question, man: is she sound, on thy word?

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 这是什么意思？

**平奇怀夫.** 上回，先生，您知道，我给您捎来一封情书；这回，您瞧，捎来一位情人；我想您得承认，我待您够客气了。

**霍纳.** 是啊，叫魔鬼抓了我去，我这就承认：你是我平生见过的顶客气的人；我见过的可也不少。我看我这会儿懂你这个人，比懂那封信懂得多了。不过，凑你耳边说句话——

**平奇怀夫.** 说什么？

**霍纳.** 没什么，还是那句老话，老兄：她干净么？你担保？

===Original===
Pinchwife
What, you take her for a wench, and me for a pimp?

Horner
Pshaw! wench and pimp, paw14 words; I know thou art an honest fellow, and hast a great acquaintance among the ladies, and perhaps hast made love for me, rather than let me make love to thy wife.

Pinchwife
Come, sir, in short, I am for no fooling.

Horner
Nor I neither: therefore prithee, let’s see her face presently. Make her show, man: art thou sure I don’t know her?

Pinchwife
I am sure you do know her.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 怎么，您把她当窑姐儿，把我当皮条客？

**霍纳.** 啐！窑姐儿呀、皮条客呀，多「坏坏」14的字眼；我知道你是个正派人，在太太们跟前交游广阔，说不定还宁可替我牵线传情，也不叫我自个儿去追你太太。

**平奇怀夫.** 得了，先生，长话短说，我不来虚套。

**霍纳.** 我也不来：所以行行好，这就叫我们瞧瞧她的脸。伙计，叫她露出来：你敢保我不认得她？

**平奇怀夫.** 我敢保你认得。

===Original===
Horner
A pox! why dost thou bring her to me then?

Pinchwife
Because she’s a relation of mine—

Horner
Is she, faith, man? then thou art still more civil and obliging, dear rogue.

Pinchwife
Who desired me to bring her to you.

Horner
Then she is obliging, dear rogue.

Pinchwife
You’ll make her welcome for my sake, I hope.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 遭瘟！那你干吗把她带到我这儿来？

**平奇怀夫.** 因为她是我的一位亲戚——

**霍纳.** 当真么，老兄？那你就更客气、更多情了，亲爱的家伙。

**平奇怀夫.** 是她自个儿求我，把她带到您这儿来的。

**霍纳.** 那她可真多情，亲爱的家伙。

**平奇怀夫.** 您总肯看我的面子接待她罢，我希望。

===Original===
Horner
I hope she is handsome enough to make herself welcome. Prithee let her unmask.

Pinchwife
Do you speak to her; she would never be ruled by me.

Horner
Madam—***Mrs. Pinchwife** whispers to **Horner**.* She says she must speak with me in private. Withdraw, prithee.

Pinchwife
*Aside.* She’s unwilling, it seems, I should know all her indecent conduct in this business—*Aloud.* Well then, I’ll leave you together, and hope when I am gone, you’ll agree; if not, you and I shan’t agree, sir.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 我只指望她标致得自个儿就能讨得欢迎。行行好，叫她摘了面纱。

**平奇怀夫.** 您同她说罢；她从来不肯听我的管。

**霍纳.** 夫人——（平奇怀夫太太同霍纳耳语。）她说她必须同我私下谈。你回避回避，行行好。

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）看这样子，她不愿叫我知道她在这桩事里全部失体面的行径——（高声）好罢，我把你们留在一块儿，巴望我一走你们就投契；不然的话，先生，你我之间可就不投契了。

===Original===
Horner
What means the fool? if she and I agree ’tis no matter what you and I do. *Whispers to **Mrs. Pinchwife**, who makes signs with her hand for him to be gone.*

Pinchwife
In the meantime I’ll fetch a parson, and find out Sparkish, and disabuse him. You would have me fetch a parson, would you not? Well then—now I think I am rid of her, and shall have no more trouble with her—our sisters and daughters, like usurers’ money, are safest when put out; but our wives, like their writings, never safe, but in our closets under lock and key.

*Exit.*

*Enter **Boy**.*

Boy
Sir Jasper Fidget, sir, is coming up.

*Exit.*

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 这傻子什么意思？她同我投了契，你我怎么样还打什么紧。（同平奇怀夫太太耳语；她摆手叫他走。）

**平奇怀夫.** 这会儿我去请位牧师，再寻斯帕基什，点破他。你要我去请牧师，是不是？好罢——这下我想我甩掉她了，往后她再烦不着我了——咱们的妹妹和闺女，同放债人的钱一样，放出去了才最稳妥；咱们的老婆，却同放债人的字据一样，锁进咱们的密室、加上锁、拔了钥匙，才算安全。

*（下。）*

*（**童儿**上。）*

**童儿.** 先生，贾斯珀·菲吉特爵士上来了。

*（下。）*

===Original===
Horner
Here’s the trouble of a cuckold now we are talking of. A pox on him! has he not enough to do to hinder his wife’s sport, but he must other women’s too?—Step in here, madam.

*Exit **Mrs. Pinchwife**.*

*Enter **Sir Jasper Fidget**.*

Sir Jasper
My best and dearest friend.

Horner
*Aside to **Quack**.* The old style, doctor.—*Aloud.* Well, be short, for I am busy. What would your impertinent wife have now?

Sir Jasper
Well guessed, i’faith; for I do come from her.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 说绿帽汉，绿帽汉的麻烦就到了。叫他遭瘟！他拦自个儿太太的兴头还不够忙的，还要去拦旁人家太太的？——夫人，进这里面来。

*（**平奇怀夫太太**下。）*

*（**贾斯珀·菲吉特爵士**上。）*

**贾斯珀爵士.** 我最好、最亲爱的朋友。

**霍纳.**（向庸医旁白）老调子，大夫。——（高声）好罢，长话短说，我忙着呢。您那位放肆的太太这回又要什么？

**贾斯珀爵士.** 猜得真准，说真的；我正是打她那儿来。

===Original===
Horner
To invite me to supper! Tell her, I can’t come; go.

Sir Jasper
Nay, now you are out, faith; for my lady, and the whole knot of the virtuous gang, as they call themselves, are resolved upon a frolic of coming to you tonight in masquerade, and are all dressed already.

Horner
I shan’t be at home.

Sir Jasper
*Aside.* Lord, how churlish he is to women!—*Aloud.* Nay, prithee don’t disappoint ’em; they’ll think ’tis my fault: prithee don’t. I’ll send in the banquet and the fiddles. But make no noise on’t; for the poor virtuous rogues would not have it known, for the world, that they go a-masquerading; and they would come to no man’s ball but yours.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 来请我吃晚饭！告诉她，我来不了；去罢。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 嗐，这回您可猜岔了，真的；因为我太太，还有那伙自称「贞洁帮」的一整帮人，打定了主意今夜要胡闹一场，蒙面化装上您这儿来，装扮都已齐备了。

**霍纳.** 我不在家。

**贾斯珀爵士.**（旁白）天哪，他对女人好粗鲁！——（高声）嗐，行行好，别叫她们失望；她们要怪到我头上的：千万别。酒宴和乐队我打发人送来。只是千万别声张；因为那伙可怜的贞洁家伙，打死也不肯叫人知道她们出去化装游乐；而且除了您的舞会，谁的舞会她们也不肯赏脸。

===Original===
Horner
Well, well—get you gone; and tell ’em, if they come, ’twill be at the peril of their honour and yours.

Sir Jasper
He! he! he!—we’ll trust you for that: farewell.

*Exit.*

Horner
Doctor, anon you too shall be my guest,
But now I’m going to a private feast.

*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 好罢，好罢——去罢；告诉她们，要来就来，可她们和您的荣誉都得自担风险。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 嘿！嘿！嘿！——这个我们信得过您：再会。

*（下。）*

**霍纳.** 大夫，回头您也来做我的宾客，
可眼下我要去赴一场私密的宴。

*（同下。）*

### Scene III / 第三场

===Original===
The Piazza of Covent Garden.

*Enter **Sparkish** with a letter in his hand, **Pinchwife** following.*

Sparkish
But who would have thought a woman could have been false to me? By the world, I could not have thought it.

Pinchwife
You were for giving and taking liberty: she has taken it only, sir, now you find in that letter. You are a frank person, and so is she, you see there.

Sparkish
Nay, if this be her hand—for I never saw it.

Pinchwife
’Tis no matter whether that be her hand or no; I am sure this hand, at her desire, led her to Mr. Horner, with whom I left her just now, to go fetch a parson to ’em at their desire too, to deprive you of her forever; for it seems yours was but a mock marriage.

===Chinese===
科文特花园的皮亚扎广场。

*（**斯帕基什**手持一信上，**平奇怀夫**随上。）*

**斯帕基什.** 可谁想得到，一个女人居然会对我负心？凭全世界起誓，我想不到。

**平奇怀夫.** 你原是主张有给有收的自由的：如今她光是收了，先生，你那封信里瞧见了。你是个爽快人，她也是位爽快的，你瞧。

**斯帕基什.** 嗐，这要真是她的笔迹——我从来没见过她写字。

**平奇怀夫.** 那是不是她的笔迹不打紧；我拿得准，这只手，应她所求，把她领到了霍纳先生那儿——我刚把她撂在那儿，也是应他们所求，去给他们请位牧师，好叫你永远失掉她；因为看来你们那场婚事，不过是装样子的。

===Original===
Sparkish
Indeed, she would needs have it that ’twas Harcourt himself, in a parson’s habit, that married us; but I’m sure he told me ’twas his brother Ned.

Pinchwife
O, there ’tis out; and you were deceived, not she: for you are such a frank person. But I must be gone.—You’ll find her at Mr. Horner’s. Go, and believe your eyes.

*Exit.*

Sparkish
Nay, I’ll to her, and call her as many crocodiles, sirens, harpies, and other heathenish names, as a poet would do a mistress who had refused to hear his suit, nay more, his verses on her.—But stay, is not that she following a torch at t’other end of the Piazza? and from Horner’s certainly—’tis so.

*Enter **Alithea** following a torch, and **Lucy** behind.*

Sparkish
You are well met, madam, though you don’t think so. What, you have made a short visit to Mr. Horner? but I suppose you’ll return to him presently, by that time the parson can be with him.

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 说起来，她一口咬定，给我们主婚的是哈考特本人，穿着牧师的衣裳；可我拿得准他告诉过我，那是他兄弟内德。

**平奇怀夫.** 哦，露馅了；受骗的是你，不是她：谁叫你是这么个爽快人。可我得走了。——你上霍纳先生那儿找她去罢。去，信你自己的眼睛。

*（下。）*

**斯帕基什.** 不成，我这就找她去，把鳄鱼、海妖、鸟身女妖，连同一切异教名目，一股脑儿骂她个够——诗人骂一个不肯听他求爱、更不肯听他献诗的情妇，也就骂这么多。——慢着，广场那头跟着火把走的那位，不是她么？准是打霍纳那儿出来的——正是她。

*（**阿莉西娅**随一火把上，**露西**跟在后面。）*

**斯帕基什.** 碰见您可真巧，夫人，虽说您不这么想。怎么，您去霍纳先生那儿小坐了一趟？不过我想，等牧师一到他那儿，您也就回他身边去了。

===Original===
Alithea
Mr. Horner and the parson, sir!

Sparkish
Come, madam, no more dissembling, no more jilting; for I am no more a frank person.

Alithea
How’s this?

Lucy
So, ’twill work, I see. *Aside.*

Sparkish
Could you find out no easy country fool to abuse? none but me, a gentleman of wit and pleasure about the town? But it was your pride to be too hard for a man of parts, unworthy false woman! false as a friend that lends a man money to lose; false as dice, who undo those that trust all they have to ’em.

Lucy
He has been a great bubble, by his similes, as they say. *Aside.*

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 霍纳先生和牧师，先生！

**斯帕基什.** 得了，夫人，别再装了，别再耍人了；我可不是爽快人了。

**阿莉西娅.** 这是怎么说？

**露西.**（旁白）好，见效了，我看。

**斯帕基什.** 你就寻不出一个好摆布的乡下傻子么？偏挑中我，一个城里有才有闲的绅士？可你就仗着这份骄傲，非斗赢一个有才的男人不可，不知羞的假女人！假得像借钱给人去输光的「朋友」；假得像骰子，谁把身家性命全押上去，它就毁谁。

**露西.**（旁白）听他这些比方，他们说的「冤大头」，他当得可不小。

===Original===
Alithea
You have been too merry, sir, at your wedding-dinner, sure.

Sparkish
What, d’ye mock me too?

Alithea
Or you have been deluded.

Sparkish
By you.

Alithea
Let me understand you.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 先生，您喜酒吃得太高兴了，八成是的。

**斯帕基什.** 怎么，连你也耍我？

**阿莉西娅.** 要不就是您叫人骗了。

**斯帕基什.** 叫你骗的。

**阿莉西娅.** 叫我听明白您的意思。

===Original===
Sparkish
Have you the confidence, (I should call it something else, since you know your guilt,) to stand my just reproaches? you did not write an impudent letter to Mr. Horner? who I find now has clubbed with you in deluding me with his aversion for women, that I might not, forsooth, suspect him for my rival.

Lucy
D’ye think the gentleman can be jealous now, madam? *Aside.*

Alithea
I write a letter to Mr. Horner!

Sparkish
Nay, madam, do not deny it. Your brother showed it me just now; and told me likewise, he left you at Horner’s lodging to fetch a parson to marry you to him; and I wish you joy, madam, joy, joy; and to him too, much joy; and to myself more joy, for not marrying you.


Alithea
*Aside.* So, I find my brother would break off the match; and I can consent to’t, since I see this gentleman can be made jealous.—*Aloud.* O Lucy, by his rude usage and jealousy, he makes me almost afraid I am married to him. Art thou sure ’twas Harcourt himself, and no parson, that married us?

Sparkish
No, madam, I thank you. I suppose, that was a contrivance too of Mr. Horner’s and yours, to make Harcourt play the parson; but I would as little as you have him one now, no, not for the world. For, shall I tell you another truth? I never had any passion for you till now, for now I hate you. ’Tis true, I might have married your portion, as other men of parts of the town do sometimes; and so, your servant. And to show my unconcernedness, I’ll come to your wedding, and resign you with as much joy, as I would a stale wench to a new cully; nay, with as much joy as I would after the first night, if I had been married to you. There’s for you; and so your servant, servant.
*Exit.*

===Chinese===
**斯帕基什.** 亏你还有脸——你自知有罪，我本该换个说法——受我这一番义正词严的数落？你没给霍纳先生写过一封厚脸皮的信？他拿他那份厌恶女人哄我，如今我才明白，原来是同你串通好了的，好叫我不疑心他做我的情敌。

**露西.**（旁白）您瞧，这位先生如今吃得上醋了罢，夫人？

**阿莉西娅.** 我给霍纳先生写信！

**斯帕基什.** 嗐，夫人，别抵赖了。您兄长方才拿信给我看了；还告诉我，他把您撂在霍纳的寓所，去请牧师把您嫁给他；我祝您喜，夫人，喜，喜；也祝他，大喜；更祝我自己，喜上加喜——幸亏没娶您。


**阿莉西娅.**（旁白）好，我看出来了，兄长是想退掉这门亲；既然这位先生是能叫他吃上醋的，我也乐得点头。——（高声）哦露西，他这么粗鲁、这么吃醋，闹得我几乎怕起自个儿真嫁了他了。你拿得准，给我们主婚的是哈考特本人，不是什么牧师？

**斯帕基什.** 不，夫人，多谢您费心。我想，那也是霍纳先生同您的一路机关，叫哈考特扮牧师；可如今我也同您一样，绝不要他当牧师，不，打死也不要。因为——要不要再告诉您一句实话？我从前对您从来没有过热情，如今才算有了，因为我如今恨您。不假，我原可以娶您的陪嫁，像城里有才的男人有时干的那样；那么，您安。为了显出我的满不在乎，您成婚那天我要来，欢欢喜喜把您让出去——就像把一个用旧了的粉头让给新冤大头一样欢喜；不，就像我真娶了您、熬过头一夜之后那样欢喜。这个给您；那么您安，您安。
*（下。）*

===Original===
Alithea
How was I deceived in a man!

Lucy
You’ll believe then a fool may be made jealous now? for that easiness in him that suffers him to be led by a wife, will likewise permit him to be persuaded against her by others.

Alithea
But marry Mr. Horner! my brother does not intend it, sure: if I thought he did, I would take thy advice, and Mr. Harcourt for my husband. And now I wish, that if there be any over-wise woman of the town, who, like me, would marry a fool for fortune, liberty, or title, first, that her husband may love play, and be a cully to all the town but her, and suffer none but Fortune to be mistress of his purse; then, if for liberty, that he may send her into the country, under the conduct of some huswifely mother-in-law; and if for title, may the world give ’em none but that of cuckold.

Lucy
And for her greater curse, madam, may he not deserve it.

Alithea
Away, impertinent! Is not this my old Lady Lanterlu’s?15

Lucy
Yes, madam.—*Aside.* And here I hope we shall find Mr. Harcourt.

*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 我看男人，竟看走了眼到这个地步！

**露西.** 这会儿您总信了罢，傻子也是能叫他吃上醋的？因为他那份好说话，容得老婆牵着他走，也就容得旁人在他跟前说她的坏话。

**阿莉西娅.** 可是嫁给霍纳先生！兄长总不至于打这个主意罢：我要真这样想，就听你的劝，收哈考特先生做丈夫。这会儿我只许个愿：城里但凡有哪个过分精明的女人，像我一样，为了财产、自由或名头去嫁个傻子——头一条，愿她丈夫爱赌，全城人前当冤大头、只在她跟前例外，只许命运女神一个管他的钱袋；其次，若为自由，愿他把她打发下乡，交给哪位管家婆似的后娘押着；若为名头，愿世人只给他们夫妇一个名头——绿帽汉。

**露西.** 再添一条顶毒的咒，夫人：愿他偏偏不配戴这顶帽子。

**阿莉西娅.** 去你的，多嘴！这不是老兰特尔卢夫人（Lady Lanterlu）的宅子么？15

**露西.** 是的，夫人。——（旁白）而且我希望，在这儿能找见哈考特先生。

*（同下。）*

### Scene IV / 第四场

===Original===
**Horner’s** Lodging. A table, banquet, and bottles.

*Enter **Horner**, **Lady Fidget**, **Mrs. Dainty Fidget**, and **Mrs. Squeamish**.*

Horner
A pox! they are come too soon—before I have sent back my new mistress. All that I have now to do is to lock her in, that they may not see her. *Aside.*

Lady Fidget
That we may be sure of our welcome, we have brought our entertainment with us, and are resolved to treat thee, dear toad.

Mrs. Dainty
And that we may be merry to purpose, have left Sir Jasper and my old Lady Squeamish, quarrelling at home at backgammon.

Mrs. Squeamish
Therefore let us make use of our time, lest they should chance to interrupt us.

===Chinese===
霍纳的寓所。一桌酒宴，杯瓶罗列。

*（**霍纳**、**菲吉特夫人**、**丹蒂·菲吉特小姐**与**斯奎米什太太**上。）*

**霍纳.**（旁白）遭瘟！她们来得太快——我还没把我那位新情人打发走。眼下只有一条：把她锁在里头，别叫她们撞见。

**菲吉特夫人.** 为保准儿不受冷遇，我们把酒菜自家带来了，打定了主意要款待你，亲爱的癞蛤蟆。

**丹蒂小姐.** 为叫咱们乐个痛快，我们把贾斯珀爵士和老斯奎米什夫人撂在家里，由他俩下十五子棋拌嘴去。

**斯奎米什太太.** 所以咱们得抓紧时辰，免得他们碰巧闯进来搅了。

===Original===
Lady Fidget
Let us sit then.

Horner
First, that you may be private, let me lock this door and that, and I’ll wait upon you presently.

Lady Fidget
No, sir, shut ’em only, and your lips forever; for we must trust you as much as our women.

Horner
You know all vanity’s killed in me; I have no occasion for talking.

Lady Fidget
Now, ladies, supposing we had drank each of us our two bottles, let us speak the truth of our hearts.

Mrs. Dainty and Mrs. Squeamish
Agreed.

===Chinese===
**菲吉特夫人.** 那就坐罢。

**霍纳.** 先容我把这道门、那道门都锁上，好叫你们清静，我立马就来伺候。

**菲吉特夫人.** 不必，先生，掩上就是，还有您的嘴唇，永远掩上；因为我们待您，得同待自家的女仆一样信得过才是。

**霍纳.** 你们知道，我身上一切虚浮都已除尽；我用不着多嘴。

**菲吉特夫人.** 来，诸位，就算咱们每人已经灌下两瓶，那咱们说说心窝里的真话罢。

**丹蒂小姐与斯奎米什太太.** 依了。

===Original===
Lady Fidget
By this brimmer, for truth is nowhere else to be found—*Aside to **Horner**.* not in thy heart, false man!

Horner
You have found me a true man, I’m sure. *Aside to **Lady Fidget**.*

Lady Fidget
*Aside to **Horner**.* Not every way.—But let us sit and be merry. *Sings.*

Why should our damned tyrants oblige us to live
On the pittance of pleasure which they only give?
We must not rejoice
With wine and with noise:
In vain we must wake in a dull bed alone,
Whilst to our warm rival the bottle they’re gone.
Then lay aside charms,
And take up these arms.16
’Tis wine only gives ’em their courage and wit;
Because we live sober, to men we submit.
If for beauties you’d pass,
Take a lick of the glass,
’Twill mend your complexions, and when they are gone,
The best red we have is the red of the grape:
Then, sisters, lay’t on,
And damn a good shape.

===Chinese===
**菲吉特夫人.** 凭这满杯起誓——因为真话旁处再寻不着——（向霍纳旁白）你心里就寻不着，负心的男人！

**霍纳.** 您可寻着了我是个真男人，我拿得准。（向菲吉特夫人旁白。）

**菲吉特夫人.**（向霍纳旁白）样样都真，可算不上。——罢了，坐，乐咱们的。（唱。）

凭什么那该死的暴君逼着我们，
光靠他们施舍的一口欢乐过活？
不兴我们快活，
用酒也用喧闹：
我们只能徒然独守死闷的空床，
他们却搂着我们暖热的情敌——酒瓶——去眠。
那么撇下媚色，
拿起这些兵器。16
唯有酒给他们胆气与才情；
只因我们活得清醒，才向男人俯首。
你若要充个美人，
且舔一舔杯口，
舔了气色保管见好；待姿色一凋，
咱们顶好的红色，便是葡萄的红：
那么，姐妹们，往脸上搽，
管什么好身段，去它的。

===Original===
Mrs. Dainty
Dear brimmer! Well, in token of our openness and plain-dealing, let us throw our masks over our heads.

Horner
So, ’twill come to the glasses anon. *Aside.*

Mrs. Squeamish
Lovely brimmer! let me enjoy him first.

Lady Fidget
No, I never part with a gallant till I’ve tried him. Dear brimmer! that makest our husbands shortsighted.

Mrs. Dainty
And our bashful gallants bold.

Mrs. Squeamish
And, for want of a gallant, the butler lovely in our eyes.—Drink, eunuch.

===Chinese===
**丹蒂小姐.** 亲爱的满杯！好，为表咱们敞亮爽直，把面纱都掀到头顶上去罢。

**霍纳.**（旁白）好，回头就该轮到酒杯了。

**斯奎米什太太.** 可爱的满杯！让我先受用他一回。

**菲吉特夫人.** 不行，情人不经我试过，我从不放手。亲爱的满杯！是你叫咱们丈夫眼神不济。

**丹蒂小姐.** 也叫咱们羞答答的情人胆壮。

**斯奎米什太太.** 还叫咱们缺情人的时节，连管家仆役都眉目可爱。——喝呀，阉人。

===Original===
Lady Fidget
Drink, thou representative of a husband.—Damn a husband!

Mrs. Dainty
And, as it were a husband, an old keeper.

Mrs. Squeamish
And an old grandmother.

Horner
And an English bawd, and a French surgeon.

Lady Fidget
Ay, we have all reason to curse ’em.

Horner
For my sake, ladies?

===Chinese===
**菲吉特夫人.** 喝呀，你这做丈夫的替身。——丈夫这东西，该咒！

**丹蒂小姐.** 还有那俨然半个丈夫的——老包养汉，也该咒。

**斯奎米什太太.** 还有老祖母，也该咒。

**霍纳.** 还有英格兰的老鸨、法兰西的外科大夫，也该咒。

**菲吉特夫人.** 是，咱们个个有理由咒他们。

**霍纳.** 为我么，夫人们？

===Original===
Lady Fidget
No, for our own; for the first spoils all young gallants’ industry.

Mrs. Dainty
And the other’s art makes ’em bold only with common women.

Mrs. Squeamish
And rather run the hazard of the vile distemper amongst them, than of a denial amongst us.

Mrs. Dainty
The filthy toads choose mistresses now as they do stuffs, for having been fancied and worn by others.

Mrs. Squeamish
For being common and cheap.

Lady Fidget
Whilst women of quality, like the richest stuffs, lie untumbled, and unasked for.

===Chinese===
**菲吉特夫人.** 不，为咱们自己；因为头一个——老鸨——败尽了年轻情人们的上进心。

**丹蒂小姐.** 那外科大夫的手艺呢，只叫他们有胆量去招惹下等粉头。

**斯奎米什太太.** 宁可在她们那边冒那脏病的险，也不肯在咱们这边冒个回绝的险。

**丹蒂小姐.** 那些脏蛤蟆如今挑情妇，同挑衣料一个挑法，专挑旁人瞧中过、上身过的。

**斯奎米什太太.** 图个大众化、价钱贱。

**菲吉特夫人.** 咱们上等女人呢，同顶贵的料子一样，无人翻动，无人问津。

===Original===
Horner
Ay, neat, and cheap, and new, often they think best.

Mrs. Dainty
No, sir, the beasts will be known by a mistress longer than by a suit.

Mrs. Squeamish
And ’tis not for cheapness neither.

Lady Fidget
No; for the vain fops will take up druggets, and embroider ’em. But I wonder at the depraved appetites of witty men; they use to be out of the common road, and hate imitation. Pray tell me, beast, when you were a man, why you rather chose to club with a multitude in a common house for an entertainment, than to be the only guest at a good table.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 是啊，干净、便宜、崭新，他们常想顶好的就是这几样。

**丹蒂小姐.** 不，先生，这班畜生，包一个情妇的时日，可比穿一套衣裳的时日长久。

**斯奎米什太太.** 再说也不是图便宜。

**菲吉特夫人.** 不是；那些虚荣的纨绔，赊一匹粗呢回来也要绣上花。可我就纳闷，有才的男人胃口竟这般败坏；他们素来不走寻常路，最恨人云亦云。请问，畜生，你做男人的那会儿，宁可在大饭馆里同一大群人凑份子吃一顿，也不肯独占一席好菜，为的什么？

===Original===
Horner
Why, faith, ceremony and expectation are unsufferable to those that are sharp bent. People always eat with the best stomach at an ordinary, where every man is snatching for the best bit.

Lady Fidget
Though he get a cut over the fingers.—But I have heard, that people eat most heartily of another man’s meat, that is, what they do not pay for.

Horner
When they are sure of their welcome and freedom; for ceremony in love and eating is as ridiculous as in fighting: falling on briskly is all should be done on those occasions.

Lady Fidget
Well then, let me tell you, sir, there is nowhere more freedom than in our houses; and we take freedom from a young person as a sign of good breeding; and a person may be as free as he pleases with us, as frolic, as gamesome, as wild as he will.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 嗐，说真的，礼数和干等，对饿狠了的人最是难挨。人总是在公共食肆里胃口最好——在那儿人人都抢着夹顶好的那一箸。

**菲吉特夫人.** 哪怕指头上挨一筷子。——可我听说，人吃旁人的菜——也就是不用自家掏钱的菜——吃得顶香。

**霍纳.** 只要拿得准受欢迎、无拘无束；因为情场和饭桌上的客套，同打架时的客套一样可笑：那些场合，讲究的就是干脆利落地扑上去。

**菲吉特夫人.** 那么我告诉您罢，先生，天底下再没比咱们家里更自由的地方；年轻人对咱们放肆，咱们只当是有教养的表现；在我们这儿，谁爱怎么放肆都随他，爱怎么闹、怎么玩、怎么撒野都由他。

===Original===
Horner
Han’t I heard you all declaim against wild men?

Lady Fidget
Yes; but for all that, we think wildness in a man as desirable a quality as in a duck or rabbit: a tame man! foh!

Horner
I know not, but your reputations frightened me as much as your faces invited me.

Lady Fidget
Our reputation! Lord, why should you not think that we women make use of our reputation, as you men of yours, only to deceive the world with less suspicion? Our virtue is like the statesman’s religion, the quaker’s word, the gamester’s oath, and the great man’s honour; but to cheat those that trust us.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 我没听你们个个痛骂过野男人么？

**菲吉特夫人.** 骂过；可尽管骂，我们心里想：男人野，同鸭子野、兔子野一样，是巴望不得的好品性；驯男人！呸！

**霍纳.** 我说不上，反正你们的名声吓退我，同你们的脸蛋招引我，分量一样重。

**菲吉特夫人.** 咱们的名声！天哪，您怎么想不到，我们女人使唤名声，同你们男人使唤你们的一样，不过叫世人少起疑心地受骗？咱们的贞洁，就像政客的信仰、贵格会的言语、赌徒的誓、大人物的荣誉——专用来哄那些信咱们的人。

===Original===
Mrs. Squeamish
And that demureness, coyness, and modesty, that you see in our faces in the boxes at plays, is as much a sign of a kind woman, as a vizard-mask in the pit.

Mrs. Dainty
For, I assure you, women are least masked when they have the velvet vizard on.

Lady Fidget
You would have found us modest women in our denials only.

Mrs. Squeamish
Our bashfulness is only the reflection of the men’s.

Mrs. Dainty
We blush when they are shamefaced.

===Chinese===
**斯奎米什太太.** 还有您在戏园子包厢里瞧见的咱们脸上那副端庄、娇羞、贤淑，同池座里一张面罩一样，都是「好说话」女人的招牌。

**丹蒂小姐.** 因为您放心，女人蒙上丝绒面罩的时节，恰恰最不遮掩。

**菲吉特夫人.** 你们原只会在我们的推拒里，见到我们端庄淑女的一面。

**斯奎米什太太.** 咱们的害臊，不过是映男人害臊的回光。

**丹蒂小姐.** 他们臊了，咱们才红脸。

===Original===
Horner
I beg your pardon, ladies, I was deceived in you devilishly. But why that mighty pretence to honour?

Lady Fidget
We have told you; but sometimes ’twas for the same reason you men pretend business often, to avoid ill company, to enjoy the better and more privately those you love.

Horner
But why would you ne’er give a friend a wink then?

Lady Fidget
Faith, your reputation frightened us, as much as ours did you, you were so notoriously lewd.

Horner
And you so seemingly honest.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 请诸位恕罪，我把你们看岔了，看岔得厉害。可何苦把荣誉那样大张旗鼓地装门面？

**菲吉特夫人.** 早告诉您了；有时同你们男人常装着有正事一样——躲开乏味的应酬，好把心爱的人儿受用得更透、更隐秘。

**霍纳.** 那从前怎么连个眼色也不肯给朋友递一个？

**菲吉特夫人.** 说真的，你们的名声吓退我们，同我们的吓退你们一样；你们那荒唐，是满城出了名的。

**霍纳.** 你们那正经，也是装得满城皆知。

===Original===
Lady Fidget
Was that all that deterred you?

Horner
And so expensive—you allow freedom, you say.

Lady Fidget
Ay, ay.

Horner
That I was afraid of losing my little money, as well as my little time, both which my other pleasures required.

Lady Fidget
Money! foh! you talk like a little fellow now: do such as we expect money?

===Chinese===
**菲吉特夫人.** 就这点把您拦住了？

**霍纳.** 还有那么破费——你们许人放肆，是这么说罢。

**菲吉特夫人.** 是，是。

**霍纳.** 闹得我既怕赔上我那点小工夫，也怕赔上我那点小钱——我旁的乐子，两样全离不开。

**菲吉特夫人.** 钱！呸！您这会儿说话，可像个小家子气的人了：我们这号人，会指望钱？

===Original===
Horner
I beg your pardon, madam, I must confess, I have heard that great ladies, like great merchants, set but the higher prices upon what they have, because they are not in necessity of taking the first offer.

Mrs. Dainty
Such as we make sale of our hearts?

Mrs. Squeamish
We bribed for our love? foh!

Horner
With your pardon ladies, I know, like great men in offices, you seem to exact flattery and attendance only from your followers; but you have receivers about you, and such fees to pay, a man is afraid to pass your grants. Besides, we must let you win at cards, or we lose your hearts; and if you make an assignation, ’tis at a goldsmith’s, jeweller’s, or china-house; where for your honour you deposit to him, he must pawn his to the punctual cit, and so paying for what you take up, pays for what he takes up.

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 您恕罪，夫人，我得招认：我听说过，大户太太们同大商贾一个样——正因为犯不上接头一份出价，把自家的货倒标更高的价。

**丹蒂小姐.** 我们这号人，会发卖真心？

**斯奎米什太太.** 我们的爱情，是行贿得来的？呸！

**霍纳.** 诸位恕罪，我知道，你们同衙门里的大人物一样，明面上只向追随者索取奉承和伺候；可你们身边自有经手人，过路的使费又重，男人轻易不敢兑你们的「特许状」。再说，打牌我们得让你们赢，不然就输你们的心；你们要约会，约的地点不是金匠铺、珠宝铺，就是瓷器铺；您为了自个儿的荣誉，把货记在他账上，他就得把自个儿的荣誉典给那分文不赊的市侩钱庄——你们赊的账他来结，他赊的账也归他结。

===Original===
Mrs. Dainty
Would you not have us assured of our gallants’ love?

Mrs. Squeamish
For love is better known by liberality than by jealousy.

Lady Fidget
For one may be dissembled, the other not.—*Aside.* But my jealousy can be no longer dissembled, and they are telling ripe.—*Aloud.*—Come, here’s to our gallants in waiting, whom we must name, and I’ll begin. This is my false rogue. *Claps him on the back.*

Mrs. Squeamish
How!

Horner
So, all will out now. *Aside.*

Mrs. Squeamish
Did you not tell me, ’twas for my sake only you reported yourself no man? *Aside to **Horner**.*

Mrs. Dainty
Oh, wretch! did you not swear to me, ’twas for my love and honour you passed for that thing you do? *Aside to **Horner**.*

Horner
So, so.

Lady Fidget
Come, speak, ladies: this is my false villain.

Mrs. Squeamish
And mine too.

Mrs. Dainty
And mine.

Horner
Well then, you are all three my false rogues too, and there’s an end on’t.

===Chinese===
**丹蒂小姐.** 难道您不要我们对情人的爱拿得准么？

**斯奎米什太太.** 因为爱情这东西，靠慷慨验真，可比靠吃醋验真灵。

**菲吉特夫人.** 因为一样装得出来，一样装不出来。——（旁白）可我的醋意再也装不下去了，火候也到了。——（高声）来，敬咱们「随侍的情人」一杯——是得点名了，我先来。这个，是我的负心贼。（拍他的背。）

**斯奎米什太太.** 什么！

**霍纳.**（旁白）好，全要抖出来了。

**斯奎米什太太.**（向霍纳旁白）你不是告诉我，你散布自个儿不男人，只是为了我么？
