**丹蒂小姐.**（向霍纳旁白）哦，没良心的！你不是对我赌咒，你顶了如今这个名头，是为了我的爱情和荣誉么？

**霍纳.** 好，好。

**菲吉特夫人.** 来，说呀，诸位：这是我的负心坏。

**斯奎米什太太.** 也是我的。

**丹蒂小姐.** 也是我的。

**霍纳.** 好罢，那你们仨也都是我的负心贼，这笔账两清。

===Original===
Lady Fidget
Well then, there’s no remedy; sister sharers, let us not fall out, but have a care of our honour. Though we get no presents, no jewels of him, we are savers of our honour, the jewel of most value and use, which shines yet to the world unsuspected, though it be counterfeit.

Horner
Nay, and is e’en as good as if it were true, provided the world think so; for honour, like beauty now, only depends on the opinion of others.

Lady Fidget
Well, Harry Common, I hope you can be true to three. Swear; but ’tis to no purpose to require your oath, for you are as often forsworn as you swear to new women.

Horner
Come, faith, madam, let us e’en pardon one another; for all the difference I find betwixt we men and you women, we forswear ourselves at the beginning of an amour, you as long as it lasts.

*Enter **Sir Jasper Fidget**, and **Old Lady Squeamish**.*

===Chinese===
**菲吉特夫人.** 那么，没法子了；姐妹合伙人们，咱们别窝里斗，各自看好自个儿的荣誉罢。虽说从他手里没捞着礼、没捞着珠宝，咱们总算保住了自个儿的荣誉——那才是顶值钱、顶顶用的珠宝，如今在世人眼里还亮堂堂的，没人疑心，虽说是件赝品。

**霍纳.** 不假，只要世人这么想，它同真的也一般好用；因为荣誉这东西，如今同美貌一样，全凭旁人的口舌定成色。

**菲吉特夫人.** 好罢，哈利·共有（Harry Common），我指望你对得起三个人。起誓罢；不过讨你的誓也白讨，因为你每对一个新女人起一回誓，也就每起一回伪誓。

**霍纳.** 得了，说真的，夫人，咱们彼此饶了罢；我看你们女人同我们男人的分别只有一条：我们在私情开头起一回伪誓，你们是私情一天不散，伪誓一天不起了完。

*（**贾斯珀·菲吉特爵士**与**老斯奎米什夫人**上。）*

===Original===
Sir Jasper
Oh, my Lady Fidget, was this your cunning, to come to Mr. Horner without me? but you have been nowhere else, I hope.

Lady Fidget
No, Sir Jasper.

Lady Squeamish
And you came straight hither, Biddy?

Mrs. Squeamish
Yes, indeed, lady grandmother.

Sir Jasper
’Tis well, ’tis well; I knew when once they were thoroughly acquainted with poor Horner, they’d ne’er be from him: you may let her masquerade it with my wife and Horner, and I warrant her reputation safe.

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 哦，菲吉特夫人，这就是您的巧计，撇开我上霍纳先生这儿来？可您没到旁处去过罢，我希望。

**菲吉特夫人.** 没有，贾斯珀爵士。

**老斯奎米什夫人.** 你也是一径到这儿来的，碧迪？

**斯奎米什太太.** 是，真的，祖母夫人。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 好，好；我早知道，她们同可怜的霍纳一旦处熟了，就再也离不开他：您只管放她同我太太和霍纳一处化装游玩，她的名声我担保万无一失。

===Original===
*Enter **Boy**.*

Boy
O, sir, here’s the gentleman come, whom you bid me not suffer to come up, without giving you notice, with a lady too, and other gentlemen.

Horner
Do you all go in there, whilst I send ’em away; and, boy, do you desire ’em to stay below till I come, which shall be immediately.

*Exeunt **Sir Jasper Fidget**, **Lady Fidget**, **Lady Squeamish**, **Mrs. Squeamish**, and **Mrs. Dainty Fidget**.*

Boy
Yes, sir.

*Exit.*

*Exit **Horner** at the other door, and returns with **Mrs. Pinchwife**.*

===Chinese===
*（**童儿**上。）*

**童儿.** 哦，先生，您吩咐过、不通报不许他上来的那位先生来了，还带着一位太太，另有几位先生。

**霍纳.** 你们都进那边去，我把他们打发走；孩子，你请他们在楼底下略等，我立马就下来。

*（贾斯珀·菲吉特爵士、菲吉特夫人、老斯奎米什夫人、斯奎米什太太与丹蒂·菲吉特小姐同下。）*

**童儿.** 是，先生。

*（下。）*

*（**霍纳**从另一道门下，偕**平奇怀夫太太**复上。）*

===Original===
Horner
You would not take my advice, to be gone home before your husband came back, he’ll now discover all; yet pray, my dearest, be persuaded to go home, and leave the rest to my management; I’ll let you down the back way.

Mrs. Pinchwife
I don’t know the way home, so I don’t.

Horner
My man shall wait upon you.

Mrs. Pinchwife
No, don’t you believe that I’ll go at all; what, are you weary of me already?

Horner
No, my life, ’tis that I may love you long, ’tis to secure my love, and your reputation with your husband; he’ll never receive you again else.

Mrs. Pinchwife
What care I? d’ye think to frighten me with that? I don’t intend to go to him again; you shall be my husband now.

Horner
I cannot be your husband, dearest, since you are married to him.

Mrs. Pinchwife
O, would you make me believe that? Don’t I see every day at London here, women leave their first husbands, and go and live with other men as their wives? pish, pshaw! you’d make me angry, but that I love you so mainly.

Horner
So, they are coming up—In again, in, I hear ’em.—

*Exit **Mrs. Pinchwife**.* Well, a silly mistress is like a weak place, soon got, soon lost, a man has scarce time for plunder; she betrays her husband first to her gallant, and then her gallant to her husband.

*Enter **Pinchwife, Alithea, Harcourt, Sparkish, Lucy**, and a Parson.*

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 你不肯听我的话，赶你丈夫回来之前回家去，这下他全要戳穿了；可求你了，我的宝贝，听我一回劝，家去罢，余下的交给我张罗；我送你打后楼梯下去。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 我不认得回家的道，就是不认得。

**霍纳.** 我的仆人送你去。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 不，你别信我肯走；怎么，你就腻了我了？

**霍纳.** 不是，我的性命，正是为能爱你得长久，为保牢我的爱，也保牢你丈夫对你的器量；不然他再不肯收留你了。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 我管那些？你想拿这个吓我？我不打算再回他那儿去了；往后你就是我的丈夫。

**霍纳.** 我当不成你的丈夫，顶亲爱的，你已经嫁给他了。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 哦，你想叫我相信这个？我在伦敦这儿，天天不都瞧见么？女人撇了头一个丈夫，跑去找旁的男人，当他们的太太过日子！啐，呸！你真要惹我恼了——幸亏我这么死心塌地爱你。

**霍纳.** 瞧，他们上来了——再进去，进去，我听见他们了。——

*（**平奇怀夫太太**下。）* 唉，一个傻情人同一座不设防的城池一样，得来容易，失掉也容易，男人连掳掠的工夫都腾不出；她先把丈夫出卖给情人，再把情人出卖给丈夫。

*（**平奇怀夫**、**阿莉西娅**、**哈考特**、**斯帕基什**、**露西**与一名牧师上。）*

===Original===
Pinchwife
Come, madam, ’tis not the sudden change of your dress, the confidence of your asseverations, and your false witness there, shall persuade me I did not bring you hither just now; here’s my witness, who cannot deny it, since you must be confronted.—Mr. Horner, did not I bring this lady to you just now?

Horner
Now must I wrong one woman for another’s sake—but that’s no new thing with me, for in these cases I am still on the criminal’s side against the innocent. *Aside.*

Alithea
Pray speak, sir.

Horner
It must be so. I must be impudent, and try my luck; impudence uses to be too hard for truth. *Aside.*

Pinchwife
What, you are studying an evasion or excuse for her! Speak, sir.

Horner
No, faith, I am something backward only to speak in women’s affairs or disputes.

Pinchwife
She bids you speak.

Alithea
Ay, pray, sir, do, pray satisfy him.

Horner
Then truly, you did bring that lady to me just now.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 来，夫人，你突然换了行头、一口咬定的胆气，还有你身边那位假证人，都劝不动我，说我不曾方才把你领到这儿来；我这位证人抵赖不得，你当面对质罢。——霍纳先生，方才是不是我把这位夫人领到你这儿来的？

**霍纳.**（旁白）这会儿我不得不为一个女人亏负另一个了——不过于我也不算新鲜事，这种官司，我一向站在有罪的那边，亏负清白的那边。

**阿莉西娅.** 求您开口，先生。

**霍纳.**（旁白）只得如此了。我只好不要脸，碰一碰运气；不要脸这东西，对付真话向来一拿一个准。

**平奇怀夫.** 怎么，你在替她琢磨遁词、寻托辞！说，先生。

**霍纳.** 不，说真的，我只是素来不大愿意插嘴女人的事、女人的官司。

**平奇怀夫.** 她叫你说。

**阿莉西娅.** 是啊，求您了，先生，求您叫他安心罢。

**霍纳.** 那么，说实在的，您方才确实把这位夫人领到我这儿来了。

===Original===
Pinchwife
O ho!

Alithea
How, sir?

Harcourt
How, Horner?

Alithea
What mean you, sir? I always took you for a man of honour.

Horner
Ay, so much a man of honour, that I must save my mistress, I thank you, come what will on’t. *Aside.*

Sparkish
So, if I had had her, she’d have made me believe the moon had been made of a Christmas pie.

Lucy
Now could I speak, if I durst, and solve the riddle, who am the author of it. *Aside.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 哦嚯！

**阿莉西娅.** 什么，先生？

**哈考特.** 什么，霍纳？

**阿莉西娅.** 您什么意思，先生？我一向当您是个讲荣誉的人。

**霍纳.**（旁白）是啊，讲荣誉讲到了家，只得搭救我的情人了，谢谢您，天塌下来也顾不得。

**斯帕基什.** 瞧，我要真娶了她，她能哄得我相信，月亮是圣诞肉饼做的。

**露西.**（旁白）这会儿我倒是能说话——只要敢——把谜底揭开；这谜，原是我一手造的。

===Original===
Alithea
O unfortunate woman! A combination against my honour! which most concerns me now, because you share in my disgrace, sir, and it is your censure, which I must now suffer, that troubles me, not theirs.

Harcourt
Madam, then have no trouble, you shall now see ’tis possible for me to love too, without being jealous; I will not only believe your innocence myself, but make all the world believe it.—*Aside to **Horner**.* Horner, I must now be concerned for this lady’s honour.

Horner
And I must be concerned for a lady’s honour too.

Harcourt
This lady has her honour, and I will protect it.

Horner
My lady has not her honour, but has given it me to keep, and I will preserve it.

Harcourt
I understand you not.

Horner
I would not have you.

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 哦，不幸的女人！一群人串通了算计我的荣誉！这会儿最叫我挂心的，是您也分担了我的耻辱，先生；我往后要受的是您的指摘——叫我难受的是这个，不是他们的。

**哈考特.** 夫人，那就别难受，您这就要见到，我这个人也可能又爱、又不吃醋；我不但自个儿信您清白，还要叫全世界都信。——（向霍纳旁白）霍纳，这会儿我得顾全这位夫人的荣誉了。

**霍纳.** 我也得顾全一位夫人的荣誉。

**哈考特.** 这位夫人自有她的荣誉在，我要护住它。

**霍纳.** 我这位夫人没有她的荣誉——她把它交给我保管了，我要保它周全。

**哈考特.** 我听不懂你。

**霍纳.** 我原不想叫你懂。

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
What’s the matter with ’em all? *Peeping in behind.*

Pinchwife
Come, come, Mr. Horner, no more disputing; here’s the parson, I brought him not in vain.

Harcourt
No, sir, I’ll employ him, if this lady please.

Pinchwife
How! what d’ye mean?

Sparkish
Ay, what does he mean?

Horner
Why, I have resigned your sister to him, he has my consent.

Pinchwife
But he has not mine, sir; a woman’s injured honour, no more than a man’s, can be repaired or satisfied by any but him that first wronged it; and you shall marry her presently, or—*Lays his hand on his sword.*

*Reenter **Mrs. Pinchwife**.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 他们全都怎么了？（在后面探头。）

**平奇怀夫.** 得，得，霍纳先生，别争了；牧师在这儿呢，我没有白请他来。

**哈考特.** 确实没白请，先生，我这就用他——只要这位夫人愿意。

**平奇怀夫.** 什么！你什么意思？

**斯帕基什.** 是啊，他什么意思？

**霍纳.** 怎么，我已把令妹让给他了，他有我的首肯。

**平奇怀夫.** 可他没有我的，先生；女人受了亏负的荣誉，同男人的一样，只有头一个亏负它的人才补赎得了、平得了账；你这就娶她，不然——（手按剑柄。）

*（**平奇怀夫太太**又上。）*

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
O Lord, they’ll kill poor Mr. Horner! besides, he shan’t marry her whilst I stand by, and look on; I’ll not lose my second husband so.

Pinchwife
What do I see?

Alithea
My sister in my clothes!

Sparkish
Ha!

Mrs. Pinchwife
Nay, pray now don’t quarrel about finding work for the parson, he shall marry me to Mr. Horner; or now, I believe, you have enough of me. *To **Pinchwife**.*

Horner
Damned, damned loving changeling! *Aside.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
Pray, sister, pardon me for telling so many lies of you.

Horner
I suppose the riddle is plain now.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 哎呀老天，他们要杀了可怜的霍纳先生！再说，有我在跟前看着，他就休想娶她；我才不肯这么丢了第二个丈夫。

**平奇怀夫.** 我看见的是什么？

**阿莉西娅.** 我妹妹穿着我的衣裳！

**斯帕基什.** 哈！

**平奇怀夫太太.** 嗐，求你们了，这会儿别抢着替牧师找活儿干了，他该把我嫁给霍纳先生；再不然——这会儿总该看明白了，我是够你受的了。（向平奇怀夫。）

**霍纳.**（旁白）该死的、该死的痴情傻丫头！

**平奇怀夫太太.** 好姐姐，饶了我罢，我编了你那么多谎。

**霍纳.** 我想这会儿，谜底见了天光了。

===Original===
Lucy
No, that must be my work.—Good sir, hear me. *Kneels to **Pinchwife**, who stands doggedly with his hat over his eyes.*

Pinchwife
I will never hear woman again, but make ’em all silent thus—*Offers to draw upon his **Wife**.*

Horner
No, that must not be.

Pinchwife
You then shall go first, ’tis all one to me. *Offers to draw on **Horner**, but is stopped by **Harcourt**.*

Harcourt
Hold!

*Reenter **Sir Jasper Fidget**, **Lady Fidget**, **Lady Squeamish**, **Mrs. Dainty Fidget**, and **Mrs. Squeamish**.*

===Chinese===
**露西.** 不，那还得我来。——好先生，听我说。（向平奇怀夫下跪；他倔头倔脑地立着，帽檐压着眼睛。）

**平奇怀夫.** 我从此再不听女人的，只叫她们个个这样闭嘴——（作势要拔剑砍向他太太。）

**霍纳.** 不成，这个使不得。

**平奇怀夫.** 那就你先走，横竖一个样。（作势要拔剑砍霍纳，被哈考特拦住。）

**哈考特.** 住手！

*（**贾斯珀·菲吉特爵士**、**菲吉特夫人**、**老斯奎米什夫人**、**丹蒂·菲吉特小姐**与**斯奎米什太太**又上。）*

===Original===
Sir Jasper
What’s the matter? what’s the matter? pray, what’s the matter, sir? I beseech you communicate, sir.

Pinchwife
Why, my wife has communicated, sir, as your wife may have done too, sir, if she knows him, sir.

Sir Jasper
Pshaw, with him! ha! ha! he!

Pinchwife
D’ye mock me, sir? a cuckold is a kind of a wild beast; have a care, sir.

Sir Jasper
No, sure, you mock me, sir. He cuckold you! it can’t be, ha! ha! he! why, I’ll tell you, sir—*Offers to whisper.*

Pinchwife
I tell you again, he has whored my wife, and yours too, if he knows her, and all the women he comes near; ’tis not his dissembling, his hypocrisy, can wheedle me.

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 怎么了？怎么了？请问怎么了，先生？求您给大伙儿说道说道，先生。

**平奇怀夫.** 怎么，我太太早「说道」过了，先生——您太太保不齐也「说道」过，先生，只要她认得他，先生。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 啐，同他！哈！哈！嘿！

**平奇怀夫.** 您拿我开心么，先生？绿帽汉是一种野兽，先生；留神着些。

**贾斯珀爵士.** 不，瞧这样子，是您拿我开心，先生。他给您戴绿帽！不能够，哈！哈！嘿！这样，我告诉您，先生——（作势要耳语。）

**平奇怀夫.** 我再说一遍，他睡了我太太，也要睡您太太——只要他认得她——凡他凑近的女人，一个不落；他那装假、那伪善，哄不了我。

===Original===
Sir Jasper
How! does he dissemble? is he a hypocrite? Nay, then—how—wife—sister, is he a hypocrite?

Lady Squeamish
A hypocrite! a dissembler! Speak, young harlotry, speak, how?

Sir Jasper
Nay, then—O my head too!—O thou libidinous lady!

Lady Squeamish
O thou harloting harlotry! hast thou done’t then?

Sir Jasper
Speak, good Horner, art thou a dissembler, a rogue? hast thou—

Horner
So!

Lucy
I’ll fetch you off, and her too, if she will but hold her tongue. *Apart to **Horner**.*

Horner
Canst thou? I’ll give thee—*Apart to **Lucy**.*

===Chinese===
**贾斯珀爵士.** 什么！他会装假？他是个伪君子？嗐，这——怎么——太太——姐妹，他是个伪君子么？

**老斯奎米什夫人.** 伪君子！装假的！说，小蹄子，说，怎么讲？

**贾斯珀爵士.** 嗐，糟了——我的头也晕了！——哦，你这淫荡的夫人！

**老斯奎米什夫人.** 哦，你这偷汉的小蹄子！你真做了那事了？

**贾斯珀爵士.** 说呀，好霍纳，你是个装假的、一个流氓么？你可——

**霍纳.** 糟了！

**露西.**（向霍纳旁白）我能救你脱身，连她也救——只要她肯管住舌头。

**霍纳.**（向露西旁白）你行？我就给你——

===Original===
Lucy
*To **Pinchwife**.* Pray have but patience to hear me, sir, who am the unfortunate cause of all this confusion. Your wife is innocent, I only culpable; for I put her upon telling you all these lies concerning my mistress, in order to the breaking off the match between Mr. Sparkish and her, to make way for Mr. Harcourt.

Sparkish
Did you so, eternal rotten tooth? Then, it seems, my mistress was not false to me, I was only deceived by you. Brother, that should have been, now man of conduct, who is a frank person now, to bring your wife to her lover, ha?

Lucy
I assure you, sir, she came not to Mr. Horner out of love, for she loves him no more—

Mrs. Pinchwife
Hold, I told lies for you, but you shall tell none for me, for I do love Mr. Horner with all my soul, and nobody shall say me nay; pray, don’t you go to make poor Mr. Horner believe to the contrary; ’tis spitefully done of you, I’m sure.

Horner
Peace, dear idiot. *Aside to **Mrs. Pinchwife**.*

Mrs. Pinchwife
Nay, I will not peace.

Pinchwife
Not till I make you.

===Chinese===
**露西.**（向平奇怀夫。）求您耐着性子听我说，先生，这场乱子，不幸全是我一手招的。您太太是清白的，独我一人有罪；是我撺掇她对我家小姐编了这一堆谎，好退掉斯帕基什先生那门亲，给哈考特先生腾路子。

**斯帕基什.** 是么，你这万年烂牙？这么说，我家小姐倒不曾负我，我只是叫你骗了。本该做我内兄的，如今倒成了「有城府的人」；到底谁还爽快呢——亲自把老婆送到情人门上，啊？

**露西.** 我担保，先生，她上霍纳先生这儿来不是出于爱，因为她已经不爱他了——

**平奇怀夫太太.** 慢着，我替你撒了谎，你可休想替我撒谎；我可是拿整个灵魂爱霍纳先生的，谁也不许说个不字；求求你，别去哄可怜的霍纳先生，叫他信反面的话；你这么做，存心太坏了，一定的。

**霍纳.**（向平奇怀夫太太旁白）安静，亲爱的傻丫头。

**平奇怀夫太太.** 不，我偏不安静。

**平奇怀夫.** 等我叫你安静。

===Original===
*Enter **Dorilant** and **Quack**.*

Dorilant
Horner, your servant; I am the doctor’s guest, he must excuse our intrusion.

Quack
But what’s the matter, gentlemen? for Heaven’s sake, what’s the matter?

Horner
Oh, ’tis well you are come. ’Tis a censorious world we live in; you may have brought me a reprieve, or else I had died for a crime I never committed, and these innocent ladies had suffered with me; therefore, pray satisfy these worthy, honourable, jealous gentlemen—that—*Whispers.*

Quack
O, I understand you, is that all?—Sir Jasper, by Heavens, and upon the word of a physician, sir—*Whispers to **Sir Jasper**.*

Sir Jasper
Nay, I do believe you truly.—Pardon me, my virtuous lady, and dear of honour.

Lady Squeamish
What, then all’s right again?

Sir Jasper
Ay, ay, and now let us satisfy him too. *They whisper with **Pinchwife**.*

===Chinese===
*（**多里兰特**与**庸医**上。）*

**多里兰特.** 霍纳，您安；我是大夫的宾客，咱们不请自来，他得多担待。

**庸医.** 这是怎么了，诸位？看老天爷分上，到底怎么了？

**霍纳.** 哦，来得正好。咱们住的这世道，长舌得狠；你许是给我捎来了缓刑令，不然我就要为一桩从没犯过的罪送命，连这几位清白的夫人也要陪我遭殃；所以，求你叫这几位可敬的、荣誉的、吃醋的先生们安心——就说——（耳语。）

**庸医.** 哦，我懂了，就这点事？——贾斯珀爵士，皇天在上，凭一个大夫的誓词，先生——（向贾斯珀爵士耳语。）

**贾斯珀爵士.** 嗐，我真信了。——饶了我罢，我贞洁的太太，亲爱的荣誉心肝。

**老斯奎米什夫人.** 怎么，这就又都对头了？

**贾斯珀爵士.** 是，是，这会儿咱们也叫他安心罢。（他们同平奇怀夫耳语。）

===Original===
Pinchwife
An eunuch! Pray, no fooling with me.

Quack
I’ll bring half the chirurgeons in town to swear it.

Pinchwife
They!—they’ll swear a man that bled to death through his wounds, died of an apoplexy.

Quack
Pray, hear me, sir—why, all the town has heard the report of him.

Pinchwife
But does all the town believe it?

Quack
Pray, inquire a little, and first of all these.

Pinchwife
I’m sure when I left the town, he was the lewdest fellow in’t.

Quack
I tell you, sir, he has been in France since; pray, ask but these ladies and gentlemen, your friend Mr. Dorilant. Gentlemen and ladies, han’t you all heard the late sad report of poor Mr. Horner?

All the Ladies.
Ay, ay, ay.

Dorilant
Why, thou jealous fool, dost thou doubt it? he’s an arrant French capon.

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫.** 阉人！行行好，别拿我打趣。

**庸医.** 我把城里半数的外科大夫都请来起誓。

**平奇怀夫.** 他们！——他们能把一个伤口流血流死的男人，发誓成中风死的。

**庸医.** 听我说完，先生——嗐，满城谁没听见传他的那桩事。

**平奇怀夫.** 可满城就都信么？

**庸医.** 不信您打听打听，先问这一屋子人。

**平奇怀夫.** 我拿得准，我离城那会儿，他是城里最荒唐的一个。

**庸医.** 我告诉您，先生，打那以后他去过法兰西；不信只问这几位太太先生，还有您的朋友多里兰特先生。诸位先生太太，可怜的霍纳先生近来那桩伤心事，你们谁没听说过？

**众位夫人.** 是，是，是。

**多里兰特.** 嗐，你这吃醋的傻子，你还疑心？他是一只道地的法兰西阉鸡。

===Original===
Mrs. Pinchwife
’Tis false, sir, you shall not disparage poor Mr. Horner, for to my certain knowledge—

Lucy
O, hold!

Mrs. Squeamish
Stop her mouth! *Aside to **Lucy**.*

Lady Fidget
Upon my honour, sir, ’tis as true—*To **Pinchwife**.*

Mrs. Dainty
D’ye think we would have been seen in his company?

Mrs. Squeamish
Trust our unspotted reputations with him?

Lady Fidget
This you get, and we too, by trusting your secret to a fool. *Aside to **Horner**.*

Horner
Peace, madam.—*Aside to **Quack**.* Well, doctor, is not this a good design, that carries a man on unsuspected, and brings him off safe?

Pinchwife
Well, if this were true—but my wife—*Aside.*

***Dorilant** whispers with **Mrs. Pinchwife**.*

===Chinese===
**平奇怀夫太太.** 是假的，先生，不许你糟践可怜的霍纳先生，因为我拿得准——

**露西.** 哦，打住！

**斯奎米什太太.**（向露西旁白）堵她的嘴！

**菲吉特夫人.**（向平奇怀夫。）凭我的荣誉，先生，千真万确——

**丹蒂小姐.** 你们当我们肯叫人瞧见同他作伴？

**斯奎米什太太.** 当我们肯把清白名声托付给他？

**菲吉特夫人.**（向霍纳旁白）这就是你把秘密托给一个傻子的下场——咱们也跟着遭殃。

**霍纳.** 别说了，夫人。——（向庸医旁白）怎么样，大夫，这条妙计使得么——叫人一路无人疑心，还能全身而退？

**平奇怀夫.**（旁白）唉，这要是真的——可我太太——

*（**多里兰特**同**平奇怀夫太太**耳语。）*

===Original===
Alithea
Come, brother, your wife is yet innocent, you see; but have a care of too strong an imagination, lest, like an over-concerned timorous gamester, by fancying an unlucky cast, it should come. Women and fortune are truest still to those that trust ’em.

Lucy
And any wild thing grows but the more fierce and hungry for being kept up, and more dangerous to the keeper.

Alithea
There’s doctrine for all husbands, Mr. Harcourt.

Harcourt
I edify, madam, so much, that I am impatient till I am one.

Dorilant
And I edify so much by example, I will never be one.

Sparkish
And because I will not disparage my parts, I’ll ne’er be one.

Horner
And I, alas! can’t be one.

Pinchwife
But I must be one—against my will to a country wife, with a country murrain to me!

Mrs. Pinchwife
And I must be a country wife still too, I find; for I can’t, like a city one, be rid of my musty husband, and do what I list. *Aside.*

===Chinese===
**阿莉西娅.** 来，兄长，您瞧，您太太眼下还是清白的；可要提防想象力太旺盛，免得像那个提心吊胆、过分操心的赌棍，越怕掷出个坏点，坏点偏偏就来。女人和运气一样，最信得过的，到底是信她们的人。

**露西.** 再说，凡是野东西，圈得越久，越凶越饿，对看守的人也越危险。

**阿莉西娅.** 这一条，够全天下的丈夫受教了，哈考特先生。

**哈考特.** 我受教了，夫人，受教得恨不能立刻也当上一个。

**多里兰特.** 我受前车之鉴的教，教得再不肯当这一个。

**斯帕基什.** 我也不能辱没我的才具，所以也永远不当。

**霍纳.** 我呢，唉！想当当不上。

**平奇怀夫.** 可我非当不可——违着心，配着个乡村老婆当，还叫一场乡村牛瘟降到我头上！

**平奇怀夫太太.**（旁白）我瞧，我也还得继续当个乡村老婆；因为我学不会城里太太那样，甩掉发霉的丈夫，爱做什么做什么。

===Original===
Horner
Now, sir, I must pronounce your wife innocent, though I blush whilst I do it; and I am the only man by her now exposed to shame, which I will straight drown in wine, as you shall your suspicion; and the ladies’ troubles we’ll divert with a ballad.—Doctor, where are your maskers?

Lucy
Indeed, she’s innocent, sir, I am her witness, and her end of coming out was but to see her sister’s wedding; and what she has said to your face of her love to Mr. Horner, was but the usual innocent revenge on a husband’s jealousy;—was it not, madam, speak?

Mrs. Pinchwife
*Aside to **Lucy** and **Horner**.* Since you’ll have me tell more lies—*Aloud.* Yes, indeed, bud.

Pinchwife
For my own sake fain I would all believe;
Cuckolds, like lovers, should themselves deceive.
But—*Sighs.*
His honour is least safe (too late I find)
Who trusts it with a foolish wife or friend.

*A Dance of Cuckolds.*

Horner
Vain fops but court and dress, and keep a pother,
To pass for women’s men with one another;
But he who aims by women to be prized,
First by the men, you see, must be despised.

*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
**霍纳.** 好了，先生，我只好宣判尊夫人清白——判的时候我脸红也罢；这会儿同她一道丢脸的，只有我一个男人；这脸红我这就拿酒淹了它，您的疑心也请照样淹了；几位夫人的烦恼呢，咱们用一支歌谣给解解闷。——大夫，你的假面戏班呢？

**露西.** 真的，她是清白的，先生，我可以作证；她出门来，也不过为瞧一眼姐姐的婚礼；她当着您的面说的那些爱霍纳先生的话，不过是妻子对丈夫吃醋的一点惯常的天真报复；——是不是，夫人，说呀？

**平奇怀夫太太.**（向露西与霍纳旁白）既然你们非要我再多撒几个谎——（高声）是，真的是，乖乖。

**平奇怀夫.** 为了自个儿，我巴不得人人都信；
绿帽汉同情人一样，只该自骗自身。
只是——（叹气。）
谁把荣誉托给蠢妻或蠢友，
谁的荣誉最不安稳——可惜我懂得太迟。

*（绿帽汉之舞。）*

**霍纳.** 愚妄的纨绔只管献殷勤、打扮、吵吵嚷嚷，
好在彼此面前冒充女人的相好；
可谁想叫女人们高看一眼，
就先得叫男人们看扁——你们瞧。

*（同下。）*

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===
1. I am out of patience when anything is blamed, not because it is thought coarsely and inelegantly composed, but because it is new: when for the ancients not indulgence, but honour and rewards are demanded. —Epist. II i 76–8 ↩︎

===Chinese===
1. 任何作品一受指摘我便按捺不住——并非因为它被认作写得粗陋失雅，而只因它是新出的：如今对古人，求的已不是宽纵，而是尊崇与奖赏。——《书札》第二卷第一篇 76–8 行 ↩︎

===Original===
2. Charles Hart, grandson of Shakespeare’s sister, Joan Hart, was one of the most distinguished actors of his time. He excelled chiefly in tragedy, and it was said of him that he “might teach any king on earth how to comport himself.” He retired from the stage in 1682, and died in the following year. ↩︎

===Chinese===
2. 查尔斯·哈特（Charles Hart），莎士比亚之妹琼·哈特（Joan Hart）的孙子，是他那个时代最负盛名的演员之一。他尤擅悲剧，据说他「足以教世上任何一位国王如何端持王度」。1682 年他退出舞台，翌年去世。↩︎

===Original===
3. A character in Ben Jonson’s comedy, *The Alchemist*. ↩︎

===Chinese===
3. 本·琼森（Ben Jonson）喜剧《炼金术士》（The Alchemist）中的人物。↩︎

===Original===
4. *Sir Martin Mar-all* is the title of a comedy by Dryden, first produced in 1667. In the scene referred to, Sir Martin serenades his mistress, going through the motions of singing and accompanying himself with the lute, while the actual performance is that of his man, who is concealed behind him. The lady discovers the imposition, through Sir Martin’s failing to leave off at the right time. ↩︎

===Chinese===
4. 《马丁·马奥尔爵士》是德莱顿一部喜剧的剧名，1667 年首演。在所指的那场戏里，马丁爵士向情人唱小夜曲，装模作样地歌唱、弹鲁特琴伴奏，实际的演唱演奏全出自藏在他身后的仆人。只因马丁爵士没有在恰当的时候停下来，那位小姐便识破了这套把戏。↩︎

===Original===
5. Chatelain’s was a famous French ordinary in Covent Garden, much frequented by wits and men of fashion. It is mentioned by Pepys, and often referred to by Shadwell in his plays. The Cock Tavern was in Bow Street, near where Wycherley and his first wife, the Countess of Drogheda, lodged; and it was here that the windows had to be left open when Wycherley frequented it, that the countess might see there were no ladies in the company. ↩︎

===Chinese===
5. 夏特兰馆是科文特花园一家有名的法国菜馆，才子与时髦人物常聚之所。佩皮斯（Pepys）提起过它，沙德威尔（Shadwell）的剧本里也常引用。「公鸡」馆则在鲍街，离威彻利与其第一任妻子德罗赫达伯爵夫人（the Countess of Drogheda）的寓所不远；威彻利常去那儿时，馆子的窗子都得敞着，好叫伯爵夫人看清席间并没有女士。↩︎

===Original===
6. I.e. The New Exchange, a long building, erected upon the site of the stables of Durham House, on the south side of the Strand, and nearly opposite Bedford Street. Opened in 1609, it became a fashionable lounge after the Restoration, and was pulled down in 1737. “It was erected partly on the plan of the Royal Exchange, with vaults beneath, over which was an open paved arcade; and above were walks of shops, occupied by perfumers and publishers, milliners and sempstresses.” —*Timbs’ Curiosities of London* ↩︎

===Chinese===
6. 即新交易所，一座长条形建筑，建在斯特兰德街南侧达勒姆府（Durham House）马厩的旧址上，与贝德福德街（Bedford Street）几乎正对。1609 年开张，王政复辟后成为时髦人物的游憩所，1737 年拆除。「它部分仿皇家交易所的格局建成，底下有拱顶地窖，其上为一圈露天铺石的拱廊；再上是有店铺的游廊，租与香水商、出版商、女帽商和缝衣妇。」——《廷布斯伦敦奇观》（Timbs’ Curiosities of London）↩︎

===Original===
7. “*Covent Garden Drolery, Or a Colection of all the Choice Songs, Poems, Prologues, and Epilogues (Sung and Spoken at Courts and Theaters) never in Print before. Written by the refined’st Witts of the Age*. And Collected by R[ichard] B[rome] Servant to His Majestie. London, Printed for James Magnes neer the Piazza in Russel-Street, 1672.”—*Tarugo’s Wiles, or the Coffee House*; a comedy by Sir Thomas St. Serle, produced in 1668.—*The Slighted Maid*, a comedy by Sir Robert Stapleton, produced in 1663. ↩︎

===Chinese===
7. 「《科文特花园谐趣集，或历年宫廷与剧院中所唱所诵之一切上选歌谣、诗篇、开场白与收场白（此前从未付梓。出自当世最风雅之才子手笔）。由国王陛下仆从理[查德]·布[罗姆]辑录。伦敦，罗素街皮亚扎广场附近，詹姆斯·马格尼斯刊行，1672 年。」——《塔鲁戈的诡计，或咖啡馆》（Tarugo’s Wiles, or the Coffee House），托马斯·圣瑟尔爵士（Sir Thomas St. Serle）的喜剧，1668 年上演。——《受冷落的侍女》（The Slighted Maid），罗伯特·斯特普尔顿爵士（Sir Robert Stapleton）的喜剧，1663 年上演。↩︎

===Original===
8. A sweet-scented powder. ↩︎

===Chinese===
8. 一种气味香甜的粉。↩︎

===Original===
9. Carefully. ↩︎

===Chinese===
9. 细心地；精巧地。↩︎

===Original===
10. Succeeds. ↩︎

===Chinese===
10. 成事；顺遂。↩︎

===Original===
11. Romp; tomboy. ↩︎

===Chinese===
11. 野丫头；疯姑娘。↩︎

===Original===
12. A well frequented ordinary near Charing Cross, on the site of Drummond’s Bank. ↩︎

===Chinese===
12. 查令十字附近一家宾客盈门的餐馆，在德拉蒙德银行（Drummond’s Bank）旧址上。↩︎

===Original===
13. In Wycherley’s time the square of Covent Garden must have presented an elegant appearance. The Piazza, designed by Inigo Jones, extended, like the modern piazza, along the northern and eastern sides; on the west stood St. Paul’s Church, built by the same famous architect, and on the south the square was bordered by the gardens of Bedford House. “The area was enclosed with railings, at sixty feet from the buildings; and in the centre was a dial, with a gilt ball, raised upon a column.” *Timbs’ Curiosities of London*. In 1671 the market was established, but was held, originally, only on the southern side of the square, under the wall of Bedford Gardens. The houses within the Piazza were then occupied by persons of rank and fashion. ↩︎

===Chinese===
13. 在威彻利的时代，科文特花园的广场想必一派雅致景象。皮亚扎广场由伊尼戈·琼斯（Inigo Jones）设计，同如今的柱廊一样，沿广场北面与东面延伸；西面立着同一位名建筑师所建的圣保罗教堂，南面则以贝德福德府（Bedford House）的花园为界。「场子以铁栏围起，离四周建筑六十英尺；中央是一座日晷，晷顶一颗镀金圆球，架在柱上。」《廷布斯伦敦奇观》。1671 年市场设立，但起初只在广场南侧、贝德福德花园的墙下开市。皮亚扎以内的宅邸，当时住的都是有身份、赶时髦的人物。↩︎

===Original===
14. Paw, adj. naughty. An affected word fashionable in the latter half of the seventeenth century. —Wright ↩︎

===Chinese===
14. Paw，形容词，「坏的；淘气的」。十七世纪后半叶流行的一种矫饰字眼。——赖特（Wright）↩︎

===Original===
15. “Lanterloo” or “langteraloo” was the name of a game at cards. ↩︎

===Chinese===
15. 「Lanterloo」或「langteraloo」（兰特尔卢）是一种纸牌戏的名字。↩︎

===Original===
16. The glasses. ↩︎

===Chinese===
16. 指酒杯。↩︎

===Original===
17. Or Knipp, the friend of Mr. Pepys. She played the part of Lady Fidget. ↩︎

===Chinese===
17. 又作克尼普（Knipp），佩皮斯先生的朋友。她扮演菲吉特夫人一角。↩︎

===Original===
> Indignor quidquam reprehendi, non quia crasse
> Compositum illepideve putetur, sed quia nuper:
> Nec veniam antiquis, sed honorem et praemia posci.1
> — Horat.

===Chinese===
> 任何作品一受指摘我便按捺不住——并非因为它被认作写得粗陋失雅，而只因它是新出的：如今对古人，求的已不是宽纵，而是尊崇与奖赏。1
> ——贺拉斯（Horat.）

## Epilogue / 收场白

===Original===
Spoken by Mrs. Knep.17

Now you the vigorous, who daily here
O’er vizard-mask in public domineer,
And what you’d do to her, if in place where;
Nay, have the confidence to cry, “Come out!”
Yet when she says, “Lead on!” you are not stout;
But to your well-dressed brother straight turn round,
And cry “Pox on her, Ned, she can’t be sound!”
Then slink away, a fresh one to engage,
With so much seeming heat and loving rage,
You’d frighten listening actress on the stage;
Till she at last has seen you huffing come,
And talk of keeping in the tiring-room,
Yet cannot be provoked to lead her home.

===Chinese===
由克奈普夫人（Mrs. Knep）17 朗诵。

此刻你们这些雄风勃勃的，天天在这儿
当众对面罩女郎发号施令，
放话说一旦到了得手的去处，想拿她怎样便怎样；
不，还敢扯开嗓子喊「出来！」
可她一说「带路！」，你们立马就泄了气；
转身便凑向你那位衣冠楚楚的兄弟，
嚷道「她准不干净，内德，去她的！」
随后溜之大吉，另去钓一个新鲜的人，
那副火急火燎、情热似怒的模样，
能把戏台上侧耳倾听的女伶都吓着；
直到她末了见惯了你咋咋呼呼地上门，
听惯了你说要把她包养进后台，
却怎么也激不出你送她回家。

===Original===
Next, you Falstaffs of fifty, who beset
Your buckram maidenheads, which your friends get;
And whilst to them you of achievements boast,
They share the booty, and laugh at your cost.

===Chinese===
再有，你们这些五十岁的福斯塔夫们，围猎着
你们那些硬布浆的贞操，进账却全归朋友；
你们还对着他们自夸战功赫赫，
他们分了赃，回头还笑话你们破费。

===Original===
In fine, you essenced boys, both old and young,
Who would be thought so eager, brisk, and strong,
Yet do the ladies, not their husbands wrong;
Whose purses for your manhood make excuse,
And keep your Flanders mares for show not use;
Encouraged by our woman’s man today,
A Horner’s part may vainly think to play;
And may intrigues so bashfully disown,
That they may doubted be by few or none;
May kiss the cards at picquet, ombre, loo,
And so be taught to kiss the lady too;
But, gallants, have a care, faith, what you do.

===Chinese===
总而言之，你们这些熏香的小子们，老的小的一起算，
个个想叫人瞧着那么起劲、那么麻利、那么强壮，
干的却是对不起太太们、而非她们丈夫的勾当；
那些钱袋替你们的男子气概打圆场，
养着你们的佛兰德母马，只摆样子，不派用场；
受了我们今日这位「女人的男人」的鼓舞，
你们大可以妄想扮演一回霍纳；
可以把私情推得那样羞羞答答，
惹得几乎无人、或竟无一人起疑；
可以在皮克牌、奥姆布雷牌、卢牌上亲吻牌张，
就此学会亲吻牌桌边的太太；
可是，情郎们，千万当心，说真的，别做过头。

===Original===
The world, which to no man his due will give,
You by experience know you can deceive,
And men may still believe you vigorous,
But then we women—there’s no cozening us.

===Chinese===
这世道不肯把各人应得的给他，
你们凭经验知道，世人尽可受你们欺骗，
男人们也尽可继续信你们雄风不减——
可我们女人嘛——想糊弄我们，没门。

## Introduction / 导言

===Original===
*The Country Wife* was written, according to its author’s own statement, about the year 1671 or 1672. Its production upon the stage was subsequent to that of *The Gentleman Dancing-Master*, to which allusion is made in the prologue, and antecedent to that of the earlier-written *Plain Dealer*, in the second act of which the author inserted some critical observations upon *The Country Wife*. The first performance of *The Plain Dealer*, as will afterwards appear, admits not of a later date than that of March, or the very beginning of April, 1674; it follows then that *The Country Wife* was brought forward some time between the early spring of 1672 and that of 1674. It was acted by the King’s Company, established during these two years at the theatre in Portugal Street, Lincoln’s Inn Fields, and was published in the year 1675.

===Chinese===
据作者自述，《乡村妻子》约写于 1671 或 1672 年。它搬上舞台，晚于《绅士舞蹈教师》（The Gentleman Dancing-Master）——开场诗中曾暗指此剧——而早于动笔在先的《直率人》（The Plain Dealer）；作者在后者第二幕里，插入了针对《乡村妻子》的若干批评性议论。《直率人》的首演之期，如下文将见，不会迟于 1674 年 3 月或 4 月之初；由此可知，《乡村妻子》的搬演当在 1672 年早春至 1674 年早春之间。该剧由国王剧团（the King’s Company）演出——那两年间该团常驻林肯律师学院广场（Lincoln’s Inn Fields）葡萄牙街（Portugal Street）的剧场——并于 1675 年出版。

===Original===
If we can overlook the immorality which, in this play, is more offensive and pronounced than in any of Wycherley’s other dramas, we shall find in *The Country Wife* a brilliantly written and skilfully constructed comedy, superior to either of the preceding dramas from the same pen, and surpassed, among comedies of the Restoration, only by its author’s own masterpiece, *The Plain Dealer*. The plot of *The Country Wife* is partly based upon two comedies by Molière—*L’Ecole des Femmes* and *L’Ecole des Maris*. From the former of these Wycherley derived his conception of the jealous man who keeps under close restraint a young and ignorant woman, with the vain hope of thereby securing her fidelity to him. Agnes’s innocent confessions to Arnolphe of her lover’s stratagems and her own esteem for him find a counterpart in the Country Wife’s frankness on a similar occasion, but beyond these points of coincidence there is little resemblance between the two plays. From *L’Ecole des Maris*, again, Wycherley has borrowed one or two incidents: the imprisoned girl’s device of making her would-be husband (in the English play, her actual husband) the bearer of a letter to her gallant, and the trick by which Isabella causes her tyrant, under the impression that she is another woman, to consign her with his own hands to his rival.

===Chinese===
倘若我们能够放过这部剧中的不道德成分——它比威彻利其他任何剧作都更刺眼、更露骨——我们便会在《乡村妻子》中读到一部笔致辉煌、结构巧妙的喜剧：它出自同一支笔，胜过此前的两部剧作，而在王政复辟时期的喜剧之中，也只有作者自己的杰作《直率人》能压过它。《乡村妻子》的情节部分取材于莫里哀的两部喜剧——《妇人学堂》（L’Ecole des Femmes）与《丈夫学堂》（L’Ecole des Maris）。从前一部，威彻利得来了他的醋精构想：把一个年轻无知的女人严严实实看管起来，徒然指望以此保住她对自己的忠贞。阿涅丝（Agnes）向阿尔诺尔夫（Arnolphe）天真坦白情人的种种巧计以及她自己的倾心，恰与乡村妻子在类似场合的坦率互为对照；但除了这几处暗合，两剧并无多少相似之处。从《丈夫学堂》，威彻利又借来了一两个情节：被禁闭的姑娘设下巧计，叫她那位未来的丈夫（在英文剧里是她现任的丈夫）充当给情人送信的信使；还有伊莎贝拉（Isabella）用的那条妙计——叫她的暴君误把她当作另一个女人，亲手把她交到情敌的手里。

===Original===
Steele has published, in the *Tatler* of April 16, 1709, a very just criticism upon this play, which, as it cannot fail to interest the reader, I venture to subjoin.

> “Will’s Coffeehouse, April 14.
> “This evening the Comedy, called *The Country Wife*, was acted in Drury Lane, for the benefit of Mrs. Bignell. The part which gives name to the Play was performed by herself. Through the whole action she made a very pretty figure, and exactly entered into the nature of the part. Her husband, in the Drama, is represented to be one of those debauchees who run through the vices of the town, and believe, when they think fit, they can marry and settle at their ease. His own knowledge of the iniquity of the age makes him choose a wife wholly ignorant of it, and place his security in her want of skill to abuse him. The Poet, on many occasions, where the propriety of the character will admit of it, insinuates that there is no defence against vice but the contempt of it: and has, in the natural ideas of an untainted innocent, shown the gradual steps to ruin and destruction which persons of condition run into, without the help of a good education to form their conduct. The torment of a jealous coxcomb, which arises from his own false maxims, and the aggravation of his pain by the very words in which he sees her innocence, makes a very pleasant and instructive satire. The character of Horner, and the design of it, is a good representation of the age in which that Comedy was written: at which time love and wenching were the business of life, and the gallant manner of pursuing women was the best recommendation at Court. To this only it is to be imputed that a Gentleman of Mr. Wycherley’s character and sense condescends to represent the insults done to the honour of the bed without just reproof; but to have drawn a man of probity with regard to such considerations had been a monster, and a Poet had at that time discovered his want of knowing the manners of the Court he lived in, by a virtuous character in his fine gentleman, as he would show his ignorance by drawing a vicious one to please the present audience.”

===Chinese===
斯蒂尔（Steele）在 1709 年 4 月 16 日的《闲谈者》（The Tatler）上，刊出了一篇对此剧极为公允的评论，谅必能为读者添趣，谨冒昧转录于下。

> 「威尔咖啡馆（Will’s Coffeehouse），4 月 14 日。
> 「今晚在德鲁里巷（Drury Lane），为比格内尔夫人（Mrs. Bignell）义演了名为《乡村妻子》的喜剧。给剧名点题的那个角色，由她本人扮演。全剧之中，她身段仪态甚美，而且恰到好处地进入了角色的内里。剧中的丈夫，被写成那样一种浪荡子：把城里的种种罪恶尝了个遍，还自信只要愿意，随时可以娶妻成家、安享清福。他既深知这个时代的种种邪僻，便挑了一个对此全然无知的妻子，把自家的安稳，安放在她不谙骗术这一点上。诗人在许多人物性格许可的场合，都暗指御恶之法，唯有轻蔑一途；又借一个未曾玷污的天真者自然而然的心思，写出了体面人家若无良好教养规范其行止，便会一步步滑进的覆灭深渊。一个醋劲十足的草包所受的煎熬——这煎熬生于他自家的谬误信条，而她言语之间处处显露的天真，又把他这份苦楚越搅越深——构成一段既逗趣又益人的讽刺。霍纳这个人物，连同设计这一人物的用意，正是那部喜剧所写时代的一幅绝好写照：彼时谈情说爱、追蜂逐蝶便是人生的正业，而追求女人时那副情场功夫，是在宫廷里最好的荐书。惟其如此，像威彻利先生这样人品与见识的绅士，才肯俯就着去描摹床笫之荣所受的种种侵犯，而不施以正当的斥责；但在当时，若把一个在这些事上有操守的男人画出来，反倒是怪物了；那时的诗人若在自己笔下的风雅绅士身上安一副美德，便如画一个恶棍来讨好眼前的观众一般，恰恰自曝其对所居宫廷风尚的无知。」

## Prologue / 开场诗

===Original===
Spoken by Mr. Hart.2

Poets, like cudgelled bullies, never do
At first or second blow submit to you;
But will provoke you still, and ne’er have done,
Till you are weary first with laying on.

===Chinese===
由哈特先生（Mr. Hart）2朗诵。

诗人，同挨了棍棒的恶棍一样，绝不
挨一下两下就向你低头；
偏要一味撩拨你，永无休止，
直等你先抡累了胳膊。

===Original===
The late so baffled scribbler of this day,
Though he stands trembling, bids me boldly say,
What we before most plays are used to do,
For poets out of fear first draw on you;
In a fierce prologue the still pit defy,
And, ere you speak, like Castril3 give the lie.

===Chinese===
近日那个刚被哄倒的蹩脚写家，
虽浑身打颤，却命我放胆放言，
往常多数戏开演之前我们如何行事——
因为诗人总是出于害怕，先动手的是他；
借一篇凶巴巴的开场白挑衅安静的池座，
还没等你开口，就像卡斯提尔（Castril）3那样骂你撒谎。

===Original===
But though our Bayes’s battles oft I’ve fought,
And with bruised knuckles their dear conquests bought;
Nay, never yet feared odds upon the stage,
In prologue dare not hector with the age;
But would take quarter from your saving hands,
Though Bayes within all yielding countermands,
Says, you confederate wits no quarter give,
Therefore his play shan’t ask your leave to live.

===Chinese===
可我虽常替咱们的贝斯（Bayes）打仗，
指节都打青了，才买下他们金贵的胜利；
不错，在戏台上我从没怕过寡不敌众，
在开场白里却不敢同这个时代逞威风；
只愿从你们留情的手里受降讨生，
虽说贝斯在里头传令寸土不让，
说你们结党的才子们从不留活口，
因此他这出戏休想求你们恩准活命。

===Original===
Well, let the vain rash fop, by huffing so,
Think to obtain the better terms of you;
But we, the actors, humbly will submit,
Now, and at any time, to a full pit;
Nay, often we anticipate your rage,
And murder poets for you on our stage:
We set no guards upon our tiring-room,
But when with dying colours there you come,
We patiently, you see, give up to you
Our poets, virgins, nay, our matrons too.

===Chinese===
好罢，由那虚荣莽撞的花花公子去咋呼，
只当他能从你们手里讨来更宽的条款；
可我们这些做演员的，只有恭顺，
此刻也好，往后任何时候也好，向满座的池座低头；
不但如此，我们还常赶在你们发作之前，
在咱们的戏台上替你们杀诗人：
我们的后台从不设一兵一卒把守，
等你们打着「欲仙欲死」的旗号杀到，你们瞧，
我们耐耐烦烦，双手奉上
我们的诗人、我们的少女，连我们的妇人也一并奉上。
